**Coverage check.** Brief §C.1–C.14 (C.13 is F39's and C.14 is F40's, both appended) map onto principles 11, 10, 4, 5, 6/7, 5, 12, 13, 2/3, 14/15, 7, 1, 3 and 3 respectively — one-to-one, with C.5, C.9 and C.10 each splitting across two principles because each states two independent obligations. **C.13 is F39's and C.14 is F40's**, both appended rather than inserted: the brief's §C list is cited by item number from four documents (AC-019, AC-303, AC-466, AC-509, SM-035 and this document's own §7 rows all name a §C.10/C.11/C.12), so a new implication takes the next free number and never a position inside the sequence [documented, the AC register's own §0.4 rule that renumbering breaks every citation already written]. Principles 8, 9, 16, 17, 18 and 19 are carried by the proposal, the research pass and the ADR set rather than by a §C implication, and their sources are named in the row.

---

## 3. Target architecture — kernel, store, git, and the five ports

### 3.1 The binary

One Rust binary owns the loop. A deterministic kernel holds stage selection, gate evaluation, budgets, retry, memoisation and replay and holds no non-determinism of its own; a store owns the typed artifacts behind a single write path; a git layer owns trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` and is deliberately not abstracted. Five ports mark the named variation axes. Every `.await` in the process lives below the port line (ADR-0076).

```
        ┌───────────────────────── redkiln (Rust, one binary) ─────────────────────────┐
        │ kernel   packs → stages → activities · gates · budgets · memo · replay       │
        │          deterministic; no clock, no rand, no I/O, no .await   (ADR-0022/76)  │
        │ store    .bklg / .kb typed artifacts · one write path · canonical serialiser  │
        │ git      trailers · worktrees · reconcile() · merge queue      (NOT a port)   │
        └──┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────────┘
           │            │            │            │            │      ← sync trait objects,
           ▼            ▼            ▼            ▼            ▼        Arc<dyn Port+Send+Sync>;
      EventStore     Harness      Tracker      DocsSite     Toolchain   cancel = root's token
  P1    sqlite      claude -p       —            —          node, rust  ← adapters own every
  alt     —      codex*, pi*   github*, linear* mdbook*,  dotnet*,        .await and their own
                                                astro*    python*,       runtime handle
  fake  in-mem Vec  scripted    in-mem board   no-op rec.  fixture-      (ADR-0076)
                    responder                              driven
        (* named, not built in Phase 1 · no Postgres adapter is planned — ADR-0003, ADR-0094)
```

Phase 1 ships **four real adapters** — SQLite (`EventStore`), Claude Code (`Harness`), and **Node and Rust** (`Toolchain`, `rk-adapter-toolchain-node` and `rk-adapter-toolchain-rust` with a Rust technology pack; ADR-0094, superseding ADR-0003's `Toolchain` row in part, because Phase 1's self-hosting run is on a Cargo workspace and no adapter could otherwise run `cargo test` on it) — and **no tracker and no docs-site adapter at all**; GitHub and mdBook are Phase 3 (SM-148, corrected against proposal §7). ADR-0003's "named real adapter" column reads "in the phase §10.2 assigns", so AC-499's `{fake, real}` matrix carries a phase clause for `Tracker` and `DocsSite` [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-004, PIA-072]. The consequence is recorded in SM-148: `StepError::Unsupported` is knowingly saturated at zero until a second adapter of any port exists, so its first non-zero value is the signal, not its level.

### 3.2 Authority — who writes what, most to least trusted

| Rank | Layer | Owner and write path | What it carries | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Git** — commits and trailers `Story:` / `Slice-Verdict:` / `Gate:` | kernel, through `rk-git`; the write path shells out to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` / `git commit --trailer` | checkpoint commits, slice verdicts, gate marks | `reconcile.rs` (a re-implementation of the old tree's `src/store/verify.ts`) run over 360 commits / 74 slices in the spike runner, not validated against an oracle [measured, F12]; the second trailer axis is forced — 20.3% of slices (15/74) passed through a rejected state, so `Story:` alone is insufficient [measured, F12]. ADR-0018, ADR-0077 |
| 2 | **Item store** — `.bklg`, `.kb` typed artifacts | kernel via the store's single write path; agents return values, the store renders files | specs, plans, reports, KB atoms | Nothing parses structure out of prose [measured, S-MIG census, 1,312 files / 6,877 records at pin `47faec9f`; the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus, so these are shares under a tuned locator, not a blind parse]. ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075 |
| 3 | **Event log** — behind the `EventStore` port | kernel | every stage / gate / dispatch / cost / drift / memo event; the telemetry tier is disposable | Journalled results are what make replay possible at all; the replay instrument itself has never been run — F19's 174/174 is about the state *carrier*, and the FSM was never replayed [measured, F19, with its own stated limit]. ADR-0021, ADR-0022 |
| 4 | **Projections** — Tracker, DocsSite, web | kernel, derived | labels, sub-issues, docs pages, views | Derived, never authoritative: a human edit to an orchestrator-only label produces a typed `Drift` record and **no** state transition (AC-507); drift is 12/174 = 7% overall and 3.6× more likely on gates over 1 h [measured, F19]. ADR-0003 |

Two rules follow from the ordering and are cited, not restated, here. The tier-1/tier-2 split test — *if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?* — and the store's file layout are §5's. `reconcile()` at every entry, with no optimisation permitted to skip it, is also §5's (ADR-0018). What §3 fixes is only the direction of trust: **a projection is never read back as authority, and an inbound tracker event is a typed input, never a transition** (AC-507, AC-508).

Two authority-adjacent guards belong here because they are port and kernel obligations. The orchestrator never writes to the user's checkout — every mutating call resolves through a run-owned worktree or the store under the resolved common dir, asserted by `git status --porcelain` empty plus `git diff-index --quiet HEAD` plus HEAD unmoved across a full lane run (AC-518) [documented, shipmates L5.3: 0.1.18 committed into the user's checkout, 0.1.19 pinned the write to a dedicated worktree — one project, one maintainer]. And agents hold no tracker credential: the assertion is made from the child's own reported environment, not from the parent's spawn arguments (AC-509), because an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one [measured, F8: 12 MCP servers, ~280 tools inherited by a default `claude -p`; MCP was 4,631 tokens = 56% of the cache write].

### 3.3 The five ports

| Port | Responsibility (sync trait) | Phase-1 real adapter | Named alternatives | Required fake | Why this axis exists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EventStore` | append; query by item / SHA / run; snapshot; rebuild *(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, §8e.2; **ADR-0181**, proposed: a fifth method, `append_tier1(event, &TierOneAnchor)` — the tier-1 writer D21-41 e names, which writes the `t2_event` row anchored to its trailer commit and, where the kind has one, its `t1_*` row, in one transaction through `rk-store`'s `tier_one` statements; called only from the kernel's boundary function, after the commit (D21-42 8a). The port count stays five (ADR-0003). `query` honours `ByItem` (the item's rows plus, in the interim, the unkeyed rows; **ADR-0182**) and `BySha` (by anchor); `ByRun` stays inert)* *(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P7, §8e.9; ADR-0181 decision 11, proposed: `snapshot` and `rebuild` are implemented on the SQLite row — a `Snapshot` is the ordered list of rows a store holds, each with its anchor, its boundary tree and its opening run, with a public constructor; `rebuild` writes one append-only, into a store opened with `SqliteStore::open_at` on the rebuild's staging file; both registry rows carry a recorded probe)* | SQLite (WAL, `busy_timeout`), one writer connection, never a pool | **none planned** — the port exists for the fake and future safety, not for a Postgres adapter | in-memory `Vec` | SQLite serialises writers, so one writer connection per process behind a mutex, no queue, never a pool; `busy_timeout` = 5000 ms (rusqlite 0.40.2's default), every write transaction shorter than it, readers one per thread with the same timeout, and `doctor` refuses a store on a network filesystem [documented, docs.rs rusqlite 0.40.2 (`Connection: Send + !Sync`) and sqlite.org WAL, fetched 2026-09-10; ADR-0105 clause 7]. The two-process contention test §9.4 asks for is what **R-SQLITE** still owes. ADR-0003, ADR-0084 |
| `Harness` | `dispatch(&ActivitySpec, &mut dyn EventStore) -> DispatchOutcome { record: DispatchRecord, result: Result<Typed, StepError> }` — **blocks to completion**, the record is AC-360's, `Typed` is an erased validated value plus its schema id; capability registry with dated probes; cost/usage; cancellation by a **`CancelToken` the composition root owns**, cloned into the adapter at construction and fired only by the CLI signal path or the per-activity timeout — the kernel never calls cancel (ADR-0099, superseding ADR-0076's `cancel(handle)`; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-010) | Claude Code (`claude -p`), subscription auth only, never `--bare`, asserts no API key | Codex (`codex exec --json --output-schema`), Pi | scripted responder | Second-harness quality is unmeasured — E3 is Claude-only; a Codex-sourced result may not close a gate until an E3-shaped run exists (AC-239, AC-496, both PROVISIONAL; S-CODEX unrun). ADR-0003, ADR-0007, ADR-0010 |
| `Tracker` | `project(item) -> ExternalRef`; `inbox() -> Vec<TypedInput>`; idempotent writes | **none in Phase 1** (GitHub is Phase 3) | Linear (GraphQL, signed webhooks), Jira | in-memory board | Every external side effect needs an idempotency key [documented, shipmates L5.4: one thread holds the same report posted three times by their own orchestrator]. AC-508 |
| `DocsSite` | `build(DocSet)`, `check_links`, `drift(changed_sources)` | **none in Phase 1** (mdBook is Phase 3) | Astro/Starlight, rustdoc, DocFX | no-op recorder | Generator-agnostic invariants live in the kernel, generator checks in the adapter; the three invariants are exercised against the fake with a negative case each, or the suite is vacuous (AC-510) |
| `Toolchain` | detect; `build`; `test(selection)`; `lint`; `classify(path) -> Text/Binary/Generated`; `change_summary(range) -> ChangeSummary` *(dated 2026-09-26, `s8c` build; it reads so under the operator's flip lines of ADR-0169, ADR-0170 and ADR-0171, all three `proposed` until then: under T (ADR-0169), `test` takes a closed `Selection` and returns a `TestOutcome`, a red suite being `Ok` with counts, and `build` returns a `BuildReport`; under B (ADR-0171), every step-reached method (`build`, `test`, `lint`, `change_summary`, `run_verb`) takes a `StepBound`; under V (ADR-0170), a seventh method, `run_verb`, routes the registry's `toolchain` rows)* | Node (vitest/pnpm) **and Rust (cargo build/test or nextest, `cargo metadata`)** — ADR-0094 | .NET, Python, Unreal | fixture-driven | The kernel knows no language. **`ChangeSummary`'s field set is BLOCKED** — see below |

**`ChangeSummary` is blocked, and this section does not route around it.** S-BIN is registered and **unrun**, [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: run 2026-10-14, ten records valid at corpus `835b122`; F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*, fix the `Binary` arm's four fields and route an item carrying a `Binary` path to the human gate] and no review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff [documented; ADR-0008, ADR-0012]. The port *shape* is asserted — a `Binary` classification means the reviewer receives a typed `ChangeSummary` and no textual diff of that path is assembled or dispatched (AC-497) — and the type's **contents are out of scope until S-BIN reports**; meanwhile `ChangeSummary` is an **opaque, non-finalised newtype in `rk-ports`**, the one site Checkpoint 5's absence grep over `crates/` excludes (ADR-0099 clause 5). *(amended 2026-09-24, `s8a`, ADR-0165: no longer so. `ChangeSummary` is an enum defined only in `rk-ports`, with a `Text` arm finalised now and a `Binary` slot whose payload is sealed until S-BIN reports at `s8i`; S-BIN now gates the `Binary` arm and its body only)* S-BIN's block is scoped to exactly that type and the port's `change_summary` body; it blocks no other Phase-1 commit (ADR-0093 decision 4). The five toolchain-keyed kinds of ADR-0073 cannot be finalised without it. Consumer: the Phase-1 type-design work for `ChangeSummary`; spike register in §10.

**The card-delivery route is blocked in the same disciplined way.** The Harness adapter's Phase-1 default is the static `--system-prompt` carrying the card body under `cache_profile = static`, with `--append-system-prompt` / `--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections` absent from argv (AC-211, PROVISIONAL, its GIVEN "a card whose profile is `static`"; ADR-0016, untouched by ADR-0078's partial supersession); under `cache_profile = default` the adapter omits `--system-prompt` and prepends the card body to the user message — F28's measured arm — with a golden argv test per profile (§7.1; PIA-012). The card + `--system-prompt` combination itself is **unmeasured**: S-WT measured a cardless one-line static prompt, never combined with `--agent` (PIA-066). The append route **may not be enabled as anything but an unmeasured config option until S-H1c runs**; the argv is capped before spawn and `--system-prompt-file` stays unused pending S-SPF (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #18). Its cost consequences are §7's.

#### Port rules

| # | Rule | Instrument | Criterion | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1 | One trait per port; **no generic parameter bound by a port trait appears anywhere in the kernel**, and every port is held as one object-safe trait object | API-surface lint + a compile-fail fixture | AC-480 | ADR-0003. The dyn boundary is load-bearing for the fakes; making the kernel generic to recover native async is explicitly forbidden (ADR-0076) |
| P2 | A fake per port is a hard requirement of adding the port, and the equality is asserted **as a number** | `traits.len() == fakes.len()` inventory test; a sixth trait with no fake must fail | AC-481 | [documented, shipmates L5.8: make the count the assertion] |
| P3 | Adapters are resolved **once at startup** from `.redkiln/config.toml`; the adapter set is a run-scoped constant recorded on the run-start event | mid-run config mutation test + replay assertion | AC-485 | ADR-0003 |
| P4 | A configured adapter that is not compiled in, or lacks required configuration, refuses the run **before any process is spawned and before any store write**; `doctor` reports the same defect at zero model cost | startup-resolution test; `doctor` rule row | AC-486 | Principle "explicit over discovered"; motivated by F7/F8 [measured] — the generalisation that an unhonoured setting looks identical to an honoured one is [inferred] from them, not measured |
| P5 | Capability differences are **data**: `{capability, verified_on, probe}`, with no capability readable except through a row carrying all three, and an unknown token failing the build rather than defaulting | registry schema test; vocabulary test; catch-all-arm lint | AC-489, AC-492 | [documented, shipmates L4.3/L5.7: capability research has a shelf life of weeks — Gemini→Antigravity, Windsurf→Devin Desktop invalidated a table by its own follow-up comment] |
| P6 | The kernel contains **no adapter-identity branch** — no adapter name string, no `Any::downcast`, no adapter-discriminant enum | kernel denylist lint + compile-fail fixture | AC-487 | ADR-0003. The lint proves absence of identity checks only; that a capability lookup exists is carried by AC-494/AC-495 |
| P7 | A missing capability is `StepError::Unsupported(capability)` carrying the token — never a generic error, never a retry, never a `pass`; a pack whose only route through a stage needs it fails validation with a span at the requiring row | resolver unit test; pack-validation test over a routed and an unroutable fixture | AC-494, AC-495 | ADR-0003, ADR-0014 |
| P8 | A **sixth port** is admitted only if a concrete substitute adapter is named, or the fake alone is judged to pay for the abstraction | routed to a human gate — **not machine-decidable**; the machine-checkable half is P2 | AC-482 | ADR-0003, ADR-0017. The failure mode guarded against is a plausible-sounding port with no substitute |
| P9 | The same shared conformance suite runs against `{fake, real}` for each port, and an assertion that passes on the fake and fails on the adapter is reported as a divergence | parameterised conformance suite, one CI matrix row per instantiation | AC-499 (with a phase clause: Phase 1 instantiates `{fake, real}` for `EventStore`, `Harness` and `Toolchain` — two reals — and fake-only for `Tracker` and `DocsSite` until Phase 3, ADR-0094), SM-151 | SM-151 is **known to saturate**: a green matrix reads zero by construction, so the tracked quantity is assertion-count growth and the divergence count is its alarm (brief C.12) |
| P10 *(added 2026-09-11, PIA-010; ADR-0099)* | **`dispatch` blocks to completion**; the adapter's declared per-activity timeout (AC-223) is the in-band cancel; **`cancel` is a `CancelToken` constructed at the composition root**, cloned into the adapter at construction, fired only by the CLI signal path (Ctrl-C / SIGTERM) or the per-activity timeout — **the kernel never calls it**; only the `Harness` port carries cancellation in Phase 1 (the two `Toolchain` adapters run bounded commands under the same timeout without a token); every port is held as **`Arc<dyn Port + Send + Sync>`** with `Send + Sync` as supertraits on all five traits, a bare trait object and not a newtype | S-PORTS (a throwaway crate on the 1.98.1 pin); the port-inventory lint; a compile-fail fixture for a port trait without the supertraits | AC-223, AC-224, AC-225, AC-480, AC-482 | ADR-0099. The `dyn` boundary diverges from Microsoft's M-DI-HIERARCHY ranking (generics, then enum dispatch, then `dyn`) on a stated ground: one fake per port swaps at one root, the call count is small, and the fakes are scripted responders, not sans-io mocks; the cost is one vtable call per dispatch [documented, Microsoft Rust guidelines M-DI-HIERARCHY / M-MOCKABLE-SYSCALLS / M-AVOID-WRAPPERS, fetched 2026-09-10; PIA-045] |

The kernel's side of P1 is enforced twice: the workflow module may not name an adapter crate (AC-005, `cargo-deny` plus a `cargo tree -i` assertion returning empty) and the workspace graph may carry no edge, direct or transitive, from `rk-kernel` to any `rk-adapter-*` (AC-484). Kernel and pack-validation suites run with network egress denied, no adapter crates linked and a dispatch counter asserted at 0 (AC-498) — every replay and lattice test therefore costs $0.

#### Git is deliberately not a port

Trailers, worktrees, disposable clones and `reconcile()` are load-bearing and have measured behaviour [measured, F12, F19], and **no alternative VCS is named** — so the axis fails P8 on its own terms. The port inventory lint carries a denylist over the git surface, and a declared `trait GitPort` is a compile-fail fixture (AC-483). `rk-kernel` depends on `rk-git` as a concrete crate.

Two claims about the *implementation* of that layer are **BLOCKED**, and are named here rather than smoothed over. gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states that "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — precisely the mechanism the one-store-per-repository design leans on [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched 2026-09-08]. That interplay is a **read** every startup performs, so its no-go branch has an owner: `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` through the exec wrapper, once at startup, cached for the process (S-GIX/a; ADR-0098 decision 1). Trailer **parsing** exists at the pin (`BodyRef::trailers()`, gix-object 0.64.1, despite `crate-status.md`'s unchecked box) and no first-class trailer-**writing** API does — a [documented] absence from a docs.rs read of 2026-09-10, over what is tier-1 state, with S-GIX/b's executed-test arm still owed (ERRATA E-0077-1). The hybrid ships meanwhile (ADR-0077, as ADR-0098 restates it): gix for `rev-parse`, status and trailer parsing; the shallow clone by SHA (`PrepareFetch::with_revision` + `with_shallow`) is a gix **write** retained deliberately, its advisory exposure named and covered by the `cargo audit` job and the Windows CI target (S-GIX/c); `git worktree add/remove`, the trailer write (with `-c trailer.ifexists=add` and `-c core.hooksPath=<empty>`), `write-tree` on a temporary index, `patch-id` and the integration-ref merge shell out — ADR-0105 clause 5 enumerates the wrapper's write set, and every git op on the activity side goes through the journalled `GitOps` seam so replay performs zero writes. **gix may not own the write path, and no section may claim gix can write a trailer, until S-GIX/b passes.** Spikes S-GIX/a–c, consumer: the `rk-git` read and write paths; register in §10 [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-009, PIA-042, PIA-040]. The store's file layout and the read/write split in detail are §5's.

### 3.4 The workflow/activity split, and the determinism ban list

The split is transplanted deliberately: the **workflow** — stage selection, gate evaluation, retry policy, DAG order — is deterministic Rust replayable from the event log; **activities** hold all non-determinism and each carries a retry policy, a timeout with a number, and an idempotency key (ADR-0022).

**The ban list is CONVERGENT, not a Temporal-ism.** AWS's first-party Durable Execution SDK (2025/2026, Lambda-backed, distinct from Step Functions, supporting TypeScript/Python/Java/C# and **explicitly not Rust**) independently re-derived the same list for its `step` primitive: ban `Date.now`/`time.time`/`Instant.now`, UUID generation, HTTP/DB/SDK calls, `Math.random`, and any branching on these [documented, 1P AWS Durable Execution SDK determinism guide + 1P docs.temporal.io/workflow-definition, fetched 2026-09-08; evidence-augmentation §2, §3]. ADR-0086 forbids presenting the list as vendor-specific for exactly this reason. It is two vendors' documented designs, not a measurement that the split is optimal.

| Banned inside workflow code | Enforced by | Criterion | Where it may live instead |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wall clock, monotonic instant, timezone-dependent value (`SystemTime::now`, `Instant::now`, `chrono::Utc::now`) | clippy `disallowed_methods` over `rk-kernel::workflow` with `-D warnings`, plus a `trybuild` must-fail fixture | AC-001 | Time reaches the workflow only as a journalled activity result — including the circuit breaker's expiry comparison (AC-035) |
| Randomness and non-deterministic id minting (`rand`, `uuid::new_v4`, address hashing) | same list, same fixture | AC-002 | Ids derive from `(run_id, activity_id, attempt)` or from a content hash |
| I/O — filesystem, network, process spawn, blocking sleep (`std::fs`, `std::process`, `std::net`, `tokio::time::sleep`, `thread::sleep`) | `disallowed_methods`/`disallowed_types` + fixture | AC-003 | Activities, which carry retry, a numeric timeout and the idempotency key |
| Iteration over an unordered map or set in a decision | `disallowed_types` on `HashMap`/`HashSet` in workflow decision signatures and locals + fixture | AC-004 | Ordered containers, or an explicit sort with a total key. Declared order is load-bearing — seed-order tie-breaking — so "invariant under input permutation" is a property the kernel **must not** have and was cut as an instrument |
| A dependency edge on any adapter crate | `cargo-deny` ban + empty `cargo tree -i` | AC-005 | The workflow reaches the world only through `rk-ports` traits |
| `async fn`, `#[async_trait]`, a returned future, or any dependency on `tokio`/`async-std`/`futures`/`async-trait` | see §3.8 | ADR-0076's forbids-block | Adapters, which own their runtime handles |

Replay is the instrument that would prove the split works, and it **has never been run**. A replay binds every port to its fake and asserts the decision sequence and next action are byte-identical to the record (AC-006); a divergence halts with a typed `ReplayDivergence { activity_id, recorded, recomputed }` and no continuation on the recomputed value is reachable (AC-007); the replay must reach the recorded terminal state with all N results served from the journal and **zero** harness dispatches, so that a regression which re-dispatches is a red test rather than a bill, and a kernel that does nothing fails the terminal-state half (AC-008). SM-009 has **no baseline**: F19's 174/174 losslessness is about the state carrier, and the brief records that the FSM was never replayed, so "same next action" is unproven [measured, F19, with its stated limit]. ADR-0086 names the residual honestly — Temporal and AWS detect ban-list violations at replay; here, without a runtime enforcing it, a violation is a latent and possibly silent memoisation bug, which is why the ban is a build failure rather than a convention.

One kernel rule exists because of a **BLOCKED** area and must not be misread as relieving it. Where a stage's selection consumes a model-emitted classification or context vector, that vector is recorded verbatim in the activity result that produced it, and every later read — replay, resume, or a second stage — resolves from the journal rather than a new elicitation (AC-009). This is a journalling rule and nothing more. It does **not** claim the vector is stable and does not license selection over one: P1's kill fired 4 of 4, with the modal `R(stage, ctx)` identical across ten draws on only 5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20 items against an 80% line, the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair failing, `external_dep` collapsing to a constant (200/200 false), and three axes with byte-identical anchors drifting up to −0.168 [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37]. Arm D has since been **scored** — no derived facet clears 0.95 and `auth` scores 0.00 (0/12 [0.00, 0.24] after the panel-file key fix, E-0089-2) [measured, F39] — which discharges AC-071's fourth clause and leaves the block standing on its first, identity 0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10 against 0.80 (ADR-0079, status `superseded` — ADR-0090; corrected 2026-09-15, this read *"status `proposed`"* — see §4.1). The same journalling rule now also carries a **`null`**: under ADR-0089 a derived boolean that cannot be computed is recorded as `null`, never coerced to `false`, and the journal carries the abstention rather than a manufactured answer. The selection engine's semantics are §4's; both blocks are §4's to state at full strength.

### 3.5 The idempotency key and the memo key

| Key | Shape | Rules it enforces | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Idempotency** | `(item, activity_id@version, attempt)` — three-part, in the schema as everywhere else (ADR-0097 decision 2, ADR-0107; the `run_id`-keyed triple first written here is amended 2026-09-11, PIA-008) | A dispatch cannot be constructed without it — the port takes it by value, not `Option` — and a malformed key is refused before any process is spawned. A retry carries `attempt + 1` and never reuses an attempt number; the triple is unique across the whole log. A late second attempt whose sibling already committed is discarded as a duplicate; `attempt` increments and `activity_committed` (AC-012) is emitted per step activation under ADR-0022, at most once per triple | AC-010, AC-011, AC-012 |
| | | The key is **not** required to be globally fresh: re-entry after a kill deliberately reuses `run_id`, and uniqueness is asserted over the triple only. `redkiln run <item>` is the re-entry — there is no `--resume` flag — and no committed step re-executes | AC-013 (**PROVISIONAL**) |
| **Memo (step key)** | **`SHA-256` over a length-prefixed ordered tuple** (`u32-length \|\| bytes` per component) of exactly: (1) `activity_id@version`; (2) the rigor variant selected; (3) the step id; (4) the digest of each resolved step input in declaration order, each canonicalised by `rk-store`'s serialiser (sorted keys, fixed float format, LF); (5) the card digest, **or** for a `tool` step the Toolchain adapter's detected version string plus the command; (6) the digests of the **resolved** injected KB atoms, sorted — the *resolved atom set*, never the inclusion rule that produced it, because a workspace-scoped conditional-inclusion mechanism resolves differently by workspace (§8.6; research pass contradiction #12) and would make the key non-reproducible; (7) the loop iteration counter (0 outside a loop); (8) the output type's `schema_version` (ADR-0097 decision 2 [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-008]) | Changing any one of the eight components while the others are held fixed misses the memo; all eight identical hits it — one property case per component (AC-014, AC-019). A stale `.kb` atom therefore invalidates stale work — editing or superseding an injected atom produces a `memo_miss` and a re-execution | AC-014, AC-015; ADR-0021, ADR-0097 |
| | per **step**, not per activity — **and the activity-level key is a different formula** | An activity realised as an encapsulated graph has a key per step (the tuple above), and the activity-level key the stage sees is a separately derived value — `SHA-256` over **every card digest reachable in the subgraph** (ADR-0022) — not the step key and not a hash of step keys; a key computed from a single card reference is a lint error. The two formulas are stated once, here, and :325's row does not restate a third (PIA-008) | AC-016; ADR-0021, ADR-0022, ADR-0097 |
| | **loop-iteration rule** | A step inside a `repeat` / `on_false` cycle whose resolved inputs are byte-identical across iterations **does not** hit the previous iteration's entry, because the loop iteration counter is part of the step key. Two iterations, two keys, two executions — the direct regression test for "loops cannot learn" | AC-017; ADR-0021, ADR-0043 (its first sentence and lint stand; its second sentence — a step declaring `memo = false` to assert liveness — is withdrawn by ADR-0059, which moves liveness to a `live = true` flag on the verb's registry entry that implies `memo = false` on every step using it) |
| | live state is the verb's `live = true` flag, and nothing else | A `tool` step whose verb's registry entry carries `live = true` is never memoised; a step that is memoised, or that declares `memo = true` (or `memo = false` — the per-step declaration is withdrawn, ADR-0097 decision 3, as ADR-0059 withdrew it from ADR-0043), fails pack validation at the step's span [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-008] | AC-018; ADR-0059, ADR-0064, ADR-0097 |
| | never silent | A memo hit appends a `memo_hit` event carrying the key and exactly its eight components; a skip is never invisible | AC-019; ADR-0036, ADR-0097 |

AC-013 is **PROVISIONAL** and says why: P8 is PARTIAL — 3 of a pre-registered 27+3 runs, and its recorded state held **exactly one step**; the 65-minute arm, the 24-hour arm and all three no-kill controls are unrun [measured/PARTIAL, F38]. What was measured is real and narrow: **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** across a real `taskkill /T /F` [measured, F38, n=3, one machine, one operator]; the resume's cost against a stage gate is **unmeasured** — the sub-1× output-token ratios first quoted here divided `output_tokens` by `cache_creation_input_tokens` (E-0087-1; ADR-0106). The memo stays tier 2 and is rebuildable by re-execution, never by `doctor --rebuild` (ADR-0097 decision 1; §5.4). The kernel test generalises to N what the spike measured at 1.

Two measurement honesties belong with the memo table. SM-001 forbids an undifferentiated hit rate: the rate must be split into steps outside loops, steps inside `repeat`/`on_false` cycles — a cache miss **by construction** under ADR-0021 — and `memo = false` tool steps, since only the first bucket is a meaningful rate. And the cost of memoisation is untested: whether a guaranteed in-loop miss is materially more expensive than the useless memoised loop it replaces is unmeasured, and bucket (b) is where it would show. The A18 evidence behind AC-016..AC-018 is [documented, no n] — ADR-0021 records it as an observed, named failure mode from one author's one loop, not as a measurement, and it may not be re-tagged.

### 3.6 Budgets, retry and degradation — the layering

| Layer | Who | Policy | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Budgets | three counters, **never shared** | attempts per activity · tokens per item (appetite, fixed at triage) · wall clock per run. Distinct newtypes with no `From`/`Add` crossing them, so a shared counter does not compile. `attempts_total` counts the agent/tool steps of a **pass**, not the sum of retried steps across passes; a `for_each` over N items charges **once**, independent of N (clarified 2026-09-15 per **ADR-0122** decision 5(d), accepted 2026-09-14, which names this clarification as owed against this line: the sentence above governs the **declared** field — `budget.attempts_total` as a catalog author writes it, on **ADR-0037**'s unit, which is lint rule 8's load-time check that the declared minimum is the agent/tool steps of one pass, so a two-pass graph does not require `2A`. It does **not** govern the runtime draw-down. That quantity is **AC-026**'s, and inner harness attempts charge it — one charge per recorded `system/api_retry` event, in the pass that dispatched it — which is the next row, §7.5's `attempts_total` row and ADR-0122 decision 1. `attempts_total` names two quantities and this row states the first; the two rows must not be read as one, and ADR-0122 decision 3 is the reading under which this table is coherent) | AC-020, AC-021, AC-022, AC-023; ADR-0037, ADR-0053, **ADR-0122** (decision 5(d), 2026-09-15) |
| Inner retry | the harness itself (`system/api_retry`) | observe, record, do not duplicate; inner attempts count against the activity's budget — the payload is **captured** on 139 rows across 12 result files, `spikes/results/e3.jsonl` included, which is the fixture both criteria cite [measured, PIA-068] | AC-026, AC-232 |
| Outer retry | kernel policy per error class | `Transport` / `Platform` 5xx / `Timeout`: exponential backoff with full jitter, **base 2 s, cap 5 min — kernel policy constants (AC-029 and the schema), not pack values** [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-055], ≤ the pack's `N`. `RateLimited { resets_at }`: **no sleep, never backoff** — write a `retry_at(resets_at)` mark and exit 0; a child that exhausts the subscription mid-dispatch resolves to this class, `resets_at` from the last `rate_limit_event` in the same stream else the pack delay, detected from the structured `subtype` first (AC-523; ADR-0095). `Schema`: re-prompt exactly once carrying the validation error, then `Schema { attempts: 2 }`. The closed variant set and each variant's retry class, `spoke()` value and event arm are ADR-0095 decision 2's table | AC-029, AC-030, AC-031, AC-233, AC-235, AC-523 |
| Never retried | — | `NotAdopted` (the wrong agent answering), `NotIsolated` (tool-set, MCP-server or environment inequality against the card — one class for the condition, ADR-0095; DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #5), `Unsupported(_)`, `Overage` — configuration, not weather. Zero re-dispatches, the typed error surfaced | AC-027, AC-234; SM-003 (a **tripwire that saturates at zero**, not a discriminator — one retry against any of the four blocks the release that introduced it) |
| Not an error at all | — | An activity that completed and returned blocking findings is **not** retried: the success and failure channels are disjoint (`Result<Findings, StepError>`), so "the reviewer said no" cannot be constructed as a `StepError` | AC-032; ADR-0004 (superseded by ADR-0074; cited here as the origin of the type split) |
| Circuit breaker | per model family | K consecutive `Platform` failures refuses the next dispatch for that family behind a **durable mark**; other families continue; the refusal survives process death, and breaker state is never held only in memory | AC-033, AC-034, AC-035, AC-237 |
| Degradation | policy, never silent | a model substitution is refused unless the pack permits it, and a permitted one records `Degraded { from, to, why }` in both the event log and the digest; a review produced under a substitution below a gate's demanded tier does not close that gate | AC-036, AC-037, AC-038, AC-236 |
| Budget exhaustion | — | An exhausted budget yields `Err(BudgetExhausted)` and the gate does not close; no code path maps an exhausted budget to `pass`. The run writes a durable mark and exits 0 — nothing sleeps waiting to be extended | AC-024, AC-025 |

**Variant set (2026-09-11, later the same day).** The error classes this table names are ADR-0095's `StepError` table as ADR-0112 extends it — **eleven** variants: `Transport`, `Schema`, `Timeout`, `Cancelled`, `NonZero`, `RateLimited`, `NotAdopted`, `NotIsolated`, `Platform { status, retry_after }`, `Unsupported { capability }`, `Overage { reason }`. ADR-0095 alone stopped at eight and would have made this table, §3.3 P7, §7.6 and thirteen criteria unbuildable (register §0.3 row C5); ADR-0112 is the successor that adds the three. `BudgetExhausted` in the last row is **not** a `StepError`: it is `rk-kernel::budget`'s gate-evaluation result (AC-024, AC-025), raised by the kernel after a dispatch, never by an adapter.

**`K`, `N`, the backoff base and cap, the two-tick headroom margin and the per-item token appetite have no measured values.** No spike sized a `Platform` failure rate at all, and the AWS backoff post uses generic base/cap variables — "base 2 s, cap 5 min" does not appear in it; the post favours Full or Decorrelated Jitter and calls Equal Jitter "the loser", which is what the full-jitter choice above follows [documented, 1P aws.amazon.com/blogs/architecture/exponential-backoff-and-jitter, currency-noted May 2023, fetched 2026-09-08; the earlier "nudges toward Equal Jitter for throttling" clause is deleted — that recommendation lives in the AWS SDK retry-mode documentation, not this post — amended 2026-09-11, PIA-091]. `K`, `N` and the appetite are declared in the pack and refused as code defaults (AC-033, AC-023); **the backoff base and cap are kernel policy constants** (AC-029 and the schema's position — §0.3's authority table records it; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-055); the criteria verify conformance to the declared envelope, never that the envelope is right. Whether a halted run's budget is extended or the item abandoned is routed to a human gate and is **not machine-decidable** — no measured signal in this corpus discriminates it — though the mechanism around it is machine-checked (AC-039).

#### 3.6.1 The harness contract — AC-200..AC-239, named by subject

The harness area (AC-200..AC-239, SM-061..SM-070) is the largest area in the thirteen and had, until this revision, no design home at all; it was discharged in one sentence and two citations. It is still **cited rather than restated** — the register owns the wording (`references/2026-09-08-acceptance-criteria.md` §3; the area files were session scratch, never committed — ADR-0092 decision 2) — but the subjects are named here so that a reader can tell what is covered and a runbook can tell what is owed.

| Subject | What the area fixes | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stream and event retention | Unknown event **types** and unknown **fields on known events** are retained verbatim — the rule §9.6's raw-payload pattern depends on; verbatim event persistence across the run record | **AC-201**, **AC-227**; SM-061 |
| Isolation and adoption | Isolation asserted from the child's own `system/init`, adoption by **set equality** against the card's allowlist — an inequality is `NotIsolated` in AC-204 as in AC-202/AC-386 and §7.4 (ADR-0095; SM-061's instrument is a set digest), `NotAdopted` being reserved for the wrong agent answering — and a run whose flags were not honoured marked non-comparable | **AC-202**, **AC-204**, AC-214, AC-215, AC-386 |
| Structured output | Refusal **before spawn** when a schema is demanded and the card cannot satisfy it; **exit 0 with no `structured_output` resolves to `Err`**, never to a pass — this is the area that owns the rule §8.1's AC-439 re-derives from the cli-doctor side; the rule's record is **ADR-0108** (the check is on the assembled argv, shared with ADR-0095's per-request budget lint) | **AC-205**, **AC-206**, AC-208 |
| Prompt, cache and card delivery | Prompt on stdin never argv; **the argv Phase 1 builds, per profile**: under `cache_profile = static` the card body is the static `--system-prompt`; under `default` `--system-prompt` is omitted and the card body is prepended to the user message (F28's measured arm) — a golden argv test per profile (§7.1; PIA-012); the append route BLOCKED on S-H1c; an argv-length assertion refuses before spawn with a typed configuration error (fixture at the cap), `--system-prompt-file` unused pending S-SPF; the card + `--system-prompt` combination is unmeasured (PIA-066) | **AC-209**, **AC-210**, AC-211, AC-384 |
| Model, effort, permissions, auth | Model and effort pinned per dispatch from the **role**, materialised by the binder into the bound card (the authored card file may not carry them — AC-118/AC-418; PIA-015); a **timeout human gate**; **never `--bare`**; **no API key in the child environment** — the child environment is the captured map inherited minus a committed denylist (`rk-adapter-claude/src/env_denylist.rs`), received by the adapter as a value in `Config`, never read from the process environment (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #16); the harness-area home of a rule §2 and §8.4 also cite through AC-488/AC-422, with AC-511 settled to ports-dist by the register's §0.4 | **AC-213**, **AC-216**, **AC-217**, AC-203, AC-212, AC-236 |
| Errors, headroom and cancellation | `is_error` / `api_error_status` at **exit 0** (e3.jsonl holds seven such rows, so the fixture exists — PIA-075); the `RateLimited` mark, including exhaustion-on-result (AC-523, ADR-0095); headroom read only from `rate_limit_event` — typed in the Agent SDK as `SDKRateLimitEvent`, which the adapter parses tolerating both the documented `rate_limit_info` shape and F1's `unifiedWindows` shape with a named error on neither (PIA-069); cancellation as the composition root's token, containment a Job Object on Windows and a process group on POSIX, AC-225's classifier keyed on the token, not an exit code (ADR-0099) | **AC-218**, **AC-219**, **AC-220**, **AC-221**, AC-222, **AC-223**, AC-224, AC-225, AC-523 | 
| Version pinning and fan-out | **CLI-version mismatch** against `TESTED_CLI_VERSION` (pinned at **2.1.263**, §8.4; the latest measured instrument is 2.1.268, 2026-09-10 — PIA-019) attaches a typed warning to every cache metric; the fan-out stagger (the vendor's mechanism is hold-until-first-response-begins capped at 5 s then release together, not a per-sibling interval — AC-388, §7.7) | **AC-226**, AC-228 (PROVISIONAL), AC-385 |
| Unverified capability | A capability with no dated probe is `Unsupported`, never assumed | **AC-238**, AC-489–AC-495 |

**Two reconciliations this table records as settled by the register** (ADR-0092 decision 2: the register owns each criterion's id and state; this document is amended toward it). §8.1 marked **AC-428** PROVISIONAL for `is_error`/`api_error_status` on the grounds that those strings appear nowhere in the brief — `e3.jsonl` holds seven such rows (slugs fix-doctor-git-config-repair, issue-122 §2, issue-130, issue-131, issue-72 for 529; issue-82 for 500), so the fixture exists (PIA-075), **AC-218 in this area covers the same behaviour**, and the register's §0.3 row names the owner of the pair. The `--bare` / API-key assertions have homes in *three* areas (AC-216/AC-217 here, AC-488 in ports-dist, AC-422 in cli-doctor), and **AC-511 is ports-dist's** — the register's §0.4 hazard table settles the id, and the "unresolved" reading this document carried is withdrawn [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-036]. Measures SM-062, SM-065, SM-066, SM-067, SM-069 and SM-070 remain uncited by any section and are owed a home in the runbook.

The two rules that bear on the kernel's shape, restated because §3.6's layering depends on them, are that a tool-restricted card **silently** voids `--json-schema` at exit 0 with `subtype: success` [measured, F20], which is why `StructuredOutput` is a configuration-time check and nothing parses JSON out of prose (AC-031's sub-clause, AC-208; ADR-0108), and that `--agents` is **inert without `--agent`** [measured, F7], which is why adoption is verified from the child's own report. **The pre-spawn effective tool set is the card's declared `tools:` list and nothing else**; a card with no `tools:` key is a `doctor` configuration error, not an inherit-everything default — Claude Code inherits every tool when the key is absent [documented, shipmates L4.4], which is the fail-open direction the rule closes (ADR-0108 decision 2; DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #16; PIA-060).

### 3.7 No durable-execution framework — the exclusion, re-argued

The conclusion is unchanged and the rationale is not. Two of the three facts the corpus rested it on were checked against live sources and are **false**: `temporalio-sdk` is **1.0.0 (2026-09-04, "considered stable") on `temporalio-sdk-core` 0.9.0** — not the abandoned 2021 `temporal-sdk-core` 0.1.0-alpha.1, and not the "v0.5.0 Public Preview" this section first stated, which was already four releases behind on the 2026-09-08 read date (sdk-core did not go 1.0 with the SDK crate); and `graph-flow` **0.8.0** (2026-09-07) makes postgres an optional Cargo feature that is **on by default** — "no required database" holds only under `default-features = false`, and 0.6.0 hard-required `sqlx`, so the charter was right for the version it read — with an in-memory session store [documented, crates.io / docs.rs / GitHub, fetched 2026-09-10; ERRATA E-0086-1; evidence-augmentation §1 row 5 — "the conclusion stands, the rationale does not"; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-074]. `dbos` 0.5.0 (2026-09-09) is a first-party Rust SDK — a library with no server, the database arbitrating, hard-requiring `sqlx`/Postgres — so "Rust remains second-class across every first-party durable-execution SDK" is withdrawn; it is excluded on ADR-0003's ground alone. The exclusion survives on facts that hold (ADR-0086): Temporal's Rust SDK still mandates a server or Temporal Cloud with no embedded mode found; graph-flow's in-memory mode does not survive a process restart and so is not *durable*, while its postgres feature reintroduces the server; and `iopsystems/durable` — new in this pass, never rejected by the charter — requires **Postgres**, crossing ADR-0003, **and** compilation of workflow code to WASM components via WASI (version tags through v0.6.2; no GitHub Releases). AWS's own SDK is not Rust at all. **One candidate was named in the research pass and not surveyed here:** whether LangGraph's per-node checkpointing works as described was read **only via a secondary aggregator**, so it is neither excluded nor admitted — the exclusion above stands on the four crates actually read, and LangGraph is recorded as an unread alternative rather than a rejected one [documented, research pass §5 unresolved]. It is not Rust either, which is why it does not gate the decision. What is kept from the frameworks is the ban list of §3.4, adopted as a convergent idiom. What is taken on is everything they would have supplied: the memo table, the replayable log, retry and backoff, and the test harness for all of it. The "~800 LOC" figure is a projection with no implementation behind it and is not quoted here as a measurement. ADR-0086 also registers one scoped open item — whether `temporalio-sdk` ships a time-skipping local test server, which would soften "mandates a server" **for local development and testing only** and does not reopen the exclusion. The crate graph that realises this is §9's.

### 3.8 The two changes the research forced

#### 3.8.1 Port traits are SYNC, and every `.await` is confined to an adapter (ADR-0076)

The corpus implied that a dyn-dispatched async port needed no `async-trait` under current Rust. The research pass contradicts that at severity **high**: native `async fn` in traits has been stable since 1.75 but is **still not dyn-compatible**, and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` remains nightly-only — confirmed on 2026-09-10 by a full read of the 1.97.0 and 1.98.0 release notes and the open tracking issue rust-lang/rust #133119 (R-ASYNC, discharged negative through the 1.98.1 pin) [documented, 1P rust-lang.github.io/async-fundamentals-initiative + docs.rs/async-trait + [blog] debasishg.github.io, fetched 2026-09-08; blog.rust-lang.org 1.97.0/1.98.0 release notes, fetched 2026-09-10]. So "no generics leak into the kernel" and "dyn-dispatched port" are in direct tension the moment a port method is `async`. The corpus never recorded a choice; ADR-0076 records it.

| Option | What it costs | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `#[async_trait]` on every dyn port | Makes the kernel async — an executor inside a core whose defining property is deterministic replay, with the replay path then depending on task scheduling no event log records. One heap-allocated `Pin<Box<dyn Future>>` per call, on the one boundary every operation crosses. A proc-macro dependency in the compile graph of every crate that names a port | **Rejected.** And it does not buy what it appears to — see 3.8.2 |
| Generic (statically dispatched) ports with native `async fn` in trait | Exactly the generics leak ADR-0003 exists to prevent; monomorphises the kernel per adapter set; the fake/real swap stops being a value substitution and becomes a type-level one that spreads through every signature | **Rejected** (AC-480 is its instrument) |
| Wait for `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` | Nightly-only through 1.98.1 — the 1.97.0 and 1.98.0 release notes were read in full on 2026-09-10 and stabilise nothing async/dyn — so building the Phase-1 kernel on it makes ADR-0102's toolchain pin a hostage | **Rejected** |
| **Sync port traits; `.await` in adapters** | Adapters block the calling thread, so a kernel operation fanning out to three ports takes the sum of their latencies unless the composition root introduces threads. The `Handle::block_on`-inside-a-runtime-thread hazard becomes an adapter-authoring rule enforced by review, not by the type system. Streaming responses must be consumed inside the adapter and surfaced as a completed value or an explicit sync callback. Timeouts move into adapters and can drift apart | **Adopted** |

The decision: **no port method is `async fn` and none returns a future; the kernel contains no `.await`, holds no runtime handle, and depends on no async runtime crate.** An adapter needing async I/O owns its runtime handle, constructed once at the composition root and never per call. Concurrency across ports is the composition root's job — expressed as threads or an adapter-internal concurrent call, with results entering the kernel as values in a defined order, so the kernel never observes interleaving.

**Why the kernel may not depend on a live future.** Nothing may depend on a process surviving: ~12 background-job kills against one 12,000 s survivor under `Start-Process`, and `issue-81` killed three times as a background job while completing in **116 s** in the foreground [measured, operational, F22; one machine, one operator, **cause still unestablished**]. F22 licenses "do not depend on a process surviving" and nothing about *why* they died. Every wait is therefore a durable mark plus a later read, not an in-flight future — the same rule that makes the human gate a mark rather than a waiting process (§6) and `RateLimited` a `retry_at` mark rather than a sleep (AC-030, AC-222). A replayable core has no business owning an executor: what makes F19's lossless suspension (174/174) and F38's mid-graph resume (3/3, digests identical) expressible as replay over an event log rather than as executor state is precisely that there is no executor state.

The working assumption is recorded as such: through **1.98.1** — the pinned channel (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #7; ADR-0102) — a dyn-compatible native async fn in trait does not exist on stable Rust. The evidence was search-only on 2026-09-08; **the release-notes read was taken on 2026-09-10** (R-ASYNC: 1.97.0 and 1.98.0 notes in full, the 1.97.0/1.98.1 `RELEASES.md` sections, tracking issue #133119 — negative) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; PIA-024]. The check re-runs as a direct read of the release notes for the version pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml` plus the tracking issue, performed as part of the review of any toolchain-pin bump. That review is the named consumer. It does **not** gate Phase 1, because the decision is correct under either answer.

#### 3.8.2 Cancellation is a token the composition root owns, backed by a real tree kill, never an implicit drop

`async-trait`'s implicit drop-cancellation buys nothing here, and this is the second half of why the first option loses. **Dropping a boxed future does not reap a spawned process tree.** An explicit kill is required under either option [inferred — standard Rust/OS semantics, stated as reasoning; ADR-0076 records it as reasoning and not as a citation], and F22 is the measured shape of the failure that follows from assuming otherwise.

A sync `dispatch` blocks to completion, so no caller can hold a handle minted when the operation started — the thing that can cancel is outside the call, and cancellation is therefore **a value handed in, not a handle handed out** (ADR-0099, superseding ADR-0076's handle clause; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-010). The `CancelToken` is constructed at the composition root, cloned into the adapter at construction, and fired by exactly two owners — the CLI's signal path and the per-activity timeout; the kernel never calls it. When it fires the adapter kills the child tree and `dispatch` returns `Err(StepError::Cancelled { by })`. **Containment is per operating system and both are named:** on Windows a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` is created **before** spawn, the child assigned before it can fork, the handle held for the child's lifetime so parent death reaps the tree — the `unsafe` FFI in one module of `rk-adapter-claude` under a commented `#![allow(unsafe_code)]` (ADR-0102's one permitted `allow`); on POSIX `CommandExt::process_group(0)` plus `killpg`. The lifted runner's `taskkill /T /F` is the Windows evidence that a POSIX process-group kill never obtains there (victim exit code 1, n=1). Verified by an integration test that spawns a fixture forking a detached grandchild, cancels, and enumerates the group via the job (Windows) or the pgid (POSIX) asserting no live PID, run in CI as a regression against F22's 12,000 s survivor (AC-224); only the Harness port carries cancellation in Phase 1.

**Counter-evidence, named rather than smoothed over.** The programme recorded a case the "no descendant survives the call" claim does not cover: **a killed `vitest` leaves a Windows handle that no `Stop-Process` releases**, and parallel arms consequently need separate fixture trees [documented, `spikes/HANDOFF.md`, "Three defects worth carrying forward" (3), issues #26/#30]. A tree kill — job or process group — reaps *processes*; it does not release an OS handle the dead process left behind. So AC-224's assertion is **scoped to live PIDs in the group**, which is what it actually tests, and **SM-068's "zero survivors" target is a PID count and not a claim that the filesystem is clean afterwards**. The operational consequence is §7.7's: one worktree per concurrent story is a *requirement*, not an optimisation, because a shared fixture tree cannot be reclaimed after a killed run on Windows. A handle-leak check on the cancel path is owed and is registered as **S-CANCEL-WIN** (§10.4/§10.5). The outcome of the adapter's own cancel is `StepError::Cancelled { by }` and never `NonZero`, `Timeout` or `Schema` — AC-225's WHEN is "terminated by the adapter's own cancel" and its classifier is keyed on the **cancel token, not on the exit code** ("exits 143" is a POSIX observation recorded per platform; on Windows the victim exits 1) — with a negative test that an *externally* killed child does not silently classify as `Cancelled` (AC-225; ADR-0099) [documented, platform docs: SIGTERM → 143, no result message]. A cancel that races a completion is resolved by the durable mark, not by the future's state.

SM-068 measures descendant PID count after cancel, with a target of zero survivors and zero dispatches whose result depends on a detached process. Its baseline is F22's incident record, not a rate, and its kill is that a survivor in production reopens F22's unestablished cause and blocks any design treating cancel as reclaiming the account's headroom.

### 3.9 Decisions this section records

| Decision | Alternatives considered | Why this one | Evidence | ADR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Clean-break single Rust binary owning the loop | keep the JS orchestrator; embed the loop in a harness plugin | **No difference was detectable, and the instrument could not have detected one** — detection **23/23 vs 23/23**, McNemar **p=0.625**, in-session **1.72×** the cost ($97.39 vs $56.47). The primary metric **saturated**: the result is "no discriminating power", not "headless wins on quality"; 20/20 across two harder tiers, blind and sighted — **saturated in all four cells, so no separation was detectable there either** | [measured, F23, F26] | ADR-0001, ADR-0002 |
| Five named ports, sync traits, **one fake each; four real adapters in Phase 1** (SQLite, Claude Code, Node, Rust — two behind `Toolchain`); no Postgres adapter | ports at every seam; no ports at all; generic ports; enum dispatch per M-DI-HIERARCHY | A port exists where a substitute is named or the fake alone pays for it; the fake is what lets the kernel be tested at $0 (AC-498). The `dyn` boundary diverges from Microsoft's M-DI-HIERARCHY ranking (generics, then enum, then `dyn`) on a stated ground — one fake per port swapped at one composition root, a small call count, scripted responders rather than sans-io mocks — and the boundary is a bare trait object, not a newtype (M-AVOID-WRAPPERS targets wrappers that hide a type's own API; the port trait *is* the API) [documented, Microsoft Rust guidelines, fetched 2026-09-10; PIA-045] | [documented] proposal §4.1 port table; AC-481; ADR-0094 for the fourth adapter | ADR-0003, ADR-0076, ADR-0094, ADR-0099 |
| Git is not a port | a `Vcs` port with a gix and a shell adapter | The git surface has measured behaviour and no alternative VCS is named | [measured, F12, F19] | ADR-0003 |
| Port traits stay sync; `.await` confined to adapters; cancel is the composition root's token; ports are `Arc<dyn Port + Send + Sync>` (the **EventStore** port is `Arc<Mutex<dyn EventStore>>`, because `dispatch` takes `&mut dyn EventStore` — **ADR-0115**, narrowing ADR-0099 clause 4) | `async-trait` on every dyn port; generic ports with native async fn; wait for nightly; `start()`/`wait(Handle)` | Native async fn in trait is not dyn-compatible (through 1.98.1, release notes read 2026-09-10); a replayable core must not own an executor; a dropped future does not reap a process tree; a sync `dispatch` leaves no caller to hold a handle | [documented, 1P async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08; release notes 2026-09-10] + [measured, F22, F38] | **ADR-0076**, **ADR-0099** |
| No durable-execution framework; the memo table is built here | Temporal Rust SDK (1.0.0 on sdk-core 0.9.0); graph-flow 0.8.0; `iopsystems/durable`; `dbos` 0.5.0; AWS Durable Execution SDK | Server mandate / not durable in-memory (postgres on by default) / Postgres + WASM / Postgres-arbitrated library / not Rust — re-argued after two of the corpus's three supporting facts were found false, and re-corrected for version currency on 2026-09-10 (E-0086-1) | [documented, crates.io + docs.rs + GitHub, fetched 2026-09-10] | **ADR-0086** (ERRATA E-0086-1; the four crates are banned by `cargo-deny`, ADR-0100) |
| Determinism ban list as a build failure, not a convention | rely on review; rely on a runtime to detect at replay | No runtime enforces it here, so a violation would be a latent silent memoisation bug; the list is convergent, independently re-derived by AWS | [documented, 1P AWS + Temporal, fetched 2026-09-08] | ADR-0022, ADR-0086 |
| Content-addressed, loop-aware memo key, per step | activity-level key only; iteration-blind key | An iteration-blind key makes loops unable to learn; a single-card activity key misses subgraph changes | [documented, P9b finding A18 — **no n, no figure**] | ADR-0021, ADR-0043→ADR-0059 |
| Three budgets, never shared; layered retry; typed degradation | one shared budget; a global retry count; silent substitution | Independence is a type-level guarantee; configuration failures are not weather | [measured, F7, F8, F20] for the never-retried classes | ADR-0037, ADR-0022 |

Every one resolves to a row in §10's spike register (ADR-0012); none is discharged in prose here.

| Open item | Why it matters here | Hedge in this design | Named spike / consumer | Gates Phase 1? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ChangeSummary` field set — **BLOCKED** | The `Toolchain` port's return type; the five toolchain-keyed kinds of ADR-0073 depend on it | Only the port *shape* is asserted (AC-497 PROVISIONAL): a `Binary` classification means a typed value and no textual diff of that path; the type is an opaque non-finalised newtype in `rk-ports` (ADR-0099) *(amended 2026-09-24, `s8a`, ADR-0165: no longer so. `ChangeSummary` is an enum defined only in `rk-ports`, with a `Text` arm finalised now and a `Binary` slot whose payload is sealed until S-BIN reports at `s8i`; S-BIN now gates the `Binary` arm and its body only)* | **S-BIN**, unrun [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: run 2026-10-14, ten records valid at corpus `835b122`; F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*, fix the `Binary` arm's four fields and route an item carrying a `Binary` path to the human gate] · consumer: Phase-1 `ChangeSummary` type design and the port's `change_summary` body | **Yes** (type design and `change_summary` body only — ADR-0093 decision 4) |

No claim that gix can write a trailer | **S-GIX/a** (read check), **S-GIX/b** (write half), **S-GIX/c** (clone), each against `gix = "=0.87.1"` · consumer: the `rk-git` read and write paths | **S-GIX/a: Yes for the read-side owner of `rev-parse`; the fallback is decided.** /b, /c: No |
