## 5. Typed artifacts, the store, and durable state

### 5.1 The direction of travel: types render to markdown, and markdown never travels back

Every activity output is a Rust type; the harness receives its schema, the kernel validates the value at the boundary, and the store **renders** markdown from the value. No code path recovers a typed field from rendered prose. The rule is not stylistic — it is what the corpus census forces, and it is enforced by dependency reachability rather than by discipline.

| Rule | What it forbids | Instrument (all to be built — `crates/` is empty and there is no CI) | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Markdown is an output of the type, never an input to control flow | any store export that takes `&str` markdown and returns a typed field; any markdown-parsing crate reachable from store code outside `store::locator` | module-boundary architecture test; `cargo deny` / `cargo tree --invert` rule; exported-items test — **AC-120** | S-MIG: gate results free-form in **39/156** implementation reports and **19/23** reviews, so there is nothing to parse [measured, one corpus] · ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075 |
| Rendering is a pure function of (typed value, template version) | a renderer that reads the prior section text; non-deterministic output across OS or working directory | golden-file test on both CI targets; `proptest` over every artifact type; a differential render over two prior contents; a sentinel-erasure unit test — **AC-121**, **AC-123** | design rule, proposal §4.4 [documented]. **Precondition:** AC-121's cross-OS half is checkable only if CI runs two OS targets; with one target it is unverified and is marked so, not assumed |
| One write path: only `rk-store` writes bytes under a managed artifact tree | a filesystem-write handle to the artifact tree exported from `rk-store`; an out-of-band edit that leaves no record | architecture test on exported items plus a `trybuild` compile-fail case — **AC-122**; digest-comparison `doctor` rule with a control fixture — **AC-159**, whose GIVEN is "a managed artifact whose structured-section or frontmatter digest differs" (a `reconcile()` that accepted a prose-section edit counts as the write record — ADR-0105 clause 9) | proposal §4.4 line 94 [documented]; **the crate-level boundary is this design's own rule**, not the proposal's (PIA-043). AC-159's instrument is a content digest, **not mtime**: a clone or a format-on-save advances mtime with identical content, and an mtime-preserving write hides a real mutation |
| The locator is a locator, not a grammar — **an anchor is a heading at the template's declared level for that section; the slice runs from the line after the anchor to the next heading at that level or shallower, or end of file; deeper headings inside the slice are prose bytes and not anchors** [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-033] | fuzzy, first-match or nearest-match anchor resolution; any typed field populated from sliced bytes | signature test that the return carries a byte range and no content-derived field; adversarial decoy corpus (fenced block, indented block, HTML comment, blockquote, a deeper-heading decoy inside the slice, a same-level duplicate inside prose); zero-match and duplicate-match error tests — **AC-149**, **AC-150**, **AC-151** | proposal §4.4 "a heading-anchored slice, not a grammar" [documented]; F33's lint that "accepted 5 guesses silently" is the failure mode being avoided [measured] |
| Opaque prose is accepted whole | any store path that inspects, validates, lints or summarises prose content | integration test writing arbitrary bytes (headings included) into a prose section and asserting acceptance — **AC-152** | S-MIG: everything designated opaque-by-design (3,100+ sections) lifted opaque with no exceptions — and S-MIG itself calls this the trivial half of its result [measured] |
| An edit inside a rendered structured section is typed drift — surfaced, never applied | applying a rendered-section edit to a typed value; re-importing edited text through a parser or a narrow LLM step | mutate-a-section integration test asserting a `structured_drift` event and an unchanged typed value; re-render equality after the drift record exists — **AC-124**, **AC-125**, **AC-153** | proposal §4.4, ADR-0005, ADR-0006 [documented] |
| Vouched roots and template bumps are different drift kinds | reporting a human-supplied root's change as blocking; reporting a template-version bump as human drift; silently re-stamping | table-driven test over {vouched root, rendered section} × {changed, unchanged} — **AC-127**; template-version drift kind test with the template version inside the section digest input — **AC-128** | ADR-0025 (vouched roots) [documented]; the old redkiln's `template_sig`/`rendered_sig` drift is the named failure to avoid [documented] |

**What the census can and cannot carry.** Every corpus share above comes from S-MIG — **1,312 files / 6,877 sections at corpus pin `47faec9f`, zero model cost** — and S-MIG has **no F-number**; it is cited by name. Its own instrument disclosure travels with every figure: the lifters were iterated four times against this corpus before the recorded run, over one repository at one frozen SHA, so these are shares under a locator tuned to the corpus, **not a blind parse**, and no interval is computed on any of them [measured, one corpus]. The quantity this subsection's central rule would most like measured — how often humans actually edit a rendered structured section — **has never been measured at all**. SM-037 establishes it on first use; F19's 12/174 = 7% is *world* drift under a human gate, a different quantity, and is cited as the nearest neighbour only. SM-037's kill is a per-section structured-drift rate that has **not fallen after 20 items**, which moves that field to opaque prose or to a CLI command.

### 5.2 Layout C, at the revision-4 refinements

Layout C — typed frontmatter, plus rendered read-only digest-stamped structured sections, plus declared opaque prose sections; machine-only artifacts as JSON sidecars rendered to `.md` on demand (**AC-144**) — stands unchanged from ADR-0006. ADR-0075 withdraws two clauses of ADR-0005/ADR-0006 and refines the rest against the census. Both predecessors stay `accepted`; this is a partial supersession, and each clause below is a type decision the census forced, not a parser defect.

| # | Refinement (ADR-0075) | Measured basis [measured, S-MIG unless noted] | What pins it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The AC type is `{ id, criterion: text, gwt: Option<Gwt>, verification: Option<text> }` — permissive by construction, with `Gwt { given: Text, when: Text, then: Text, and: Vec<Text> }`; **migration never populates `gwt` or `verification` from criterion text — both are authored fields from the first CLI write**, so over all 153 specs at the pin every AC round-trips byte-identical with both absent (AC-454; ERRATA E-0075-2) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-033] | **19 of 153 shipped specs (12%)** write criteria with no GIVEN/WHEN/THEN or no verification note — **76 and 87 loss lines** (75/85 distinct ids; 131 ACs with a loss, 29 lacking both, of 208 ACs in the 19 specs; the per-AC sub-counts first stated were cap-truncated at `loss[:6]`, E-0075-1); acceptance criteria reclassify at **88%** strict against a 95% pre-registration | **AC-137** (minimal value accepted, empty slots rendered visibly empty and never fabricated) |
| 1a | The **policy**, not the type, decides what a missing `verification` blocks — per stage, per rigor | policy relaxation was free in F31 (lenient 9/23 blocked, detection unchanged) — **but that is measured against CLEAN, whose ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 (57%), so it is an upper bound and never a point estimate** | **AC-138** (table-driven {blocks, advises} × {present, absent}; the block is computed from the policy row, not a constant in the type) |
| 1b | The Phase-1 **default** for that row is a recorded human decision, not a spike result | no instrument discriminates the two defaults; F37's nearest spike is **powerless at n=3** — every Wilson interval overlaps by construction [measured] | **AC-139**, routed to a human gate and left there; the machine-checkable consequence is that the value is a data change (AC-138) |
| 1c | An AC with no per-AC discriminating test renders as **unpinned**, never as a pass | F25's AC-015 probe: removing step 7 turned the suite RED, while inverting the ordering left **36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, n=1 AC, one operator] | **AC-140** (the rendered token for "no discriminating test" is distinct from every pass token) |
| 2 | Gate results are **emitted by the toolchain and rendered**, never parsed | free-form in **39/156** reports (25%) and **19/23** reviews (83%) — note the 23-file denominator; the review gate table reclassifies at **17%**, so there is no grammar to find | **AC-141** (gate-outcome constructor reachable only from the toolchain adapter; a report whose prose says "all gates passed" with no typed record renders no passing gate) |
| 2a | A missing emission is a **typed error against the activity**, never a rendered outcome | F20: an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently voids `--json-schema` — `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success` [measured] | **AC-142** (absent is never rendered as pass, fail, or unknown-treated-as-pass) |
| 3 | **One sidecar per companion kind**, each with its own registered schema — never a shared union | companion frontmatter lifted at **42%** over **five of the eleven** `companion.*` kinds the census holds (plus `ledger`), below the 80% threshold (ERRATA E-0075-2 — the nineteen-of-forty-five figure was that five-kind subset), and companions are three shapes, not one; 10 of 28 carry no frontmatter at all | **AC-143** (registry-completeness test enumerating exactly six; a companion kind with no schema cannot be written). **Settled 2026-09-11** (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #3): the Phase-1 kind list is AC-143's five plus `ledger` — `_integration`, `_grounding`, `_decomposition`, `_review`, `_ledger` — and the other eight census kinds (closure, design, design-review, slices, storymap, implementation, plan, retrospective) are Phase-2 kinds `redkiln-migrate` lifts as opaque prose (ADR-0075 cl. 6) |
| 4 | **`plan.md` does not exist** — removed from the human-facing set, unreadable if found | **110 of 110** were the unfilled `1.` scaffold with template prose; planning lived in `_plan.md`, `_storymap.md` and `spec.md` | **AC-145** (no producer declares it, no template renders it, a `plan.md` in a managed directory is reported as unmanaged and never parsed) |
| 5 | Every activity input emits an **`artifact.read`** event carrying kind, item id, `schema_version` and the tree hash the read was served at | the pathology this event class exists to expose is clause 4 — an artifact nobody read, invisible for the life of the corpus because nothing instruments downstream reads. **[inferred]**: S-MIG measured that `plan.md` was the unfilled scaffold in **110/110** cases, which establishes that a dead artifact existed and was never filled; that nothing instrumented *reads* is a property of the old system asserted from the absence of any such event, not a measured share, and §5.11 separately records that the `doctor` threshold is unset by measurement | **AC-146** ("an event", not "exactly one" — proposal §4.4 licenses emission, not uniqueness); **AC-147** (`doctor` lists produced-but-never-read stages) |
| 6 | Three section kinds fall below the **80% reclassify threshold** and are carried as **opaque prose**, located and rendered verbatim | implementation-report gate sections **75%**, companion frontmatter **42%**, review gate table **17%** — against the record-weighted **typed-slot overall of 96.0%** (3,011 / 3,138 — Lifted / (Lifted + LiftedWithLoss + Failed) over typed slots; 98.3% only by counting LiftedWithLoss as lifted). The **98%** this row first quoted is the *spec-section slot only*, its own S-MIG slot, which does **not** contain impl-report gates, companion frontmatter or the review gate table [measured, uncapped re-run of `census-s-mig.py` at the pin; ERRATA E-0075-1; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-078] | ADR-0075 clause 6. Typing a below-threshold kind is forbidden; so is silently dropping one |
| 7 | Per-AC mutation evidence is a first-class column; `unknown` is a distinguishable value; the mutant itself is **author-declared per AC** (`mutation: { patch, suite }` or `not-feasible(reason)`, ADR-0104) | the column exists in **0 of 156** historical implementation reports [documented, proposal §4.6 only; not re-verified — ERRATA E-0019-1]; TDD evidence migrates `mutation: unknown` on 142 of 156 rows | ADR-0075 clause 7 — forbids rendering `unknown` as a pass, a blank or an omission. SM-044 targets the 0/156 moving off zero and explicitly does **not** target the 12% to zero |
| 8 | Gate-checked criteria are phrased as **EARS** single-trigger statements by preference; `gwt` stays optional | phrasing preference only, from the research pass [documented; Mavin et al., IEEE RE'09] — **no enforcement, and no measurement on this corpus** | ADR-0075 clause 8. The corpus and the research pass disagree here and both are acted on: the *type* stays permissive because the census requires it, the *phrasing* moves to EARS because the external evidence is about checkability |

**Two things this layout deliberately does not settle.** First, `doctor`'s produced-unread flag **never removes an artifact from a required set** — removal is a human pack edit, recorded as such, because the selection engine's context-vector source is **BLOCKED** (F36: the kill fired 4 of 4, modal `R` identical on only 5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20 **items** — each computed across all ten draws of that item — against an 80% line; ADR-0079, status `superseded` — ADR-0090; corrected 2026-09-15, this read *"status `proposed`"* — see §4.1. Arm D has since been **scored**: no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar and `auth` scores **0.00** (0/12 [0.00, 0.24] after the key fix, E-0089-2) [measured, F39], which discharges AC-071's fourth clause only and opens a second block — on the *derived* source — under ADR-0089). **AC-148** is written precisely under that block, and SM-038's kill says the same thing: an artifact kind never read across 20 items is a *candidate* for removal, never an automatic one. Second, the `doctor` threshold in clause 5 — "zero reads over N runs" — is **not set by any measurement**; S-MIG establishes that a dead artifact existed and was invisible, not how many runs of silence should flag one [inferred]. SM-038 carries it as a rolling 20-item window pending a first measurement.

**`ChangeSummary` is blocked here, and stays blocked.** The type that describes a change no diff renders is gated on **S-BIN — registered and UNRUN** [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: run 2026-10-14, ten records valid at corpus `835b122`; F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*, fix the `Binary` arm's four fields and route an item carrying a `Binary` path to the human gate; AC-154 stays PROVISIONAL, with its reason, by its 2026-10-14 note] — against the real Unreal project (ADR-0008, whose own text records that S-BIN had not been run). **AC-154 pins the rendering direction only** (binary entries render from typed fields; no binary content is embedded, parsed or diffed as text) and is **unverifiable until S-BIN runs** — its fixture cannot be written, and it must not be counted as covered in the runbook. Nothing in this section freezes that type's field set; it is an opaque non-finalised newtype in `rk-ports` meanwhile (ADR-0099). *(amended 2026-09-24, `s8a`, ADR-0165: no longer so. `ChangeSummary` is an enum defined only in `rk-ports`, with a `Text` arm finalised now and a `Binary` slot whose payload is sealed until S-BIN reports at `s8i`; S-BIN now gates the `Binary` arm and its body only)* S-BIN gates the type and the port's `change_summary` body, and nothing else in Phase 1 (ADR-0093 decision 4); see §10.

### 5.3 Schema design rules, and the golden snapshot that checks them

| Rule | Status | Instrument | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `additionalProperties: false` on every object node | design rule, externally corroborated | CI schema lint over the exported set; an open-object fixture that must fail — **AC-129** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; the vendor's published structured-output limitations require it on all objects and reject any other value [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]. F20 is **motivation, not evidence**: it is a tool-allowlist defect, not a statement about `additionalProperties` |
| Every categorical field is a **closed enum with an explicit absent/unknown variant**; no bare `String` standing in for a category | design rule | enumerated-domain registry vs exported schema; a free-`String` fixture that must fail — **AC-130** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; F36's mechanism as motivation — `unknown` is treated as `high` by position, so an axis oscillating unknown↔low moves the band its full width [measured] |
| **One rule, stated identically in AC-131, AC-157, here and the runbook** (ADR-0095 decision 1): **no `oneOf` at any node** — data-carrying enums are written `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` and their derived schema passes through a post-derive `schemars::transform::Transform` inside the export that rewrites a `kind`-discriminated `oneOf` into an equivalent `anyOf`, so the snapshot sees the exported shape; **no union nested inside a union** — no `anyOf` or type array with a descendant `anyOf` or type array; `additionalProperties: false` at every object level; `required` first | design rule — a constraint on the Rust types plus one transform, not a library guarantee [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-005] | schema lint walking the exported tree; a nested-union fixture — **AC-131**; Checkpoint 5's `grep -rnE 'oneOf\|anyOf'` over the snapshot directory expecting `oneOf` → 0, with the nesting check in the snapshot test itself; plus the golden snapshot below | proposal §4.4 [documented]; **schemars 1.2.2 renders `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` as `oneOf`** when the variants are unique (`anyOf` otherwise; `schema_exprs.rs` :347-374, :484 — generated locally against the pinned version) [measured; documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars, fetched 2026-09-10], so the prescribed shape failed the earlier rule's own assertion; the vendor's page lists `anyOf`/`allOf` and not `oneOf` [documented, platform.claude.com structured-outputs, fetched 2026-09-10]; ADR-0095 (supersedes ADR-0084's schema clause) |
| **≤ 24 optional parameters and ≤ 16 union-typed parameters** — any property whose schema uses `anyOf` or a type array, nullable `Option<T>` included — **counted per exported schema**; required properties before optional ones | **The vendor's documented "Schema complexity limits"** (24 optional parameters; 16 parameters using `anyOf` or type arrays; 20 strict tools; 180 s compile timeout — **per request, across all strict tool schemas plus the JSON output schema**), adopted per exported schema under a stated precondition: one output schema per dispatch and no strict user-defined tools on the adapter's command line, a GIVEN in AC-132/AC-157 and a lint on the assembled argv, so adding a strict tool later re-opens the budget explicitly (ADR-0095 decision 1) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-005] | property-count lint with a 25-optional fixture — **AC-132**; union-typed-parameter count with the 17-parameter fixture — **AC-157** (branches-at-a-node is withdrawn as the quantity: a nullable `Option<T>` is one under the vendor's count and zero under a branch count); property-order lint — **AC-158**; the vendor's failure shape observed by **S-SCHEMA-CAP** (§10.5), because F20 says the failure is silent | [documented, platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs, "Schema complexity limits" table, fetched 2026-09-10; present in a Wayback capture of 2026-09-07 — the earlier reading that the page "contains no such caps" was a truncation artefact, ERRATA E-0084-2]. **Precondition:** AC-158 is observable only if the export preserves insertion order (`serde_json` `preserve_order`); without it the criterion is unverifiable and the runbook must say so |
| A free-text `rationale` beside every judgement-bearing enum, and **every** shipped enum registered as judgement-bearing or not | design rule | registry-completeness test (an unregistered enum fails the lint) plus a rationale-sibling rule — **AC-133** | proposal §4.4 [documented]; F29 as motivation — 19/19 rejections were on target while class means wobbled (Reproduced 0.70 > MutationSurvived 0.37 > NarrowedPin 0.10 > control 0.00) [measured]. **Limit:** the lint cannot tell whether the yes/no is *right*; that is the human gate AC-156 |
| Enum values are compared case-insensitively and stored normalised | design rule | per-enum casing unit tests plus a persisted-form store test — **AC-134** | proposal §4.4 [documented]. The brief carries **no casing measurement** — F31's 52/52 was not stratified by casing [inferred] |
| A `schema_version` per type; a schema change is a **migration** | design rule | schema-snapshot diff that fails an unversioned change, with the previous version's snapshot retained — **AC-135**; named missing-migration error on read, never best-effort deserialisation — **AC-136**; SM-046 counts unmigratable artifacts, target zero at every release tag | proposal §4.4 [documented]; the old redkiln's `template_sig`/`rendered_sig` drift is the named failure. **Limit:** a semantics-only change that leaves the schema byte-identical passes AC-135 — the snapshot sees field sets, not meaning. Say so in the runbook rather than claiming coverage |

**None of the rules above is self-enforcing, and the instrument that enforces them is the golden-schema snapshot test (ADR-0084).** Every type crossing the closed-output boundary has its generated `schemars` output — after the export's `oneOf` → `anyOf` transform for `kind`-discriminated enums — snapshotted, and the test asserts on the generated JSON that it contains no `oneOf` anywhere and no union nested inside a union, that every object carries `additionalProperties: false`, that required properties precede optional ones, and that the 24-optional and 16-union-typed-parameter budgets hold per exported schema; data-carrying enums that must cross the boundary are written `#[serde(tag = "kind")]`, a schema that has not passed the transform may not be exported, and a changed snapshot **is** a schema change and is reviewed as one (ADR-0095). The failure class it guards is F20's — closed-output violations fail **open**, at exit 0 with `subtype: success`, so there is no loud failure for a careful author to notice [measured, F20].

**The test itself, its pins, its three disclosures and its CI wiring are §9.5's; this section owns only the constraint on the types.** Two of those disclosures bear on how the rules above may be read and are named here rather than assumed: the 24/16 budgets are **the vendor's Schema complexity limits, per request, adopted per exported schema under the stated precondition** — the earlier statement that they were this project's own and not a platform constraint is withdrawn (ADR-0095; E-0084-2) — and F31's schema conformance of **52/52 = 100%** measures that *emitted payloads conformed to a schema* and says nothing about whether the *generated schema* satisfies these constraints [measured, F31; ADR-0084].

### 5.4 Durable state: the tier-1 test

Two substrates, deliberately redundant, split by one question: **if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?** If yes, the state may not live only in the database.

| Tier | Contents | Substrate | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Tier 1 — decision-bearing** | stage transitions, gate opened/answered, verdicts, slice seals | **git first**: trailers on a checkpoint commit, or an **empty commit** carrying the trailer when no checkpoint is due *(amended 2026-09-29, `s8f` build, package S8F-P8-DOCTOR-RULES-AND-NOTES; `s8f` §8f.10; D21-43 N10; the landing record **ADR-0188**, proposed *(dated 2026-10-02, `s8g` close: ADR-0188 accepted on D21-117 at `s8f`'s close)*: a content checkpoint commit is made on `rk-run/<item>` **at every committed step that changed the tree** — the step's commit and its content checkpoint are one boundary act, the step's commit row anchored to it — and **at every tier-1 boundary when one is due** (the staged tree differs from `HEAD^{tree}`); a step that changed nothing makes no commit, and a refused landing lands nothing)* *(dated 2026-09-29, s8f build, package R6FIX-8f; refuting round 6's crossfile-safety minor; D21-114, TAKEN PROVISIONALLY under D21-105; ADR-0188 decision 2's dated note, proposed: a **third landing point, at entry**: `Content::Entry`, a trailerless, body-less `rk entry <item> <stage> <run_id>` content commit, made when the entry pass wrote (an accepted prose edit, an applied `move-to-prose`, a stored vouched root) and no drift gate's `Gate:` commit lands the write, before the stage's first read; it carries no tier-1 row, only its `git_op` rows)* | **AC-160** (a state field with no `tier1`/`tier2` annotation is a compile error, witnessed by a `trybuild` fixture — not a silent default); **AC-161** (delete the database; the selected next action is equal under the canonical serialiser, with no manual repair); **AC-162** (the trailer commit exists before any store row for that transition is readable, and a failed git write leaves **zero** store rows — fault-injection, not a structural lint); **AC-163** (empty commit, never a store-only record) |
| **Tier 2 — telemetry** | usage, cost, headroom, wall clock, drift observations, inner retries | event store only; rebuildable or disposable | **AC-164** (truncate tier 2: the stage-level next action is unchanged and `doctor` classifies the loss as **telemetry-only**, never as corruption — ADR-0097 narrows AC-161/AC-164 to the stage-level next action); the rest of the tier-2 contract is **AC-365–AC-370** and **AC-397–AC-398** in the AC register's events-and-cost section (`acceptance-criteria.md` §10) — rebuildability, disposability, the rebuild-from-git path and what a partial tier-2 loss may and may not change — with **AC-363** on degradation and **AC-380** naming `rate_limit_event` as the **sole** headroom source, and **SM-115**/**SM-116** as the tier's own measures. They are cited, not restated; §7.4's attribution table is their point of use. F6's 0.01 `utilization` quantisation — per-run cost is not measurable — is *why* cost sits here [measured; the cost model itself is §7's] |
| **Step memo** | the per-step memo entries and their keys (§3.5) | **tier 2 — rebuildable by re-execution, never by rebuild**: `doctor --rebuild` reproduces the stage-level next action and the gate records the trailers carry; step outputs and memo hits are recomputed, not restored (ADR-0097 decision 1; supersedes the reading under which AC-161 promised step-grain rebuild) [added 2026-09-11, PIA-008] | **AC-161**/**AC-164** as narrowed; AC-187 scoped to a run whose EventStore survives; AC-160's fixture drops the memo from its rebuild assertion |

**Two trailer axes, because one is provably insufficient.** `Story:`-trailer resume alone loses the rejection history: over **360 trailer commits across 74 slices**, **15/74 = 20.3%** of slices passed through a rejected state and 21.6% were sealed more than once [measured, F12; F12's registered primary was uncomputable at n=1 and that re-scoping is disclosed]. A `Slice-Verdict:` axis is therefore required (**ADR-0109**, the rule's home — ADR-0018 decides rewrite events and the wrong-worktree refusal, not the tier split or the trailer axes; PIA-082). The third axis, `Gate:`, is written when a mark is opened; its record semantics — `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` and the terminal expiry path — are §6's, and ADR-0017 enters here only at that boundary. Resume from `Story:` alone is forbidden. A trailer that does not parse against the declared grammar below is surfaced as drift with the offending text attached, and is **never skipped and never coerced into a state transition** (**AC-165**, AC-183).
**The trailer grammar, stated once** (ADR-0105 clause 3; AC-444's expected drift counts and AC-183's parse rule are pinned to this table) [added 2026-09-11, PIA-040]:

| Key | Value (ABNF, informal) | Anchoring | Vocabulary | Rule for the rest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Story:` | ~~`<project> "/" <slug>` — one item id~~ `<project> "/" <slug> [SP <stage>]`, split on whitespace first: one token is the **legacy stage-less mark** (admitted read-only, folded as membership with no stage); two tokens are a **stage-bearing mark**, the stage token carrying no whitespace and no `/`; the reserved stage token `closed` is the **lane's end** (a pack may not declare a stage of that name) *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0183**, proposed; D21-41 a, D21-43 N9: this cell read *"`<project> "/" <slug>` — one item id"*)* | Column 0 of a trailer line, no leading whitespace (`reconcile.rs` reads `strip_prefix` without `trim_start`) | `closed` (reserved) | A bare id with no `project/` form is recorded as `bare_story_ids`; ~~a second identical `Story:` for one slug is drift `duplicate-story-trailer`~~ a second identical `(project, slug, stage)` is drift `duplicate-story-trailer`, counted, not a transition; three or more tokens, a stage token carrying `/`, or a two-token value with no `project/` form is drift `unparsable-trailer`; the latest stage-bearing mark per `(project, slug)` wins, commits folded oldest-first, and a stage-less mark never sets or resets it *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0183**, proposed)* |
| `Slice-Verdict:` | `<project> "/" <slice> SP <verdict>` — exactly two whitespace-separated tokens | Column 0 | `approved` \| `changes-requested`, compared case-insensitively and stored lower-case; latest wins, commits folded oldest-first | A value without the `project/slice` form is drift `unqualified-slice-verdict`; a third token is ignored by the parser and is drift under the declared grammar |
| `Gate:` | ~~`<token> SP <tree_hash> SP <deadline>` — the `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` triple, `tree_hash` 40-hex, `deadline` RFC 3339~~ `<token> SP <tree_hash> SP <deadline> SP <kind> SP <reach> SP <state> [SP <outcome>…]` — `tree_hash` 40-hex, `deadline` **integer unix seconds** (unsigned decimal), `kind` without whitespace, `reach` a positive integer; the state's outcome tokens: `answered <option>`, `expired fail` \| `expired escalate` \| `expired auto-decide <verdict>`, none for `opened` and `voided` *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed; D21-42 8b, D21-43 N6 and N8: this cell read *"the `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` triple, `tree_hash` 40-hex, `deadline` RFC 3339"*)* | Column 0 | `opened` \| `answered` \| `expired` \| `voided` *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed)* | **A `Gate:` value that is not ~~the triple~~ the widened value's six to eight tokens is legacy drift — counted, surfaced with the offending text, never a transition** (F12's corpus carries such lines, and the old triple is now among them); a value with that count whose tokens break their grammar — an RFC 3339 deadline included — is drift `unparsable-trailer`; the latest state per token wins, commits folded oldest-first *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed)* |
| any other key | — | — | — | **Unknown keys are ignored and counted**; `Fix:`, `Slice-Repair:` and `Baseline-Repair:` are the old redkiln's keys, read by `redkiln-migrate` only and not emitted by `rk-git` |

Every trailer write runs through the wrapper with `-c trailer.ifexists=add` (and `--if-exists=add` on any `interpret-trailers` call), because `git interpret-trailers` defaults `ifExists` to `addIfDifferentNeighbor` and would silently **not append** a repeated identical `Gate:` or `Slice-Verdict:` trailer [documented, git-scm interpret-trailers(1)]; with `-c core.hooksPath=<empty directory>`, so the target repository's hooks never run on a Redkiln commit; and with trailer-affecting config pinned via `-c`, so S-GIX/b's oracle and the wrapper agree over the fixture matrix (existing trailer block, no trailing newline, `---` divider, CRLF, duplicate token). The answer record is `git commit --allow-empty --trailer` on the run's branch, `commit-tree` + `update-ref` where the wrapper needs an exact parent (ADR-0105 clause 3).
**The event-kind table** (ADR-0105 clause 7; `rk-types` is the owning crate — the `EventKind` enum is closed, a kind not in this table is a schema change reviewed as one, and the tier column is the deleted-database test applied per kind) [added 2026-09-11, PIA-040]:

| Kind | Tier | Payload fields (named in this document) | Producing step |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `stage_transition` | 1 (trailer) | item, from, to, tree hash, the trailer commit | the kernel's FSM at a stage boundary |
| `gate_opened` | 1 (`Gate:` trailer) | `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`, kind, the failed predicates and each facet's evaluated value (`reason: unknown_facet, axis` for an abstention) | selection step 4 / a `gate` step; fresh on every re-reach (ADR-0105 clause 10) |
| `gate_answered` | 1 (`Gate:` trailer) | token, answer (the option id), tree hash at answer, **`explain_sufficiency { sufficed, toml_opened }`** — AC-435's required field, two data in one member: whether `explain` answered "why did this run" without the reviewer opening a TOML file, and whether a TOML was opened anyway (yes / no each; `answer` refuses without both — *corrected 2026-09-19, the operator's answer to the one-field-or-two question: SM-126's kill is the cell sufficed = yes AND opened = yes*), answerer *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 D-05 / RB-M-08: the AC-435 field is added — the register's THEN "recorded as a required field on the gate answer" is binding and the kernel wrote only `{token, tree_hash, verdict}`; the CLI flags are ADR-0129's required `--explain-sufficed <yes|no> --toml-opened <yes|no>`, the payload member `explain_sufficiency`. `answerer` was named here but never written by the kernel and its source was recorded nowhere: the intended source is the **git author identity read through the `rk-git` wrapper** at `answer` time — **confirmed 2026-09-19 by the operator (DECISIONS-2026-09-18 R-26 closed): the field stays, sourced through the wrapper** — the implementer confirms this at Step 7d or the field is dropped from the payload by a dated note here)* | `redkiln answer` |
| `gate_expired` | 1 (`Gate: … expired <action>` trailer *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed; D21-43 N6: until this date the row named no trailer and the expiry was appended to the store alone)*) | token, the terminal expiry action taken | the deadline read at the next entry (§6.3.4) |
| `slice_sealed` / verdict | 1 (`Slice-Verdict:` trailer) | slice, verdict *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P7; `s8e` §8e.8; **ADR-0186**, proposed: `project`, `slice` (the item record's DECLARED `slice`, never the item id, the project, a stage or the run id), `verdict` (`approved` \| `changes-requested`) and `stage` — the stage whose exit outcome is sealed, which scopes `terminal()`'s `Sealed` to that stage (D21-93); a row re-derived from the trailer by the heal or the rebuild carries no `stage`, which the trailer does not hold, and is scoped to the stage it folds in. It carries the interim `item` key beside the payload, ADR-0182)* | the lane's review stage *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P7: the review pass's EXIT OUTCOME, through the kernel's tier-1 boundary — `approved` when nothing blocks, `changes-requested` before `Exit::FindingsBlock` returns; ADR-0186 decision 3)* |
| `reconcile_started` | 2 | invocation id, HEAD, the drift set that followed, **`adapter_set`**, **`config_digest`** and **`RunOwnership { worktree, branch }`** *(amended 2026-09-20, Step 7d **RT-41** and **RT-47**, settling residual **R-47** on the operator's decision of that date. This cell read "invocation id, HEAD, the drift set that followed". Three members are added, and the reason each is HERE rather than on a row of its own is stated so it is not re-litigated: **(i)** AC-485's THEN names *"the adapter set recorded on the **run-start event**"*, and design P3 names a run-start event **no row in this table carried** — `EventKind::ALL` is 38 kinds and has no `run_started` [measured 2026-09-18, `crates/rk-types/src/event_kind.rs`]; **(ii)** ADR-0105 clause 8 and AC-529 both say a tier-1 write resolves the run-owned worktree *"from the store"* and **name no table and no event that carries the pair** [measured 2026-09-18: no `rk-store` table and no payload in this section]. Both are routed onto THIS row — the first row of every action-causing invocation, written by the CLI (`reconcile_at_entry` writes nothing) — rather than onto a minted `run_started` kind, because minting one edits `crates/rk-types/src/event_kind.rs` and `s7d` §7d.15's floor is that **"`rk-types`, `rk-ports`, `rk-store`, `rk-git` and the four adapters are not edited"**: the alternative was foreclosed by the stage's own constraint, not merely judged heavier. **Tier 2 is safe here, and the argument is stated rather than assumed:** ADR-0109 decision 2 requires that dropping the event store not change `redkiln run`'s next action, so every member of a tier-2 row must be RECOMPUTABLE without it — and all three are. `adapter_set` and `config_digest` rebuild from `.redkiln/config.toml`, and the worktree and branch rebuild deterministically from the item id (`<common-dir>/redkiln/worktrees/<item>/` on `rk-run/<item>`) *(dated 2026-09-27, `s8d-ii` build, P6, under ADR-0180, proposed *(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` close: accepted on D21-91, at `s8d-ii`'s close)*; D21-44: from `s8d-ii` the path is `.redkiln/worktrees/<item>/` under the main worktree, not `<common-dir>/redkiln/worktrees/<item>/`, derived in one place, `rk_git::worktree::derive`, from the main worktree `git worktree list --porcelain` names first and the item id; it still rebuilds deterministically, so the tier-2 argument stands)*. **None of the three is a decision the store alone holds**, which is the property that would have forced tier 1. This note is what AC-485 was PROVISIONAL on and what AC-529's CLI end was ARMED until — `s7d` §7d.18's *"claimed only once the ownership carrier record (RT-47) lands"*)* | every CLI entry that can cause an action (exactly one per invocation) |
| `gate_answer_void` | ~~2 (drift observation)~~ **1 (`Gate: … voided` trailer)** *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed; D21-43 N6: *"Every gate transition (opened, answered, expired, voided) is a git-first tier-1 event"*. This cell read *"2 (drift observation)"*)* | token, bound tree hash, observed tree hash | ~~`reconcile()` (§6.3.3)~~ **the kernel at entry**, through the tier-1 boundary function (`s8e` §8e.3; ADR-0181); `reconcile()` keeps only the observation and writes nothing to git (AC-174) *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P4; **ADR-0184**, proposed: this cell read *"`reconcile()` (§6.3.3)"*)* |

| `rewrite(old_sha → new_sha, mechanism)` | 2-derived (outside the AC-184 differential) | old, new, mechanism | `reconcile()` on a recorded mechanism only (§5.5) |

This column read "every activity output". ADR-0063 stores an admitted attachment *"as a human-supplied root"* and names no row; `answer` appends one `artifact.write` per admitted attachment and is the kind's **first producer anywhere** — nothing under `crates/rk-kernel/src` writes it [measured 2026-09-20]. **This takes nothing back from ADR-0116 and is therefore a dated note and not a supersession** (ADR-0092, which forbids an erratum that touches a `## Decision`): that record's `**What this forbids**` block reads *"Forbids an activity output with no `artifact.write` event"* — a **floor** on where the row is REQUIRED, not a **ceiling** on who may write one. The producing-step column states the obligation, so a further producer is an addition the record neither mandates nor forbids, and no Decision sentence is withdrawn. **On this row's `schema_version`**: it takes the kind's registered `schema` string, or `null` — `ArtifactKind` carries `schema: Option<String>` and **no version field at all** [measured 2026-09-20, `crates/rk-kernel/src/select/artifacts.rs`:59-65], so the id is written and a version is NOT invented; residual **R-49**)* *(added 2026-09-12, **ADR-0116**: AC-521's release check names it, and without the write side `doctor` cannot tell a produced-and-never-read artifact from one never produced)* *(amended 2026-09-29, `s8f` build, package S8F-P8-DOCTOR-RULES-AND-NOTES; `s8f` §8f.10; ADR-0189 decisions 7, 8 and 10, proposed *(dated 2026-10-02, `s8g` close: ADR-0189 accepted on D21-118 at `s8f`'s close)*: **the producers exist** — `rk-store`'s single write path `render::ArtifactWriter` appends one row per write carrying `kind`, `item`, `stage`, `run`, `schema_version`, `digest` and `path` (the managed file written in the run-owned worktree); `answer`'s attach row gains `stage` and `run` (read from the token's `opened` row) and **the attach's path carrier `attached_path`** (relative to the checkout in `/` form, else absolute; not `path`, which names a managed destination); the vouched root is stored at the next entry through the writer as `<Kind>.root<ext>`, with its own row)* |

| `worktree_assigned` | 2 (the next `run` re-enters the registered tree and writes it again; `answer` refuses fail-closed until it does) | `item`, `worktree`, `branch`, `reused` (a re-entered tree), `discarded` (every in-flight path the re-entry threw away, W2) | `rk-cli`'s `run`, at **every** run start, after the tree is entered; every tier-1 write resolves its `RunOwnership` from the latest one for its item and from nothing else *(added 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P5; **ADR-0185**, proposed; D21-42 8d)* |
| `stage_empty` | 2 (the `Story:` mark carries the entry either way) | `stage`, `reason` — one of the closed set `rk_types::event_kind::STAGE_EMPTY_REASONS` (`no_applicable_activity`) | the lane's entry into a stage whose plan is empty, as a row of the entry's tier-1 act, anchored to its commit (`s8e` §8e.6) *(added 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P5; **ADR-0185**, proposed; D21-42 7)* *(amended 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package R1FIX-8e; ADR-0185 decision 9's dated residual)* — anchored ONLY when the empty stage is the lane's FIRST (its entry act); on a stage entered by an ADVANCE the row is appended beside the advance's act, tier 2 and UNANCHORED, once per `(item, stage)`, because the advance is committed before the next stage's plan is selected |
| `git_op` | 2 (the commit it describes is the durable fact) | `GitInvocation`'s own members: `op`, `cwd`, `argv_digest`, `status`, `stdout`, `stderr_digest` | the kernel's tier-1 boundary (`rk_kernel::runtime::tier_one::commit_act`), one per git invocation of a committed act, anchored to that act's commit; none for a failed act; replay serves the boundary from these rows, and a journal holding none is a run that made no git write *(added 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P5; **ADR-0185**, proposed; D21-43 N7)* |
| `verify_completed` | 2 | `report_digest`, `selection`, `basis`, `basis_reason`, `passed`, `failed`, `selected_test_count`, `adapter`, `range: { base, head }`, `paths` (the changed paths), `toolchain_version`, `origin` (`"pass"` for the verify pass's call, `"<activity>@<version>/<step>"` for a catalog step's, and `"mutation/<ac>"` for the mutation harness's *(dated 2026-09-30, `s8g` build, package R2FIX-8g; ADR-0191 decision 8's dated note, proposed; the harness wrote it before this row named it)*), `stage` (the node's stage, carried as data and never compared) — `rk_types::verify::VerifyCompleted`, every member required, no `runner` member (D21-48 X15: the runner rides on the report, through `report_digest`); the interim `item` key **beside** the payload on ADR-0182's carrier, never in it (D21-46 R3) | the kernel, once per **completed** `Toolchain::test` port call, whatever activity or pass made it, at one site; a timed-out, killed or unspawnable call writes none (its `StepError` row does). AC-321's replay reads `range`, `paths` and `toolchain_version` back from it verbatim; AC-473's and AC-485's toolchain identity half reads its `origin = "pass"` rows *(added 2026-09-29, `s8g` build, package S8G-P2-RKTYPES; `s8g` §8g.4; D21-42 10b; the verify record **ADR-0191** decision 8, proposed *(dated 2026-10-02, `s8g` close: ADR-0191 accepted on D21-131)*; ADR-0116 decision 5: the row and the `rk-types` variant land in the same change)* |
| `ReplayDivergence { activity_id, recorded, recomputed }` | — (a halt, not a durable record) | activity, recorded, recomputed | replay (AC-007) |

A `doctor` rule row asserts no credential-shaped value in any retained field (ADR-0105 clause 7).

*(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, package S8E-P5; `s8e` §8e.4; **ADR-0185** decision 8, proposed.)* **RT-47's `RunOwnership { worktree, branch }` on the `reconcile_started` row is KEPT as tier-2 context, and no reader resolves ownership from it.** `answer`'s `recorded_ownership`, its one reader, is deleted: every tier-1 write resolves its `RunOwnership` from the latest `worktree_assigned` row for its item, through `ByItem`, and a mismatch is the typed `GitError::ForeignWorktree` (AC-529). There is one source of ownership.

*(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` build, §8e.10; **ADR-0182**, proposed.)* **The interim `item` key sits beside the payload, not in it.** Every kind above carries the owning item in one of three ways, fixed by `EventKind::item_key()` and ADR-0182 decision 3: **beside the event** (`Event::with_item`, persisted in `t2_event.item`, which `0002_gate_transitions` adds) for every item-scoped kind; **in the payload** for `rate_limit_reading` alone, where ADR-0163 put it; and **not item-scoped** for `memo_hit` / `memo_miss` / `memo_evicted`, `rewrite` and the three advisory-sink kinds. The key is outside `payload`, `event_json` and `canonical_events` (ADR-0131 decision 2; D21-46 R3), so no golden log's canonical digest moves, and `query(ByItem(x))` returns the rows keyed `x` plus, in the interim, the unkeyed rows. It moves to ADR-0131's reserved `item` tag when that wiring lands.

**The limit on all of it, stated once.** F19's **174/174** is losslessness of *suspension*, and F19 explicitly **never replayed the FSM** — so "the same next action" is **unproven**, not proven [measured]. **AC-188** is the instrument written to close it (replay the deterministic workflow from the log, assert sequence equality, and deny clock/RNG/filesystem symbols in the workflow module with a fixture that must fail), and **AC-161 is only as strong as AC-188**. Until AC-188 exists, SM-056 reports **byte-recoverability, not decision-equivalence**, and this document claims no more. F38 adds mid-graph step granularity — **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** across a real `taskkill /T /F` — at **n=3, PARTIAL** (3 draws of a pre-registered 27+3, $3.01 of a $40 cap; all three no-kill controls unrun) [measured, F38].

### 5.5 Keying, orphans, and rewrite events

Every repo-referencing row is keyed by SHA or tree hash — never a branch, never a path. Rebase, reset, amend and force-push therefore produce **orphaned** rows, and an orphan is a signal, not a defect to be tidied away.

| Rule | Criterion | Evidence / source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every repo-referencing key column declares a key domain of `sha` or `tree`; anything else is a build failure | **AC-166** — schema lint over the migration files, with a `branch TEXT` key fixture that must fail. (A lint cannot recognise "a branch name" by inspection; a declared domain makes it decidable) | proposal §4.5 rule 2 [documented]; redkiln's existing verify cache already keys on `(cmd, base, tree)` |
| Orphans are **reported, never deleted and never re-anchored as a side effect of the report** | **AC-167** (orphan set equals the expected anchors; row count unchanged before and after); **AC-168** (no `DELETE` against a tier-1 table exists outside `doctor --rebuild`, and adding one fails the build) | ADR-0018 "surfaced, never deleted" [documented] |
| A successor is adopted only on a **recorded mechanism** — patch-id equality, `ORIG_HEAD`, or a reflog entry the operation itself wrote — and the re-anchor is preceded by a `rewrite(old_sha → new_sha, mechanism)` event that retains the old anchor | **AC-169** (a re-anchor with no preceding event fails) | ADR-0018 [documented] |
| Where no mechanism identifies a successor, the row **stays orphaned and drift-visible**; no successor is inferred from branch name, file path, commit message or timestamp proximity | **AC-170** — negative test whose rewritten commit shares message and path set but differs in patch-id: zero `rewrite` events, one surviving orphan | ADR-0018 [documented] |
| An orphan that blocks the next action routes to a **human gate** carrying the orphan set, both SHAs and the diff | **AC-171** — routed to a human gate; the machine-checkable half is only that the gate *opens*, never a silent continuation | **No finding in the brief measures this decision.** "Is this the same work" is a judgement about intent; a rebase that also edits content produces no patch-id match |

SM-050 has **no baseline**: the corpus offers only a different quantity — F12's 20.3% ever-rejected and 21.6% resealed, which is a **rejection/reseal rate, not a history-rewrite rate**, and must not be read as a bound on either [measured, F12]. Its target is that every orphan resolves to a `rewrite` event or an opened gate, with the count of orphans that are neither at **0**, and its kill is a steady-state orphan population growing monotonically over 10 consecutive entries — the sign that "surfaced, never deleted" is producing an unusable backlog rather than an audit trail.

### 5.6 `reconcile()` at every entry, git → store, never the reverse

| Property | Criterion | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every CLI entry that can cause an action obtains decision-driving state **only** as the output of `reconcile()` for that invocation | **AC-172** — the planner accepts only a `Reconciled<State>` value, so the compile failure is the instrument; plus exactly one `reconcile_started` event per invocation | F19: drift **12/174 = 7%**, **9/79 = 11%** on gaps over 1 h against **3/95 = 3%** at or under [measured, n=174, **one operator, one repository**]; F18: **45–48%** of gates exceed 1 h [measured, one operator] |
| No cache, TTL, "unchanged since" flag, configuration or CLI flag skips it | **AC-173** — CLI surface snapshot test; `reconcile_started` on every entry regardless of elapsed time | brief §C.3 forces reconcile on every entry and forbids an optimisation that skips it [measured]; the rule's record is **ADR-0109** (ADR-0018 is `related` — it decides rewrite events and the wrong-worktree refusal, not this; PIA-082). **Note what is *not* claimed: no measurement of `reconcile()`'s own cost exists anywhere in the brief** — the frequency of drift is measured, the price of catching it is not |
| Reconcile mutates nothing in git | **AC-174** — runs to completion against a read-only git dir, with a before/after digest comparison of the git dir | proposal §4.5 rule 3 [documented] |
| Reconcile is idempotent | **AC-175** — `reconcile(reconcile(w)) == reconcile(w)` over corpus histories, with an empty second drift set | proposal §4.5 rule 3 [documented] |
| A gate answer bound to a tree hash that no longer matches is **void**, and the gate re-opens with the drift attached | **AC-176** — the reconcile-side half only; the gate record's own semantics are §6's | F19 + F18 as above; ADR-0017 at the boundary |

**Honest reading of the 3.6×.** SM-049's Wilson 95% intervals are overall [4.0%, 11.7%], ≤1 h [1.1%, 8.9%] and >1 h [6.1%, 20.3%] — **the two conditional intervals overlap**, and the risk ratio is 3.6 with CI [1.0, 12.9], Fisher exact p = 0.039: **marginal at n=174, one operator** — so 3.6× is a directional point estimate on one operator's single repository, never an established ratio [measured, F19; intervals and the RR recomputed, PIA-073, PIA-107]. SM-049 also carries an explicit detection rule, without which its "100% of drift caught at entry" target could never fail: drift counts as *missed* when a later `reconcile()` records an observation whose causing commit predates an action already taken. The claim "none was missed in 174" does not exist in the brief and is not made here — F19 reports 12 drift events and 174/174 lossless suspension, and neither is an oracle for drift the system failed to see.

### 5.7 One store per repository, at the common dir

| Rule | Criterion | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The store resolves to `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ and is shared by every worktree; **no second store file exists anywhere under a linked worktree** | **AC-178** — N worktrees, one store path, a recursive scan finding no store file in any worktree | proposal §4.5 rule 1 [documented]; redkiln's locks already live there |
| A store opened against a **different repository** (root commit + common dir identity) is **refused** with a typed error naming both identities, reading and writing nothing | **AC-179** — fixture store plus a second repository, asserting the error variant and an unchanged file digest | proposal §4.5 rule 5 [documented — Backlog.md #558; external reference, not in the evidence brief] |
| A store at an older schema version is refused with the required migration named; **no implicit in-place migration** | **AC-180** — checked-in fixture store at version N−1, bytes unchanged after the refused open | proposal §4.5 rule 5 [documented] |
| Two processes in two worktrees both land their writes, with no lock error and one store file | **AC-181** — two-process integration test, its GIVEN "two worktrees of two different items", with a long-transaction fixture. (The observable is both rows present; `busy_timeout` = 5000 ms and every write transaction shorter than it are ADR-0105 clause 7's rule; SQLite serialises writers, so the design is **one writer connection per process behind a mutex, never a pool** — §9 owns that) | proposal §4.7 [documented]; ADR-0105 |
| **No git hooks feed the store.** Nothing is written under `hooks/`, and the product behaves identically where the hooks directory is absent or read-only | **AC-182** — source lint plus a run against a hooks-less repository asserting identical output | git-branchless's `reference-transaction` approach cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays (its issue #1334) [documented, external]. ADR-0018's own evidence note records that #1334 "is about hooks, not worktrees", so it licenses **this** rule and not the compile-time export boundary of AC-122 |
| An out-of-band git operation in a plain shell is reflected by `reconcile()` alone — no notification, daemon or hook | **AC-183** — shell out to `git` between two invocations, run with the hooks directory removed, asserting no child process outlives the first invocation | F19 [measured] |

**Rebuild from a clean clone is a CI test, not a recovery procedure.** `redkiln doctor --rebuild` over a clean clone with no store present must produce tier-1 state **byte-identical under the canonical serialiser** to the tier-1 projection of the original store, consuming only the clone — a store file planted at the target path is not read (**AC-184**). The test runs in CI on every commit against the pinned `redkiln-corpus` revision (ADR-0013), includes an adversarial-store variant that must produce identical output, asserts that the corpus pin is a 40-hex SHA and not a branch, **and asserts the ref set beside it**: F12's 74 slices need 118 refs, only 46 reachable from `47faec9f`, so the substrate is a `git bundle create --all` of the predecessor committed into `redkiln-corpus` and the test asserts the bundle's ref list matches the recorded set — F12's figures were computed *across all refs of the frozen clone*, not at one SHA [measured, PIA-002; amended 2026-09-11]. The differential is over the **tier-1 projection** — the tier-1 rows whose anchor is reachable from the clone's refs; orphan rows and rewrite events are tier-2-derived and sit outside it, with a rebased-commit fixture pinning the expected output (ADR-0105 clause 9; PIA-043). **SM-051 has no baseline: nothing in the brief measures a rebuild.** The corpus (F12's 360 trailer commits over 74 slices) is the substrate the first measurement will run against, and nothing more; `reconcile.rs` was built and run over it in the spike runner (`spikes/runner/src/reconcile.rs`), **not validated against an oracle**, and the golden event logs AC-006/AC-188 replay do not yet exist [measured, PIA-002]. SM-051's kill is sharp and is the tier test read backwards: **a non-zero divergence means tier-1 state is not fully in git**, and the offending field moves or is re-tiered. SM-056 runs the same differential on a schedule against a live repository, subject to the AC-188 limit in §5.4.

### 5.8 Resume: one idempotent entry point

| Property | Criterion | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `redkiln run <item>` is the sole entry for a first run and a resumed run; **no `--resume`, `--continue` or equivalent flag exists on any command** | **AC-186** — `clap` surface snapshot test that must be updated deliberately to add a flag, plus a committed name denylist (`resume`, `continue`, `stay`, `--stay`, `--resume`, `--continue`, `--from`, `--pick-up`) over the parser definition | proposal §4.5 (L2.3) [documented]; **ADR-0107** (the rule's home — ADR-0011 decides packs, not the CLI, PIA-082) |
| A committed step is not re-dispatched — its memo key resolves — and the next action equals, under the canonical serialiser, the action an uninterrupted control run took at the same point | **AC-187** — memo hit, zero duplicate dispatch events, equality against a recorded control trace | F38: 3/3 across a real `taskkill /T /F` [measured, **n=3, PARTIAL**]. **PROVISIONAL at long durations:** P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms are unrun, and **all three of its controls are unrun**, so the control trace this criterion compares against is a product-test artefact, not something the spike measured |
| Every output produced before a kill is present afterwards with an unchanged digest, and none is rewritten by the resumed run | **AC-185** — per-artefact digest capture before the kill, equality after | F38: **0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical** [measured, n=3, PARTIAL] |

SM-052's figures are corrected. The **8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579** F38 first reported are the resumed step's **`output_tokens`**, and dividing them by F28's **`cache_creation_input_tokens`** (~18.6k) produced sub-1× output-token ratios from unlike counters (ERRATA E-0087-1); on the like counter the resumed dispatches created **84,960 / 49,468 / 46,961** `cache_creation_input_tokens` — 4.57× / 2.66× / 2.52× of 18,603 — on which METHOD-P §5.4's >1.5× kill would have fired, subject to the cross-fixture caveat that F28's gate and F38's resume are different fixtures [measured, F38 result files; ADR-0106]. **The cost ordering between a mid-graph gate and a stage gate is UNMEASURED**; SM-002, SM-052 and SM-073 re-baseline to *no baseline* until P8-B runs on `cache_creation_input_tokens` of the first post-resume dispatch beside `total_cost_usd` (ADR-0106 clause 2) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-080]. Two disclosures travel with any figure from that run: **all three P8 runs sat inside F10's ~1 h TTL** (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label), and there is **no within-experiment baseline** because the controls are unrun. F28 bounds what a keep-warm could buy at one prefix rewrite (18.6k–29.7k) per gate, and F22 forbids the process anyway. The target "at most one prefix rewrite per resume, invariant in the length of the interruption" is therefore **currently unfalsifiable in this programme's data** and stays open until P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms run (ADR-0106; §10). Gate *placement* is a value decision and belongs to §6; the cache mechanics behind these token figures belong to §7.

### 5.9 The gix restriction — a Phase-1 risk with a decided fallback (ADR-0077)

Git is deliberately not a port (§3), which makes *how* `rk-git` talks to git a real architecture decision with no adapter to swap. The research pass withdrew two capabilities the store design had assumed.

| Direction | Owner | Operations | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Read** | `gix`, at the exact pin `=0.87.1` | `rev-parse` (including resolving the common dir AC-178 requires at startup — **on an S-GIX/a no-go this one read moves to the wrapper's `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, once at startup, cached**), repository and worktree status, **trailer parsing** (`BodyRef::trailers()`, gix-object 0.64.1) | Trailer parsing exists at the pin [documented, docs.rs gix-object 0.64.1, fetched 2026-09-10; ERRATA E-0077-1]. Nothing contradicts the read-side bet except the common-dir interplay S-GIX/a tests, whose no-go has a decided owner (ADR-0098 decision 1) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-009, PIA-042] |
| **Write, retained on gix** | `gix`, at the pin | **shallow clone by SHA** for review checkouts — `PrepareFetch::with_revision` + `with_shallow`, bare unless the review activity declares it needs a working tree (ADR-0105 clause 5) | A clone writes a working tree and is a **write** on the advisory surface (`gix-worktree`, `gix-worktree-state`), retained deliberately for the typed shallow-by-SHA clone the wrapper cannot express as cheaply; `clone` is added to the operations the `cargo audit` job and the Windows CI target cover, and **S-GIX/c** byte-compares its HEAD tree to `git clone --depth 1`'s [documented, gitoxide PR #2824 (2026-08-05) — **not** discussion #2309, which shows a panic; fetched 2026-09-10; ADR-0098 decision 2] |
| **Write** | a **process-exec wrapper** invoking the `git` executable — every op journalled through the `GitOps` seam so replay performs zero writes (ADR-0105 clause 1) | **The write set, enumerated** (ADR-0105 clause 5): `git worktree add` / `git worktree remove` (re-created per run, never reused; a failed remove is a typed error and a `doctor` orphans row) *(dated 2026-09-27, `s8d-ii` build, P6, under ADR-0180, proposed *(dated 2026-09-28, `s8e` close: accepted on D21-91, at `s8d-ii`'s close)*: "re-created per run, never reused" is superseded in part **for the run-owned worktree only**, which is reused on re-entry and never force-removed (D21-45 W2; ADR-0105 clause 6 in part), and the list gains, for the writes only (D21-47's clause-5 rule), `git worktree lock` / `unlock`, the re-entry discard's `reset --hard HEAD` and `clean -fd` on the owned tree, and the `RK_BASE` marker file in the tree's gitdir; `redkiln-migrate`'s worktree keeps "re-created per run, never reused" (AC-522); the review reference tree keeps its forced removal, ADR-0162 decision 10)*; `git add`; `git commit` (incl. `--allow-empty`, `--trailer`, with `-c trailer.ifexists=add` and `-c core.hooksPath=<empty>`); `interpret-trailers --if-exists=add`; `write-tree` on a temporary index (the `tree_hash` derivation, §6.3.2); `patch-id`; fast-forward/merge on the integration ref; clone removal; `rev-parse --git-common-dir` on S-GIX/a's no-go. Anything not listed is a successor. Paths are handed to git in drive-letter form, never `\\?\` (F20/2) | gitoxide's own `crate-status.md`: "the delicate interplay between `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` isn't implemented" — **precisely the mechanism one-store-at-the-common-dir leans on** [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 2, severity **high**]; its line 188 lists worktree create/move/remove/repair unchecked, a second reason for the shell-out. No first-class trailer-**writing** API exists at the pin — a **[documented] absence** from enumerating gix 0.87.1's 1,533 items, gix-object 0.64.1's 142 and gix-actor 0.42.0's 58 on docs.rs, 2026-09-10 — with the executed-test arm of S-GIX/b still owed [documented; §1 row 8; §5 names it "the highest-value single check in the list"; ERRATA E-0077-1; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-042] |

The constraints that come with the hybrid: **all** shelling out to git goes through **one wrapper type inside `rk-git`** — no `std::process::Command` for git exists anywhere else in the workspace; git's stderr is diagnostic payload and is **never parsed for control flow**; the wrapper asserts at startup (not at first use) that `git` is on `PATH` at or above a minimum version, **and that minimum is not yet established, so the assertion is incomplete until the wrapper is built**. Two git implementations in one process can disagree — notably about config resolution and Windows path handling — so anything read by gix and then written by the executable is **re-read after the write, never assumed**. A tier-1 write resolves the run-owned worktree **from the store**, regardless of cwd, and a target that is not run-owned is refused naming its path and branch — **AC-529** (ADR-0105 clause 8, narrowing ADR-0018's "current worktree" to the worktree the store owns for the run; PIA-029, PIA-040); AC-181's GIVEN reads "of two different items". Restricting gix to reads plus the one retained clone moves it away from *worktree* checkout — `gix-worktree-state` checkout permissions, `gix-worktree` path traversal and Windows reserved-device-name handling, where its advisory history clusters and which is exactly the disposable-worktree surface a merge queue exercises hardest (the hybrid narrows away from worktree checkout, not from clone — ADR-0098); all of those are **fixed at or before 0.87.1, so the pin is CLEAN** (gix 0.87.1 pulls gix-features ^0.49.1, gix-ref ^0.67.1, gix-worktree ^0.56.0) [documented, GitHub Advisory Database / RustSec, fetched 2026-09-08 and 2026-09-10; ERRATA E-0084-1]. §9 owns the pin, the `cargo audit` job and the bus-factor notes.

**What this section does not say.** It does not say gix can write a trailer, and it does not say gix cannot: ADR-0077 forbids treating documented parsing support as evidence of writing support, and equally forbids narrowing the record on a docs read alone — the read half of S-GIX/b is discharged on a docs read (parsing exists), the write half stays registered for its executed test (ADR-0098 decision 3). The unknowns are discharged as named spikes with named consumers (ADR-0012):

| Spike | Question | Consumer | Gates Phase 1? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **S-GIX/a** | Against the pinned `gix = "=0.87.1"`, **does gix discovery from a linked worktree return the main repository's common dir at the pin** — a READ-path check (the store is *read* from the common dir at every startup; the write path is the wrapper's regardless)? On a go, gix resolves the common dir; on a no-go, the exec wrapper's `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, once at startup, cached [reworded 2026-09-11, PIA-009; ADR-0098 decision 1] | the read-side owner of `rev-parse`; `rk-git` startup | **Yes for the read-side owner of `rev-parse`; the shell fallback is decided** — a no-go is one wrapper call site, not "costs nothing" |
| **S-GIX/b** | Does the pinned gix expose a first-class trailer-**writing** API, and does a five-line unit test round-trip a trailer byte-identically to `git interpret-trailers --in-place` under the same `-c`-pinned config over the fixture matrix? **Read half discharged** (`BodyRef::trailers()` exists, E-0077-1); the write half stands | `rk-git` trailer write path (**tier-1 state**) | **No** — the wrapper's route is decided |
| **S-GIX/c** | Does `PrepareFetch::with_revision` + `with_shallow` at the pin produce a checkout whose HEAD tree equals `git clone --depth 1` followed by `git checkout <sha>`'s, byte-compared? | the review-clone path (§6.4.3; AC-339) | **No** — a no-go falls back to the wrapper's `git clone --depth 1` (ADR-0098 decision 3) |

A **go** on an arm may narrow ADR-0077 and nothing else may; the narrowing is written as a successor record (the runbook's ADR-0091 is Step 6.1's record of the S-GIX *result* and the minimum `git` version), never as an edit. All three rows appear in §10's register.

### 5.10 Decisions

| Decision | Alternatives considered and lost | Why the alternative lost | ADR | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Layout **C** — typed frontmatter + rendered read-only structured sections + opaque prose; machine-only artifacts as JSON sidecars | A: sidecar `spec.json` canonical + generated `.md`; B: embedded canonical payload in a trailing fenced block with a digest | A gives two files per artifact in every git review; B's re-import path "is a place structure can rot back in". **Neither was benchmarked against C on any metric** — this is a design choice with a census behind its *refinements*, not behind the layout itself | ADR-0006, ADR-0075 | proposal §4.4 [documented]; S-MIG for the refinements [measured] |
| The AC type is permissive; the **policy** decides what a missing verification blocks | A strict `{gwt, verification}` grammar, failing migration on anything else | It fails **19/153 = 12%** of shipped, reviewed specs at the type boundary, converting a policy question into a migration error with no way to answer it differently per stage or rigor | ADR-0075 cl. 1 | S-MIG [measured, one corpus] |
| Gate results are emitted typed by the toolchain and rendered | Parse the gate prose with a grammar, or re-import it through a narrow LLM activity | At **17%** reclassify on the review gate table there is no grammar to find, and LLM re-import is the exact structure-from-markdown path ADR-0006 forbids | ADR-0075 cl. 2 | S-MIG [measured] |
| One schema per companion kind | A single union schema across `_integration`, `_grounding`, `_decomposition`, `_ledger` | They are three shapes; a union collapses to a mostly-optional record that validates almost nothing, and drags `oneOf` nesting into the type set | ADR-0075 cl. 3 | S-MIG: companion frontmatter **42%** over five of the eleven companion kinds (E-0075-2) [measured]; the Phase-1 kind list is settled at AC-143's five plus `ledger` (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #3) |
| `plan.md` is removed | Keep it and treat 110/110 unfilled as a discipline problem | An artifact never filled once across a whole corpus is unwanted, not under-used, and keeping it costs a schema, a template, a locator and a stage output | ADR-0075 cl. 4 | S-MIG **110/110** [measured] |
| Three below-threshold section kinds are carried as **opaque prose** | Type them anyway; or drop them from the migration | Typing a kind that lifts at 17% manufactures structure that is not there; dropping it loses corpus content silently | ADR-0075 cl. 6 | 75% / 42% / 17% against an 80% threshold [measured] |
| A **golden-schema snapshot test** enforces the closed-output rules | Trust `schemars` to produce closed, flat schemas; or rely on author discipline | schemars 1.2.2 emits `oneOf` for **data-carrying enum variants by default**; the rule binds the Rust types, not the library. And an author-discipline rule is violated by the first payload-carrying enum, silently — F20's class fails open at exit 0 | ADR-0084 | [documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars]; F20 [measured] |
| Tier 1 to **git first**, tier 2 to the event store, split by the delete-the-database test; three trailer axes | A single durable store; or trailers as a derived projection of the database; a single axis with the verdict inferred | The test is the whole point: state that changes the next action may not live only where a `rm` removes it. F12 additionally shows a single `Story:` axis is insufficient | **ADR-0109** (ADR-0018 `related`); ADR-0105 for the plumbing | F12 **20.3%** ever-rejected over 360 commits / 74 slices [measured] |
| Rows keyed by SHA/tree hash; orphans surfaced; `rewrite` events on a recorded mechanism only | Key rows by path; or trust the working directory and re-key silently | A path key contradicts proposal §4.5 rule 2 flatly (and the source records no cost for it, which is stated rather than invented); silent re-keying destroys the audit trail a rewrite is supposed to leave | ADR-0018 | proposal §4.5 rule 2 [documented] |
| One store per repository at the common dir, refusing a foreign repo | A store per worktree | Nothing at the filesystem level prevents a second worktree writing to a shared store, so ownership is checked rather than assumed; a per-worktree store forfeits the shared lock location redkiln already uses | ADR-0018 | proposal §4.5 rules 1 and 5 [documented]; Backlog.md #558 [documented, external] |
| **No git hooks** | A `reference-transaction` hook feeding the store | It cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays (git-branchless #1334) | ADR-0018 | [documented, external] |
| `doctor --rebuild` from a clean clone, run as a **CI test** | Treat rebuild as an operator recovery tool exercised on demand | A recovery path never exercised is a recovery path that does not work; run as CI it is also the standing test of the tier split | ADR-0018, ADR-0013 | SM-051 (no baseline; first measurement) |
| One idempotent resume entry, no `--resume` flag | A `--resume` flag distinguishing the two cases; a `resume` verb aliasing `run` | A flag makes the caller decide something the kernel can derive, and creates a second code path that only runs after a failure | **ADR-0107** | F19 174/174, F22 [measured]; F38 [measured, n=3, PARTIAL] |
| `gix` reads (plus the one retained clone write), the `git` executable writes through a journalled wrapper | gix for everything; `git2-rs`/libgit2 for everything; shell out for everything and drop gix; move the review clone to the wrapper | gix-for-everything means discovering both documented gaps at integration time on tier-1 state; libgit2 trades a documented Rust gap for a C toolchain and a second FFI surface; shelling out for everything loses typed rev-parse/status, shallow clone by SHA and structured trailer parsing, substituting porcelain parsing, which is worse; moving the clone gives up the one typed write with no porcelain to parse (retained as S-GIX/c's fallback) | ADR-0077, ADR-0098, ADR-0105 | [documented, 1P gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08; docs.rs and PR #2824, fetched 2026-09-10] |
