| Long-duration resume equality, duration-invariance and the resume's cost | **PROVISIONAL**; cost **UNMEASURED** | AC-187 is demonstrated at short durations and n=3 only, with all three controls unrun; SM-052's duration-invariance target is labelled unfalsifiable in current data; the cost ordering against a stage gate is unmeasured (ADR-0106) | **P8-B** — the 65-minute and 24-hour arms and the three controls, on `cache_creation_input_tokens` (ADR-0106) | No |
| The companion **kind list** — proposal §4.4, its machine-only list and the S-MIG census named different sets | **SETTLED 2026-09-11** (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #3) | AC-143's registry-completeness fixture enumerates exactly six — `_integration`, `_grounding`, `_decomposition`, `_review`, `_ledger` plus the register's fifth — and the other eight census kinds lift as opaque prose (ADR-0075 cl. 6) | a runbook decision recorded against ADR-0075 cl. 3 in s5 §5.3 | No |
| `doctor`'s "zero reads over N runs" threshold | Unset by measurement | SM-038 carries a rolling 20-item window; removal from a required set is **always a human pack edit** (AC-148), because the selection engine's input is BLOCKED | first measurement of `artifact.read` rates; the removal rule waits on ADR-0079 — whose arm-D panel is now **scored** (F39, discharging AC-071's fourth clause only) and whose block stands on AC-071's identity ≥ 0.80 clause | No (the pruning half is blocked; see §4) |
| Structured-drift rate — humans editing rendered sections | No baseline anywhere | SM-037's first measurement establishes it; its kill fires when a per-section rate has not fallen after 20 items | SM-037 | No |
| `reconcile()`'s own cost | Unmeasured | Reported per entry from the first build. **No optimisation may be introduced on the strength of an unmeasured cost** — AC-173 forbids a skip path regardless | SM-049 instrumentation | No |
| AC-121's cross-OS determinism half; AC-158's property ordering | Unverifiable as specified | Both are marked unverified rather than assumed: AC-121 needs two CI OS targets, AC-158 needs an order-preserving schema export | a runbook decision, recorded (§9 owns the CI shape) | No |
| The optional-parameter and union-typed-parameter budgets (24 / 16) | **The vendor's documented Schema complexity limits, per request** (E-0084-2; ADR-0095) | Enforced by the golden snapshot per exported schema under the stated precondition (one output schema per dispatch, no strict user-defined tools) and an argv lint; the vendor's failure shape observed by **S-SCHEMA-CAP** | S-SCHEMA-CAP (§10.5) → the `rk-types` snapshot test, AC-132/AC-157, AC-129 | No |

---

## 6. The three gates — human, review, verification

### 6.1 The three gates at a glance

| Gate | What it decides | Its record | What it may never do | Evidence | Ids |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Human** | Whether the loop proceeds past a point a human owns | `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` written as a `Gate:` trailer and a `gate_opened` event; the opening invocation exits 0 | Wait in a process; accept an answer against a tree that moved; hang past its deadline | F18, F19, F22, F28, F38 [measured] | AC-240..AC-279, SM-073..SM-084; ADR-0009, ADR-0017, ADR-0022, ADR-0038, ADR-0062, ADR-0063, ADR-0087 |
| **Review** | Which *findings* exist, as executable claims — never whether the item passes | A typed `Findings` payload with no decision field; the class computed in Rust; a block only after re-execution flips | Emit a verdict; clear an acceptance criterion; block on a class the deterministic gate has not re-executed | F17, F20, F24, F27, F29, F31 [measured] | AC-280..AC-319, SM-085..SM-096; ADR-0074 (fully superseding ADR-0004; its repro shape and `mutation-survived` definition superseded in part by ADR-0096), ADR-0015, ADR-0020, ADR-0023, ADR-0025, ADR-0026, ADR-0036, ADR-0081, ADR-0082, ADR-0101 (superseding ADR-0088 in full), ADR-0104 |
| **Verification** | Whether each AC is *pinned* by an assertion that flips under mutation | A `VerifyReport` carrying `basis`, `basis_reason`, `argv`, counts, `suite_sha`; an AC evidence ledger with an assertion locator per AC | Report `affected` for a run that did not narrow; treat a green suite as evidence at assertion granularity; accept a CI status in place of its own result | F11, F25, F31, brief §D [measured] | AC-320..AC-359, SM-097..SM-108; ADR-0019, ADR-0029, ADR-0073, ADR-0004→0074, ADR-0008, ADR-0014, ADR-0017, ADR-0030, ADR-0061 |

All three are instances of one mechanism, not three: a gate is a durable mark plus a later read (§3's kernel rule; brief §C.6, F22 [measured]). A mid-graph escalation is the same record shape and the same reconcile path as a stage gate — one gate type exists, not two (AC-272; ADR-0022, ADR-0038).

### 6.2 Neither gate is sufficient alone

This is the single most load-bearing statement in the section, and two findings force it. They are given equal weight because they fail in opposite directions.

| Direction of failure | The measurement | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The reviewer misses what the tests would catch — its verdict is a weighted coin** | A **reproduced regression was approved on 3 of 10 draws**; across 60 draws (0 void, 16.2 h) not one defect class is stable-reject, class means `reproduced` **0.70** > `mutation-survived` **0.37** > `narrowed-pin` **0.10** > negative control **0.00** — yet **19/19 rejections were on target** [measured, F29] | The reviewer's *claims* are sound; its *threshold* is not. So the model may **add** findings and its verdict channel is removed entirely (AC-280, AC-283) |
| **The tests miss what the reviewer would catch — a fully green suite is not evidence** | **Four adjudicated defects escaped the deterministic gate — two through fully green suites (issue-70 AC-001 1,890/1,890; issue-83 AC-002 1,907/1,907) and two (issue-131) with zero new failures against an already-red 54-failure baseline** [measured, F31; ERRATA E-0082-2, PIA-095] | So a green suite may never clear a finding the deterministic gate re-executed and saw flip (AC-316), and a review may never mark an AC verified (AC-315, AC-342) |

**The rule, stated once:** the review gate is high-precision and low-recall. It may **ADD** findings and may never **CLEAR** an AC. Blocking is computed in code from re-executed evidence, never from the model's `pass` (brief §C.7; ADR-0074 clauses 2–3, ADR-0019). Enforced by AC-315 (a clean review changes no AC's state), AC-316 (a green suite does not dismiss a confirmed block) and AC-342 (a review approval is not an input to any AC status), each with a type-level separation as its instrument rather than a test of model behaviour.

Externally corroborated, from the research pass: **SWE-Gate** derives review constraints from real PR review comments across 303 repair instances in 75 Python repos and finds **221 of 644 (34%)** functionally-*passing* agent repairs fail those constraints [preprint, arXiv 2609.04167, fetched 2026-09-08]. It measures a different quantity from anything in this corpus — benchmark constraint-failure, not a live override rate — and is cited as a second, fresher anchor for "green ≠ pinned", not as a rate this design inherits.

### 6.3 The human gate — a mark, not a process

#### 6.3.1 The mechanism

| Property | Rule | Evidence | Criterion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Opening a gate is a write and an exit | The opening invocation terminates **exit 0** and leaves no child, background, detached or daemon process alive | ~12 background jobs died against **one 12,000 s `Start-Process` survivor**; `issue-81` was killed 3× as a background job and completed in **116 s** in the foreground — cause never established [measured, operational, F22] | AC-240 (exit), **AC-278** (no surviving descendant — a clean exit that leaks a detached child is a distinct failure) |
| No waiting construct on the gate path | Build fails on `thread::sleep`, a blocking channel receive, or a timer/scheduler spawn in the gate modules | F22 [measured]; F28 [measured] — a gate costs a flat ~18.6k tokens however long it lasts, so a waiting process would reintroduce the cost the mark removed | AC-242. **Scope limit:** this is a syntactic lint over named modules; it cannot prove no path anywhere blocks. The behavioural half is AC-240/AC-278 |
| Zero dispatches while a gate is open | No `dispatch` event may fall between `gate_opened` and the terminal event | The tempting regression is named: F10 puts the TTL at **~1 h** (full hit at 45.1 min, share **0.690** at 61.6 min — the boundary is *bracketed at (45.1, 61.6), never located*, n=1 per gap) and F18 puts **45–48% of gates over 1 h**, so "keep the cache warm" suggests itself. F28 bounds what a keep-warm could buy at one prefix rewrite (18.6k–29.7k) per gate — **18,603 tokens at 65.2 min and 18,603 at 49.7 h**, one measured point per duration, and F28 ran no keep-alive arm — and F22 forbids the process anyway [measured; PIA-080] | AC-243; SM-077 (saturates at zero, kept as an invariant alarm) |
| The two writes are atomic together | A kill during open leaves **both** the `Gate:` trailer and the `gate_opened` event, or neither | F38 is *motivating, not confirming*: P8 killed at three arbitrary instants and never enumerated write boundaries [measured, n=3] | AC-241 — a fault-point enumeration, not a sleep race |

Gate cost is settled and it is not what the proposal assumed: **duration is free, frequency is marginally costly, and placement is therefore a value decision, not a cost decision** (brief §C.2; ADR-0017, ADR-0087). AC-273 makes the machine check only that a placement decision record exists with an owner and a date, and forbids any code path that computes placement from a token or cost figure — because the evidence removed the quantity a machine could optimise.

#### 6.3.2 The record — `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`

The triple is unconstructible with a field missing, and a record read back from the store without all three fails deserialisation (AC-244). The token is single-use (AC-246) and scoped to one gate instance, so A's token against gate B is refused and B stays open (AC-247). An answer naming only an item is refused — item-keyed answering is ADR-0017's explicitly rejected alternative, because item identity alone cannot detect drift (AC-248).

**Why the tree hash, and why it is stronger than the prior art.** The research pass surveyed six durable-execution and CI platforms [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]:

| Platform | Token semantics | Deadline | Bound to repository state? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AWS Step Functions | Task token, single-use; a fresh random token on retry after a timeout | **No server cap short of the one-year execution quota**; `HeartbeatSeconds` / `TimeoutSeconds` are opt-in bounds set by the author — the "max activity TTL 6 h" this row first carried is in no AWS source [documented, 1P AWS Step Functions Developer Guide quotas and `SendTaskHeartbeat`, fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-085] | No |
| Trigger.dev | Waitpoint token, single-consumption | Default 10 m, configurable; 1 h idempotency-key TTL | No |
| Inngest | `step.waitForEvent()` | Explicit timeout required | No |
| DBOS | `GetEvent`/`SetEvent` keyed by `(workflowID, key)` — no token object | Deadline-bearing (default 60 s); on timeout `get_event`/`recv` return **`None`, a sentinel, not an error** — only `read_stream` raises [documented, 1P docs.dbos.dev workflow-communication, fetched 2026-09-10; PIA-085] | No |
| Restate | Awakeable, explicitly one-shot | **None built in** — the docs tell you to pair it with a separate durable timer | No |
| GitHub Environments | Approval requiring a real identity and a PAT, not the ambient `GITHUB_TOKEN` | Yes | **Commit SHA/ref only** |
| **Atlassian, "Agents in Jira"** (GA May 2026) | A **native workflow Approval step** holding a transition until a named approver signs off — on decades-old generic workflow machinery | Workflow-configured | No |
| Azure Durable Functions (external events) | **No token**: an event is addressed by orchestration instance id + event name; delivery is **at-least-once**, so duplicates are possible and de-duplication is the application's job [documented, 1P learn.microsoft.com durable-task external-events, ms.date 2026-05-04, fetched 2026-09-10; the 2026-09-08 "could not be read" caveat is lifted — amended 2026-09-11, PIA-085] | **None built in** — waits indefinitely by default; expiry only by racing a durable timer | No |
| **Claude Code hook `defer`** (`PreToolUse` → `tool_deferred`; shipped 2.1.89, 2026-03-31) | **No token object** — the pending tool call lives in the session transcript on disk, addressed by session id | **No built-in deadline**; the session persists on disk and is resumed by `claude -p --resume` [documented, 1P code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#defer-a-tool-call-for-later, fetched 2026-09-10; added 2026-09-11, PIA-064] | No — and whether a resumed deferred call preserves this design's tree-hash binding is **S-DEFER**'s question (§10.5); ADR-0017 and ADR-0106 stand until it reports |

Two consequences follow, and the second is the design's actual differentiator.

1. **Single-use plus a deadline is common, not universal.** Of the platforms read, Step Functions, Trigger.dev, Inngest, Restate and GitHub mint a single-use signal, and those five plus DBOS guarantee a terminal event (an exception, a status, a `null`/`None` sentinel); **Azure Durable Functions and Claude Code's hook `defer` mint no token and carry no built-in deadline** — Azure waits indefinitely unless a durable timer is raced, `defer` persists the pending call on disk until a resume [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-064, PIA-085]. Only GitHub pins to repository state at all, and **none binds a tree hash** [documented]. The earlier sentence "every platform this pass could actually read mints a single-use signal and guarantees a terminal event" is narrowed to the five named.

1a. **The differentiation claim, narrowed.** This design's novelty claim is **not** "nobody ships stage-gated, mid-run, resumable human approval on a durable work item" — Atlassian's *Agents in Jira* ships a native workflow Approval step that holds a transition until a named approver signs off, on generic workflow machinery decades old [documented, research pass §1 contradiction #10, §4 rec 15]. Nor does "in an AI-coding-specific backlog or spec tool" survive: spec-kit ships a gate step, Archon an approval node, and Bernstein approval gates (whether Bernstein's are human or automated is not settled by its README and stays on the next-pass check list, not in this prose) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-090]. The claim that survives is narrower still and is about the *binding*: **no surveyed tool attaches a persisted human gate to a durable work item that carries a kind and a per-kind stage machine, and none binds the answer to a tree hash** — Jira, spec-kit, Archon and Bernstein included.
2. **No surveyed system invalidates a stale-but-unexpired token automatically.** Stale-answer handling everywhere else is *workflow-side re-validation*: the token reports only that a value arrived, and consuming code is expected to re-check whatever external state it cared about. Only expiry and single-use consumption are platform-enforced [documented, 1P Restate external-events, DBOS workflow-communication, fetched 2026-09-08]. **This is precisely why the tree-hash binding is stronger, and why an explicit re-validation at the read site is required rather than optional** — redkiln moves the check from "the consumer remembers to look" to "the record cannot be applied against a tree it does not name". A commit-SHA pin (GitHub's) is weaker in one direction and noisier in the other: a history rewrite to an identical tree is not drift, and a touched-then-reverted file is not drift. AC-253 forbids deciding drift from mtimes, wall-clock elapsed time, commit counts or branch-tip identity, and requires the drift predicate to read no clock.

The recorded hash is the hash of **the tree the human actually saw**, not HEAD's commit and not the index at a later instant (AC-245) — otherwise the void rule checks a claim nobody made. **The derivation is one algorithm, written once, used at gate open, at answer and for AC-254's diff** (ADR-0105 clause 4; added 2026-09-11, PIA-050): when a checkpoint commit is due, the recorded tree is that commit's tree; otherwise it is `git write-tree` on a **temporary index** built from the current index plus tracked modifications, through the wrapper — **untracked files are excluded** — so an empty commit over a dirty worktree binds to a defined tree, and AC-254's attachment is "commits since the trailer commit, and paths differing between the two trees". `write-tree` is on the wrapper's write set (§5.9).

#### 6.3.3 Void, not reconciled

An answer arriving against a tree that is no longer `T` resolves **void**: a `gate_answer_void` event is written and no approve or changes-requested decision reaches the item (AC-249). There is no merge path, no re-application path and no deferred reconciliation — ADR-0017's rejected option is literally "reconcile stale answers". AC-250 splits the claim honestly: a property test over generated `(commit, answer, resume, expire)` interleavings checks that no generated sequence advances the item, and an **architecture test** over the code carries the universal half, because a fuzz test cannot establish "there exists no sequence of inputs".

| Quantity | Figure | Reading |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drift, all gates | **12/174 = 6.9%** (Wilson 95% **4.0–11.7%**) [measured, F19; one operator, one machine] | The void path is reachable in normal operation at roughly 1 gate in 14 — neither a corner case nor a hot path; the same corpus is characterised as "the world moves rarely and predictably" |
| Drift, gates ≤ 1 h vs > 1 h | **3/95 = 3.2%** (1.1–8.9%) vs **9/79 = 11.4%** (6.1–20.3%) [measured, F19] | The 3.6× duration effect is **directional only — the intervals overlap** (RR 3.6, CI [1.0, 12.9], Fisher p = 0.039; marginal at n=174, one operator). It is never to be stated as an established ratio (SM-075) |
| Median in-gap commits | **0** [measured, F19] | Licenses no claim about the drifted subset's payload size: that statistic is over *all* gates, 93% of which did not drift. AC-254 requires the drift attachment to be exact and byte-identical across two renders **on principle**, not because it is known to be small |

A void re-opens the gate with a record differing in **all three** fields — fresh token, current tree hash, fresh deadline (AC-252) — carrying a non-empty drift attachment naming the commits and changed paths (AC-277, split out because a re-open with three fresh fields and no attachment satisfies AC-252 completely while losing the only thing that tells the human why they are being asked twice). Voids are excluded from approve/changes-requested rates and reported in their own denominator: **a void is never a miss** (AC-251). That is a reporting discipline transferred by analogy from every spike that reported a rate (F29 60 draws 0 void, F36 400 dispatches 0 void, F37 297 records 0 void [measured]) — a spike void is a discarded draw, a different quantity from a gate answer voided by drift, and only the discipline transfers.

The price of the rule is named rather than hidden: a human's answering work is discarded and must be redone when the tree moved before it landed. **SM-081 measures that cost and has no baseline** — it is the only number that would justify revisiting the all-or-nothing binding in favour of a path-scoped drift test.

#### 6.3.4 Deadline and a terminal expiry path

Every gate past its deadline is, after `reconcile()`, in exactly one of `fail`, `escalate` or `auto-decide`; the set of gates simultaneously past-deadline and open is empty, and a non-empty result from that `doctor` rule is an error (AC-256, SM-076). Expiry is observed **at the next read** — `redkiln run <item>` or `redkiln answer` — with no timer, cron entry, daemon or scheduled job required to have been alive at the deadline instant (AC-257), which is F22's lesson applied: a process that must be alive at a future moment is not a mechanism this system has. **Escalation chains terminate within one hop**: an escalation gate's class is fixed (`escalation`), and the policy-table lint (AC-260) refuses `escalate` as that class's expiry action, so every chain ends in `fail` or an auto-decide — a must-fail fixture row carries the refused table (ADR-0105 clause 10; AC-259; added 2026-09-11, PIA-050).

Two honesty constraints attach.

- **Expiry in the corpus was unobservable, not observed-absent.** The F18/F19 gates carried no deadline mechanism, so no gate *could* expire; the longest was answered at **226 h (9.4 days)** [measured, F18, n=191, one operator]. Every expiry criterion here rests on design and prior art, and the corpus supplies **no expiry base rate in either direction** [inferred] (AC-276, SM-079).
- **Which terminal action applies to which gate class is a human decision, per class** (AC-276). The lint refuses a policy-table row with no expiry action declared, because a silent default in code would make the riskiest of the three — `auto-decide` on an unreviewed tree — reachable by omission (AC-260). The research pass shows the *style* is a free choice with a uniform guarantee: Step Functions raises `States.Timeout` and invalidates the token (an exception), Trigger.dev resolves to an incomplete status the run branches on, Inngest resolves `waitForEvent()` to `null` and DBOS's `get_event`/`recv` return `None` (sentinels) — **every platform that carries a deadline guarantees a terminal event; none leaves a workflow blocked with no observable outcome**, while Azure external events and Claude Code's `defer` carry no deadline and wait until a raced timer or a resume [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08 and 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-085]. Redkiln picks the exception style, deliberately, and records it against ADR-0017. SM-079's target for `auto-decide` borrows Cloudflare's break-glass **288 of 48,095 MRs = 0.6%** [documented] as an *order of magnitude only* — a different mechanism in a different organisation, never a threshold.

#### 6.3.5 Resume re-derives from the world first

Every entry into `redkiln run <item>` re-derives the item's state from the world — tree, trailers, event log — before deciding the next action, with **no cached-state fast path that skips the derivation** (AC-261; brief §C.3). There is one idempotent entry point and `--resume` is not an accepted flag on any command (AC-264), because a separate resume verb is a second code path that would not get the re-derivation. Every channel writes through the same reconcile path, and the observable form of that is channel-independence: the same answer through the CLI adapter and through a stub adapter must produce byte-identical event sequences (AC-262). In Phase 1 the CLI is the only enabled channel and a stub tracker-comment or web-write answer is refused with a named outcome, writing no event (AC-263; ADR-0009). No agent card exposes the answer, mint or resolve path — checked by a card validator over the whole catalog and a build-configuration check, not by observing that an agent asked to answer a gate declined (AC-269; the model-behaviour observation is demoted to an optional smoke test, because one compliant sample proves nothing about reachability and F27 puts verdict flakiness at 2/4 items [measured]).

The verification-gate side of resume asserts the mirror negative: resume **re-derives** the changed-file set from the world, while the *audit* replay of a completed run reproduces the recorded path list **without reading the working tree** (AC-353 against AC-321 — each states the other's negative, so a builder cannot satisfy both with one implementation).

**The standing limit, restated because it is easy to lose:** F19 proved the state *carrier* lossless at **174/174** (Wilson 95% lower bound 97.8%) and F38 proved it at mid-graph step granularity **3/3** across a real `taskkill /T /F` with 0 changed, 0 lost and identical digests — but **the FSM was never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test (AC-258 under an injected clock and an injected filesystem root; AC-270 reconstructing gate state from the log alone). AC-258 asserts injected-clock and injected-root replay only; genuine cross-machine reproduction is not claimed, because nothing tests it.

#### 6.3.6 Mid-graph gates, and what P8 did not settle

**The decision (ADR-0087).** Escalation to a human gate **suspends the activity graph and resumes into the same step** with the answer bound as that step's gate input; it is not activity-terminal. Resume must cross a real process boundary — a reload that reads state back in-process is not a valid implementation and is not evidence for one, because the risk being managed is that a *fresh* process reconstructs something different.

**The measurement — and what it is not.** Mid-graph resume was lossless: 0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical across a real `taskkill /T /F` [measured, F38, n=3 of a pre-registered 27+3, $3.01 of a $40 cap]. **Its cost against a stage gate is UNMEASURED** (ADR-0106, superseding ADR-0087's cost clause; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-080): the sub-1× output-token ratios first reported here divided the resumed step's **`output_tokens`** (8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579; `main.rs:3288`) by F28's **`cache_creation_input_tokens`** (18,600 hard-coded at `main.rs:3349`) — unlike counters (ERRATA E-0087-1); on the like counter the resumed dispatches created **84,960 / 49,468 / 46,961** cache tokens, 4.57× / 2.66× / 2.52× of 18,603, on which METHOD-P §5.4's >1.5× kill would have fired on all three, subject to the cross-fixture caveat that F28's gate and F38's resume are different fixtures. With no within-experiment baseline (all three controls unrun) and all three draws inside F10's ~1 h TTL, the pre-registration's assumption — that a mid-graph gate would price like a stage gate *plus* the graph's replay — is **untested, not falsified**, and **nothing here establishes that mid-graph placement is safe or cheap**. The decision below is taken because it is safe under *either* answer, and placement is not derived from a cost model in either direction.

**What P8 did not settle, at equal prominence.** Per ADR-0087's own decision text, three questions are open in ways that matter, and no sentence elsewhere in this document may treat them as closed.

| Open | What is actually true | Consequence for this design |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Delay is entirely unestablished** | All three runs sat **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL — measured `gap_minutes` **0.9, 28.4, 29.1**, notwithstanding the `0-delay` arm label, because two cycles were driven manually after the orchestrator stalled. **The arm label is not the measurement.** The 65-minute and 24-hour arms — the ones that straddle the TTL and test the cache-cold resume path — and **all three no-kill controls are unrun** [measured/PARTIAL, F38] | There is **no within-experiment baseline**; the only comparison is to F28, and on the like counter it is 4.57× / 2.66× / 2.52×, cross-fixture. "Mid-graph gates are cheaper than stage gates" may not be written at all (ADR-0106). AC-266's provisional note states the class and the corrected numbers and enforces the **1.5×** bar P8 registered on `cache_creation_input_tokens` of the first post-resume dispatch beside `total_cost_usd` — the unit P8-B measures — not the ≤1× aspiration; SM-073 has **no baseline** until P8-B runs on that field |
| **The second-arrival question is not answerable and must not be reported as "did not fire"** | P8 §5.4(3) is **circular by construction**: the gate's own predicate was false on every second arrival, arrivals happened only because `--force-gate` was in effect, and the mark was minted by `repro-probe` — the driver that implements the semantics under test. Measuring A8 against an implementation that assumes A8 settles nothing | The re-reach semantics are a declared PROVISIONAL choice, not an evidenced one: **a loop-back that re-reaches a gate mints a fresh `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`** (ADR-0087 clause 4, until P8-B answers) — a new criterion, **AC-524**, outside the exhausted AC-240..279 range (ADR-0105 clause 10). **AC-271 and SM-078 are duplicate-answer instruments**: AC-271 pins that a second answer against one mark is refused as spent, by a concurrency test, so the semantics are at least *stated*; SM-078 counts duplicate answers, with no target and no kill. The discovery instrument for re-reach itself is a `gate_opened`-per-`(item, gate step)` counter, and no spike can supply the arrival-rate histogram [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-050] |
| **The §5.2 fixture design failed outright, and that is P8's other real finding** | "Remove the repro steps so the gate fires" does not work against an agent holding `Read` and `Bash` in a real repository: across three redactions of increasing severity and two selection rules the gate **never fired** (reproduced on attempt 1 at 754 s, 650 s, 480 s). Sharpest form: `issue-74-plan-briefs-dead-plan-anchor` was localised **0 times in 6** Opus attempts in P7 and **reproduced on the first haiku attempt** in P8 — **reproducing a symptom and localising its cause are different tasks of different difficulty**, and §5.2 assumed the harder gates the easier | Every gate arrival in P8 was forced, so **the arrival rate — how often a triage gate opens in practice — is entirely unmeasured**. Any future gate-frequency argument (SM-082, also a discovery instrument) starts from zero |

P8's shortfall is one of the blocked rows of §0.1 and it gates the **"design final" label**, not the build: Phase 1 starts on the decided rows, and no document, section or commit message may describe this design as final. The unrun arms and controls are registered in §10 as P8-B with ADR-0106 as their consumer.

### 6.4 The review gate — reviewer proposes, machine confirms

ADR-0074 **fully supersedes ADR-0004**; ADR-0004 is citable as history only (it chose *findings, not verdicts* "pending S-VAR" and named no mechanism). Both gating spikes have since run and each answered a different half.

#### 6.4.1 The eight clauses, and where each is enforced

| # | Clause (ADR-0074) | Evidence | Criterion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The card returns `Findings` with **no `pass` field**; each item is an executable claim `{ ac, claim, evidence (advisory label), rationale, files[], repro }` with **`repro` typed** (ADR-0096, superseding this clause's shape): `{ kind: run \| mutate, command (a shell string beginning with an executable token), mutation: Option<{ patch, suite }> (required when kind = mutate), expected: { exit } \| { assertion: Locator }, observed, expected_when_met }` — a finding whose `command` is prose or whose `mutate` lacks `mutation` is rejected at ingest as malformed, never routed to `Unverifiable` [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-007 via ADR-0096] | Schema conformance **52/52 = 100%**; `repro.command` fill **147/147** under the typed contract — **against the pre-0096 shape**; 24 of 29 `mutation-survived` commands and 111 of 176 blocking-grade commands were prose under it [measured, F31; refuter recount, PIA-007] | AC-280 (an enumerated decision-field denylist — `pass`, `verdict`, `approved`, `ok`, `status`, `decision` — refused at configuration time, before any dispatch; a name check, not a semantic one, so a verdict under an unlisted name still gets through and that is stated); AC-281 (an incomplete `repro` is rejected at ingest and never reaches the classifier) |
| 2 | **The evidence class is computed in Rust from the repro record**, never taken from the model; the model's label is stored beside it for drift monitoring only; `mutation-survived` requires a **discriminating** mutant — a reverted-fix patch, or an assertion that distinguishes mutant from original and is observed to flip — so an equivalent mutant collapses to `narrowed-pin` or `advisory` and cannot block (ADR-0096 decision 2) | Detection is stable and the verdict is not: detection **0/7 items flaky over 21 draws**, verdict **2/4 items flaky** [measured, F27, n=2–3]. **F31's block counts, sink seeds and typed-arm scoring were measured under the model-label policy** — `policy_verdict` called `policy.blocks(evidence)` on the model-emitted label (`e3.rs:254-256`) — so the Rust-computed class is **unmeasured** until S-CLASS-REPLAY runs (ADR-0096 decision 3; ERRATA E-0074-2; PIA-097) | AC-284 (a total decision table over a closed `EvidenceClass` enum with no `_ =>` fallthrough to a blocking class; every unrecognised input lands on `advisory`; **PROVISIONAL until S-CLASS-REPLAY reports**); AC-285 (`review_class_drift` event carries both labels; an architecture lint forbids the model label having a reader inside policy); AC-286 (1,000 in-process computations plus a fresh process, identical; a module-syntax lint over `std::fs`, `std::time`, `std::env`) |
| 3 | **A finding blocks only after the deterministic gate re-executes it and it FLIPS** — fails on the candidate, passes on the reference, or the mutant survives on re-application. Re-execution is a precondition of blocking, not a confirmation step that may be skipped under load | F31 adjudication **14/14 confirmed** (the human procedure, at zero model cost — not mechanical re-execution); F11: 15 mutants, **9 of 10 test-reachable killed, 1 survived**, test-reachable 90.0% (Wilson 59.6–98.2% — the interval on 1/10 is wide and is reported as wide), **over a corpus 8 of whose 23 items S0.1 could not reach at all** (§1.2) [measured] | AC-288 (four-cell candidate × reference truth table); AC-289 (re-apply `repro.mutation.patch` at the reviewed SHA and run `repro.mutation.suite` — the fields the record now carries, ADR-0096; block only if the suite is still green with it applied and the mutant discriminates, with the S0.1 fixtures as the regression set); AC-287 (no re-execution record ⇒ no block, reported `Unverifiable`; replay assertion `blocks ⊆ re-executed`) |
| 4 | **Both-fail or cannot-run is `Unverifiable`, and `Unverifiable` is advisory** — a finding whose evidence the gate cannot separate does not block, in either direction | Timeout history is the precedent: a **900 s default written for the fast condition cost a CLEAN record**, then 2,400 → 3,600 → one 5,400 s retry [documented, brief §D] | AC-290 (three reasons — `both_fail` \| `cannot_run` \| `timeout` — persisted; a `doctor` rule that every `reproduced`/`mutation-survived` finding carries either a re-execution record or an `Unverifiable` reason). The rule is per-finding: "a review with 0 executed and 0 `Unverifiable` findings is impossible" is **false**, because a review producing no findings has exactly those counts and is legal |
| 5 | **The policy table is the only place a class becomes a block**, parameterised by stage rigor | Class means `reproduced` **0.70** > `mutation-survived` **0.37** > `narrowed-pin` **0.10** > control **0.00** set the default *order and nothing else* [measured, F29] | AC-294 (a private constructor plus a module-boundary lint: the build fails if a block value is constructed outside `rk-kernel::policy`, with a `trybuild` compile-fail test); AC-296 (`advisory` is in no block set at any weight — a design invariant of the table with **no measurement behind it**, and labelled as such) |
| 6 | **`Quarantined { reason, by, until }` is a first-class finding state** — a **review-gate** state only: it stops a finding blocking *review* and stops it being re-raised until `until`; it is **not** an answer to AC-346, so a quarantined mutation survivor still blocks stage exit (ADR-0104 decision 4). The shape is **convergent**, not original: SARIF 2.1.0 §3.35 `suppression { status, justification }`, Snyk ignore `--reason`/`--expiry` (30 d default), GitHub dismissal, SonarQube, Semgrep triage — whether it maps onto `suppressions[].status` is **D4-SARIF**'s question (ERRATA E-0074-1; PIA-087) | Proposal §4.6 item 5 [documented]; F18 — a gate can outlive its own assumptions (max **226 h**); the prior-art survey [documented, fetched 2026-09-10] | AC-304 (quarantined ⇒ absent from the block set, still persisted and rendered with its reason); AC-319 (a quarantine with no `until` fails to construct); AC-306 (on expiry the finding is re-raised and subject to the prevailing weight **exactly as a newly raised finding of its class** — it blocks iff its class blocks, which is false for `advisory` and `Unverifiable`); AC-305 (identity is the computable tuple *(AC id, repro command digest, mutant id)* — "the same normalised claim" is a judgement no test can make, and the acknowledged cost is that a restatement with a different command re-raises) |
| 7 | **Negative-control fixtures stay in the corpus** as the reviewer's own regression test, and the S-SUBTLE family gains a **prior-approval framing** arm (ADR-0101, superseding ADR-0088 in full) | S-VAR control **0/10** rejections (Wilson 0–27.8%); S-SUBTLE **5/5 sighted and 5/5 blind, twice** [measured, F29, F26] | AC-312 as a **conjunction**: every seeded `reproduced` fixture blocks **and** every control produces zero — "zero blocks on controls" alone passes on a gate that never blocks anything; AC-312 ships **PROVISIONAL** with the framing-variant fixtures authored at 7c and unrun (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #17). See §6.4.5 for the new arm |
| 8 | **Every block and every override is an event**, and **false-block is measured against adjudication, never against historical approval** | F24: all six E3 rejections factually **true 6/6**, at zero model cost — the corpus's own labels were reversed by adjudication [measured] | AC-307 (the event carries `{ finding_id, ac, computed_class, model_label, stage_weight, sha, re_execution_id, actor, adjudication: null }`; replay invariant `count(blocks) == count(block_events)`); AC-308, AC-309 — see §6.4.4 |

Two structural criteria sit under clause 1 and are worth naming separately, because both are F20's lesson generalised. A review dispatch that exits 0 with **no `structured_output` block** fails the step with a missing-structured-output error and produces no finding set, empty or otherwise (AC-282) — F20 measured an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently disabling `--json-schema` at `exit 0`, `subtype: success` [measured], which is exactly how a review consumer fails open. And an architecture lint asserts **no code path in the review path extracts findings from assistant prose** — no JSON-from-text parse, no regex over the transcript body (AC-318; brief §C.5). Free text may not reach policy at all: AC-283's load-bearing instrument is a *signature* test that no free-text field is an input type to `policy::classify`, so two payloads identical except that one's rationale asserts "approved" compute byte-identical block sets.

#### 6.4.2 The policy table, the lenient default, and the Advisory sink

| Computed class | Lenient weight (default) | Strict weight | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `reproduced` | Blocks | Blocks | Class mean 0.70, highest of four; 19/19 rejections on target [measured, F29] |
| `mutation-survived` | Blocks | Blocks | 1 of 10 test-reachable mutants survived S0.1's affected-area gate [measured, F11 — wide CI on 1/10, and **8 of 23 corpus items sat outside S0.1 by construction**, §1.2]; four defects escaped the gate — two through green suites, two against an already-red baseline [measured, F31; E-0082-2]; the block counts are under the model-label policy (S-CLASS-REPLAY, PIA-097) |
| `narrowed-pin` | **Advises** | Blocks | Class mean 0.10 [measured, F29]; the relaxation cost no detection [measured, F31] |
| `advisory` (the fourth computed class) — and, carried on the finding as a **re-execution outcome, not a class**: `re_execution = Unverifiable { both_fail \| cannot_run \| timeout }` (`EvidenceClass` is the closed four computed from the repro record; `ReExecution = Flipped \| Unverifiable {…}` is a separate value, and SM-088's instrument is `re_execution = Unverifiable` — relabelled 2026-09-11, PIA-032) | Never blocks | Never blocks | ADR-0074 clauses 4–5 [documented] |

**The lenient default is the resolved weight when a stage sets none** (AC-298; ADR-0015 as renumbered by ADR-0020's deontic enum, `0.2 → optional`, `1.0 → mandatory`). `ReviewWeight` is defined once: the explicit projection of stage rigor `optional → ReadOnly`, `recommended → Lenient`, `mandatory → Strict`, in which AC-299/AC-300 are written — "weight" is the projected value, not a second axis beside stage rigor (PIA-032). The measurement behind it: lenient blocked **9/23** against strict's **12/23** on the pinned CLEAN set with **0/23 verified false positives** and detection unchanged (S-SILENT 23/23, S-SUBTLE 5/5) [measured, F31 — **under the model-label policy**: the counts came from `policy.blocks(evidence)` on the model-emitted label, and the Rust-computed class is unmeasured until S-CLASS-REPLAY, E-0074-2, PIA-097]. **Three caveats travel with that number and may not be dropped**, the model-label one being the third. First, **the severity call itself — "narrowed-pin advises" — is one operator's judgement (Ryan's), stated as such**; what was measured is only that the relaxation cost no detection. Second, **CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for ≥13 of 23 (57%)** [contested, brief §E], so *0/23 verified false positives* is an **upper bound** (Wilson 0–14.3%) and **CLEAN is never a denominator for an accuracy or false-positive-*rate* claim anywhere in this design** — the fractions in this paragraph are block **counts** over the pinned 23-item set, and the Wilson interval is quoted only to show how wide the floor result is, never as an accuracy rate. AC-295 makes the weight a one-line replayable change: moving lenient→strict alters the block set **only** by adding `narrowed-pin` items, with no re-dispatch and a byte-identical classifier output.

The relaxation leaves a hole, and **ADR-0082 fills it**: **seven `narrowed-pin` findings, every one of them adjudicated TRUE**, move from blocking to non-blocking [measured, F31 — `issue-130` AC-005, `issue-81` AC-002, `issue-82` AC-003, `issue-83` AC-001, `fix-doctor-git-config-repair` AC-002 and AC-004, `memoize-repo-root` AC-003]. F29's branch 4 says what the sink is *not* for: the negative control rejected 0 of 10 draws, so no sink is needed to absorb noise. It is needed to carry **real, adjudicated-true signal that does not block**.

| Advisory-sink rule (ADR-0082) | Enforcement |
| --- | --- |
| **Written, not dropped** — in the same typed `Findings` shape as a blocking finding, `repro` record, rationale and advisory label unaltered; the only on-disk difference is the computed disposition | AC-310: replay assertion `findings_in == blocks + sink_records`, plus a `doctor` rule that errors when a completed review's non-blocking finding count exceeds its sink's record count; the seven F31 findings are the seed fixture and must all land |
| **Surfaced at the next gate without blocking** — as typed attachments bound to the gate's question (ADR-0063), under their own heading, excluded from the block computation; a gate answer is never conditioned on one and their presence alone never opens a gate | AC-310 rendering; ADR-0063 attachment schema |
| **Retired only by re-review or an explicit recorded human act** — age, run count, gate passage and stage advancement retire nothing | AC-311 (the triage disposition is a human gate — whether an advisory finding is worth a backlog item is a value call; the machine-checkable half is that every sink record reaches a terminal disposition or is surfaced as ageing) |
| **Advisory and `Quarantined` are disjoint** — quarantine applies **only** to a finding policy computed as blocking; quarantining an advisory finding is an **error, not a no-op**, because the override telemetry would record a suppression that never happened; and quarantine is review-gate only — it does not satisfy AC-346 at stage exit (ADR-0104 decision 4) | A finding is in exactly one of `blocking`, `quarantined`, `advisory` at any tree (ADR-0082 clause 4); AC-345 and SM-095 say "review-gate only" |
| **The disposition is computed, never asserted** — the model may not label its own finding advisory to avoid a block, and code may not promote an advisory finding to blocking without re-executing the flip criterion | AC-284, AC-296 |

**Named gap:** nothing in the corpus measures whether a sink is *read*. SM-089 tracks routed-equals-created and age at disposition, with an explicit landfill kill — if the sink's p90 age exceeds F18's **stage→verdict** p90 (~9 h, n=191; a *different series* from the 10.5 min gate→verdict median, n=174) for a month, advisory findings should be dropped at source rather than pretended into a backlog.

#### 6.4.3 The reviewer's environment — the disposable shallow clone

F17 is the reason this is structural rather than instructed: the E3 reviewer **restored the fix from the object store** — 42 turns, 22,853 output tokens, 526 s — and returned `approved` [measured as an instrument defect]. The reviewer keeps `Bash`, in a **disposable shallow clone at the reviewed SHA** (ADR-0074), and the clone's shape is asserted on four properties: `--depth 1`, no `objects/info/alternates`, no remote pointing at the parent repo, and `git cat-file -e <pre-change blob>` failing (AC-292). Write isolation is likewise asserted on the **spawn configuration** — the reviewer's process has no write path to the candidate worktree or the store — with a before/after `rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` and `status --porcelain` probe kept only as corroboration (AC-291). The reasoning is explicit: a probe that depends on the model *choosing* to attempt a write cannot establish a negative, and F17 shows it took 42 turns to find its route. Every review outcome carries the tree digest of the SHA reviewed, non-optional, with no default, no empty string and no `None` (AC-293).

#### 6.4.4 Telemetry — and what false-block is measured against

| Measure | Instrument and baseline | Discipline |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **SM-086 / AC-308 — verified false-block rate** | Denominator is **adjudicated blocks only**; historical approval (merged, human-approved) is **never** usable as ground truth; unadjudicated blocks are reported as a separate `pending_adjudication` count. **No compliant baseline exists yet.** The rule is adjudicated-blocks-only, and F31 adjudicated **14/14** — so the only reading with a compliant denominator is **0 of 14 adjudicated**. The figure quoted elsewhere as 0/23 is over CLEAN's *item* count, not over adjudicated blocks; it is carried strictly as an **upper bound against a ground truth wrong for ≥13/23** (Wilson 0–14.3% over that wrong denominator) [measured, F31]. Comparator: Cloudflare break-glass **288/48,095 = 0.6%** [documented] | **NO COMPLIANT BASELINE** except **0 of 14 adjudicated**, which is SM-086's compliant baseline — and the reason the rest is non-compliant is the denominator, not the adjudication. The E3 severity adjudication **closed 2026-09-09** (facts settled 6/6; severity from the 2026-09-04 sheet, **filled by the model at Ryan's direction, NOT BLIND, κ forfeited**, §6.6 — one operator), so the two FP rates and two MCCs are settled — arm (a) 1/23 / **+0.957** (tp 23 / fn 0 / fp 1 / tn 22; E-0074-3), arm (b) 0/23 / +1.000 — and both rates remain **upper bounds** against CLEAN's wrong ground truth, which is what this measure's own rule forbids using; SM-086 stays PROVISIONAL because the sheet is not an independent rater (E3-SEV-BLIND is the open route), not because the adjudication is open [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-081]. Its target reads "n ≥ 640 adjudicated blocks at zero false blocks (Wilson upper < 0.6%), more if any false block is observed" — not "n ≥ 500" (PIA-073). The research pass found **no published false-block comparator beyond Cloudflare's 0.6%**; SWE-Gate's 34% measures a different quantity [documented] |
| **SM-087 / AC-313 — confirm-half determinism** | The k=10 replay job over recorded findings, no dispatch, $0: the block-set digest must be identical on 10/10 **and equal to the pinned non-empty expected digest** — without the non-empty clause a gate that blocks nothing scores 10/10 | This is the measure that converts F29's 3-of-10 verdict flip from a correctness problem into a **recall** problem: the machine half must not flip at all |
| **SM-091 — model label vs computed class drift** | `review_class_drift` events; **no baseline**. Nearest measured shape is F27 (verdict flaky 2/4, detection flaky 0/7) | **Diagnostic, never gated.** Two-sided and both directions informative: drift ≈ 0 for three releases means the model label is redundant and can leave the schema; sustained high drift is direct evidence for keeping computation in Rust and **must never be "fixed" by trusting the label** |
| **SM-085 — block rate by computed class** | Strict 12/23 (Wilson 33.0–70.8%), lenient 9/23 (22.2–59.2%) [measured, F31] — **counts, not accuracy** | No target rate. The signal is a step change in the ratio *between* classes, never the level. Kill: if the `reproduced` share falls below the `advisory` share for three consecutive releases, the executable-claim contract has stopped producing executable claims |
| **SM-092 / SM-093 — controls and seeded recall** | Control 0/10 (0–27.8%); seeded detection 23/23, 5/5, 23/23 vs 23/23 — no discordant pairs, no test; the McNemar **p = 0.625** is on the CLEAN FP 3-vs-1 discordant pairs (E-0001-1) [measured, F29/F26/F23] | **Both saturated, and reported as saturated.** SM-093 is a one-way canary with **no kill** — a drop below ceiling is a signal, a stay at ceiling means nothing, and it is never used to compare two configurations. The pair cannot discriminate in either direction; what keeps them from being satisfied by a gate that never blocks is AC-312's seeded half, which is a criterion, not a measure |
| **SM-096 — wall clock and timeout pressure** | Review median **1,071 s (~18 min)**, slowest **4,561 s** [measured, F31]; the deterministic gate it feeds has **no affected-area selection today** — every run is the whole 236-file suite, ~250 s to >900 s over 1,890–1,927 tests [measured] | The 5% timeout-`Unverifiable` target is **chosen, not derived**, and says so. The original second half ("review p90 inside the item's gate budget") is dropped: no gate budget exists anywhere in the evidence, so it could neither pass nor fail |

Review **cost** per tier — the $ figures, the tier comparators and the cache arithmetic — belongs to §7 and is not restated here.

#### 6.4.5 The direction this gate is *not* yet guarded against

Everything above measures one direction of judge bias: **false rejection**. Jin & Chen measure rationale-heavy prompting driving false rejection (GPT-4o FNR **26–36% → 58–88%**; a fix-guided verification filter recovers 54.8%→16.3% HumanEval, 69.0%→28.9% MBPP, 51.0%→24.0% QuixBugs) [preprint/peer, arXiv 2603.00539], and ADR-0074's rebuild answers it directly.

**The citation correction, because it changes how much the rebuild rests on.** The proposal cited "*Are LLMs Reliable Code Reviewers?* (arXiv 2603.00539)" and "Jin & Chen, fix-guided verification filter" as **two entries. They are one paper** — arXiv 2603.00539's author list is exactly "Haolin Jin, Huaming Chen", the filter is proposed in its abstract, and both number sets live in that one paper, the filter's in §7.2 / Table 4. Every individual figure re-verifies exactly; the defect is **provenance, not arithmetic** [documented; research pass §1 row 9, §4 rec 11]. Merged here into one citation, because counting one paper as two **overstated the independent replication behind the D4 rebuild**: the rebuild rests on fewer independent replications than the proposal implied. It still rests on this programme's own F29/F31/F27 measurements, which is where its weight actually is.

**The mirror risk, registered and unrun (ADR-0101, superseding ADR-0088 in full).** *Measuring and Exploiting Contextual Bias in LLM-Assisted Security Code Review* — a controlled experiment over **250 CVE vulnerability–patch pairs** — finds that **strong bug-free framing** before review cuts vulnerability detection by **16–93 percentage points on 5 of 6 models** (Opus 4.5 −4.9 pp, not significant; weak framing 0.8–24.7 pp), and that the mechanism is a **whole-classifier shift toward "clean": false negatives rise and false positives collapse** (+46 to +95 pp true negatives) — the opposite of ADR-0088's FP-stable reading [documented, arXiv 2603.18740 v2, full text fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-013]. The S-SUBTLE family therefore gains a **prior-approval framing arm** (S-SUBTLE-CB): the same items presented twice, **paired per item**, framing as the only manipulated variable, same schema, same policy table, same stage weight. It reports **detection, not verdict** — the manipulation predicts a whole-classifier shift and detection is the stable half of this instrument (F27) — and reports missed seeded defects **and the CLEAN-pair approval rate separately, every time**, because a pooled error rate hides a shift of the operating point and the corrected mechanism predicts FP will *fall*, which a pooled rate would read as an improvement (ADR-0101 clause 3). The symmetric "presented as already rejected" arm is **S-SUBTLE-SYM**, registered rather than rejected (ADR-0101 clause 7). **CLEAN is an observational instance of the framing, never the controlled arm**: it is genuinely prior-approved input carrying real defects (≥13/23), which makes it evidence that the framing occurs in the field and unusable as the comparison, since its framing cannot be removed and its ground truth is known wrong.

> **Until that arm runs, no section of this document — and no other document — may claim the review gate is guarded against false approval, and no S-VAR or S-TYPED false-positive rate may be presented as evidence of overall gate calibration. Those rates measure the other direction.** The arm must be **sized before it is run**: at the family's present n=5 with detection at ceiling, only a large effect is observable and a 16-point drop is not, so the registration carries its own sizing or reports a bound rather than a rate, with its predictions (detection down; CLEAN-pair approval up; Opus-class possibly null) in METHOD-S before any draw (ADR-0101 clauses 5–6). Registered in §10 with its named consumer.

### 6.5 The verification gate — affected-area selection and per-AC mutation evidence

#### 6.5.1 Selection must prove it selected, and the fallback is recorded as basis

The failure this half exists to catch is measured and silent: a filter that did not survive package-manager forwarding turned `pnpm test -- --run <file>` into a **whole-suite run on 26 of 68 invocations** (**38.2%**, Wilson 27.6–50.1%), where `npx vitest run <file>` is **1 file / 6 tests / 15.6 s** against 236 files and 1,890–1,927 tests at ~250 s to >900 s — a **~60× difference that ran undetected** [measured, brief §D; one repo, one machine].

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| A run that did not narrow records `basis: whole-suite`, **never** `affected` | AC-322 |
| The **argv actually handed to the OS process API** is recorded as an argument vector, not the templated command string, and re-executing it verbatim reproduces the same executed-test count | AC-325 |
| A run claiming `affected` whose executed count meets or exceeds the toolchain's last recorded whole-suite count is reclassified `whole-suite / selection-ineffective` with an event | AC-326 — with the comparator **bootstrapped**: when no whole-suite count is recorded for the toolchain at the current version pin, a run claiming `affected` is reclassified `whole-suite` with reason `probe-unverified` and its executed count seeds the comparator; AC-329's `total_test_count` on an `affected` run is the most recent recorded whole-suite count at that pin, naming the run it came from; a pin bump resets it (ADR-0104 decision 3). **Stated gap, not repaired:** this catches only a *fully inert* filter; one that drops most of its arguments but not all — selecting 200 of 236 files — passes. AC-327's probe and SM-097's narrowing distribution are the compensating instruments and neither is a substitute |
| `doctor` executes the declared selection command against a single-file probe and fails the pack unless the executed count is **strictly less** than the fixture's whole-suite count; the rule emits zero harness dispatches | AC-327 |
| No `basis: affected` record may exist for a toolchain whose `affected-selection` capability has no probe result at the current version pin — selection is refused `Unsupported` and the pack routes to an explicit whole-suite policy | AC-328; SM-105 (**zero toolchains carry such a probe today**; first measurement establishes it) |
| A non-empty changed set selecting **zero** tests never renders green — it escalates to `whole-suite / selection-empty` or fails per declared policy | AC-332 |
| `basis` and `basis_reason` are **closed enums** matched exhaustively with no wildcard arm, so a new variant is a compile error; reasons, each defined once (PIA-032): `no-selection-support` — the toolchain declares no `affected-selection` capability; `probe-unverified` — the capability has no probe result at the current version pin, or no whole-suite count is recorded there (the bootstrap case, ADR-0104 decision 3 — the reason AC-328 emits); `selection-empty` — a non-empty changed set selected zero tests (AC-332); `selection-ineffective` — the executed count met or exceeded the last whole-suite count (AC-326); `policy-forced` — the pack's declared whole-suite policy overrode selection; `toolchain-unsupported` — a changed path is claimed by a toolchain with no selection support | AC-352 |
| **The basis is shown wherever a human reads the outcome** — a reader cannot see "green" without seeing what ran, and a rendering that omits it fails | AC-323 |

Two anti-vacuity controls hold the set together: AC-355's paired fixtures (a lockfile change whose correct basis is `whole-suite`, a single-file change whose correct basis is `affected`) fail a selector that always answers either way; and AC-350 requires every changed path to be **accounted for** across a multi-toolchain repo — one result per claiming toolchain, the gate their conjunction, and a path claimed by no configured toolchain is a configuration error rather than a silent exclusion.

Selection itself is **code, not a dispatch**: a pure function of the changed-file set, the toolchain config and `facets.toml`; a verify run emitting any harness dispatch fails the activity (AC-347, SM-103 — expected to saturate at zero and tracked anyway, because the value is the moment it stops being zero). **This is where the BLOCKED selection engine touches this gate.** P1's kill fired **4 of 4**: the modal `R(stage, ctx)` repeated on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line, with alpha drift **−0.168 on byte-identical anchors** [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void]. **Two claims are licensed now, and they are about two different vectors.** F36's claim is **instability**, and it is about the **LLM-emitted** vector: nothing there is repaired by a temperature change. F39's claim is **inaccuracy**, and it is about the **derived** one: scored against 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f` (all scored after the key fix; 136 on 2026-09-09, E-0089-2), **no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar** — `auth` **0.00**, every positive false (0/12 [0.00, 0.24]), `flag_exists` **0.00** [0.00, 0.66], `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88], `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85], `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89], `ui` 1.00 [0.65, 1.00] whose point clears and whose interval does not, and `data_migration` **not measurable** — while the ≥ 0.95 clause is itself unreachable at n=20 [measured, F39; $0, panel-adjudicated]. The earlier revision of this sentence read "instability, not inaccuracy" flat; **F39 overturns that for the derived facets** and ADR-0089 is the record. AC-302 carries the same standing negative on the review side: the **review tier** derives only from the authored stage rigor weight and authored facets, and no LLM-emitted context vector is an input. AC-351 completes it at the registry: a `require` row or selection rule referencing a facet with no live entry in `facets.toml` is a lint error naming the facet — which today fails on `binary_change` (measured but unregistered — and now measured at precision **0.60** [0.23, 0.88] as well, F39) and has no entry for `dynamism`; `recent_deploy` is underivable at this pin [contested, brief §E]. **ADR-0089 adds the second half of that lint:** a live entry is no longer sufficient, because a facet may not declare `source = "derived"` without a named `deriver` and a scored precision record for it, and a lexical scan of free text does not satisfy PC8 rule 2 for `require`-row purposes at all.

#### 6.5.2 CI is independently authoritative — and independence runs both ways

A green CI status reported through the Tracker port **never satisfies verify**: with CI green and verify `Err`, the item does not advance. And no verify result writes to the Tracker port's status-check state (AC-341). The instrument is a static check that the verify gate's input type contains **no tracker or CI field**, plus a port-call assertion that a verify run issues zero status-check writes. The two systems are separately authoritative over separate questions and neither substitutes for the other; this is the same shape as §6.2's rule applied to a third party.

A verify result that lacks its `basis`, suite digest or executed-command record resolves to `Err`, never to a pass — `Result<VerifyReport, StepError>` at the boundary with no `Default` impl and a lint forbidding `unwrap_or_default` (AC-324; F20's fail-open mechanism again). A run killed by its timeout or by the OS is `Unverifiable` and is **never** recorded as a pass or as a test failure attributable to the candidate (AC-331; F22). A model-authored `pass`, `verdict` or `approved` field reaching this gate is a schema violation, not a field to ignore (AC-343).

#### 6.5.3 Per-AC mutation evidence — the measured mechanism

**An AC is a claim about ONE ASSERTION, and Red→Green is evidenced at TEST granularity.** On the AC-015 probe, removing step 7 turned the suite RED while **inverting the ordering left 36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, F25; **n=1 AC, one operator**]. That single probe is the mechanism the whole area defends, and it is a mechanism finding rather than a rate — consistent with the programme's own method finding that every discriminating result came from cost, stability or mechanism, never from success rates (brief §C.12).

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| Every AC row in the evidence ledger carries an **assertion locator** — file, test identifier, and the assertion within that test. A row naming only a test file fails validation, and an AC with no evidence is materialised `unpinned`, never omitted | AC-333 |
| An AC is `pinned` **only if the named assertion flips** when its **author-declared** mutation is applied — each AC row in a spec carries `mutation: { patch, suite }` or `not-feasible(reason)`, authored with the AC, a reverted-fix patch or one with a discriminating assertion locator (ADR-0104; no mutant is derived from prose at run time, and AC-334's flip predicate is the one predicate — AC-317 becomes the harness-invocation criterion and drops "removed, and inverted"); if the suite stays green the AC is `mutation-survived`. The AC-015 inversion is a named regression row that must yield `mutation-survived` [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-049, PIA-051] | AC-334 |
| A green suite changes **nothing** about an AC whose mutation survived — enforced by a static check that the AC-status function does not take the suite verdict as an argument | AC-335 (the mechanism: F31's four escaped defects — two through 1,890/1,890 and 1,907/1,907 green suites, two against an already-red baseline) |
| The mutation is verified **still present at completion**; a run in which it was reverted or restored before the suite finished is `Unverifiable`, never `mutation-survived` and never `pinned` | AC-338 — the F17 fixture, an agent-restorable mutation, is the named case |
| Mutation runs in a disposable clone or worktree; the item's own tree hash is identical before and after and `git status --porcelain` is empty | AC-339 |
| A surviving mutant is a `mutation-survived` **finding**, blocking under the pack's declared default weight, and is never dropped because the rest of the suite is green | AC-340 — with the honest note that F31's severity call is Ryan's, so the default weight is a decision this criterion **enforces**, not one the evidence sets. *(Added 2026-09-24, PROGRESS §5's follow-up of 2026-09-11: the policy weight is not the only route to a block. A **quarantined** survivor still blocks the stage's **exit**. Quarantine (ADR-0074 clause 6) is a review-gate state and does not pin the AC, which stays unpinned until a discriminating mutant flips or a human gate answers that AC (ADR-0104 decision 4; AC-346, AC-357). AC-345 and SM-095 govern the review gate only.)* |
| Evidence recorded at sha *N* does not count toward `pinned` at *N+1* unless the memo key (inputs, card digest, toolchain version, suite sha) is identical; a reuse is recorded as a memo hit with its key, never as a fresh run | AC-336 |
| A stage may not exit with an AC that is neither pinned by a flip-verified assertion nor answered at a human gate; the refusal names the unpinned AC ids and is an **event**, not only a console line | AC-346 |

**Where mutation is not feasible**, the toolchain declares it explicitly — an absent declaration is a configuration error — and the AC records `mutation: not-feasible(reason)`, which is not `pinned` (AC-337). The residual routes to a human gate carrying the AC text, the assertion source and the suite result, answered with `by` and `at` (AC-357) — and a review-gate quarantine is **not** that answer: an AC whose mutant survived stays unpinned until a discriminating mutant flips or AC-357 answers *that AC* explicitly (ADR-0104 decision 4): deciding whether an assertion discriminates a criterion with no executable mutation available is a judgement about meaning, and F24 and F31 both show severity judgements in this corpus remained one operator's even where the facts were mechanically settled. That human gate is the *same* gate mechanism as §6.3 — AC-359 binds it to the tree hash, voids a drifted answer, re-opens with the drift attached, and forbids carrying pre-drift verify evidence across as `pinned`.

**False-block telemetry on this gate follows §6.4.4's rule exactly** (AC-358, SM-104): the denominator is adjudicated blocks only, a block never counts as false because the item was historically approved, and an unadjudicated block is excluded rather than counted either way. SM-104's ≤1% target is an **aspiration set without a supporting baseline** — 0/23's Wilson interval does not exclude 14.3%.

**Coverage has no baseline at all.** SM-100's ≥0.90 target is likewise stated as an aspiration: **no AC-level pinning rate has ever been measured**, the nearest evidence being F25's single probe and F31's four survivors, neither of which is a rate. Its kill is the honest one — if coverage cannot exceed ~0.5 because mutation is infeasible for most ACs in real repositories, per-AC mutation evidence is not the *normal* TDD evidence and **ADR-0019's "norm" claim needs rewriting rather than enforcing**.

#### 6.5.4 Where this gate touches a blocked row

- **`ChangeSummary` — BLOCKED on S-BIN, registered and unrun.** [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*; ADR-0199 decision 5 keeps AC-337's `not-feasible(reason)` route for a `binary`-class AC, with AC-357's human gate as its evidence and no AC-334 exemption, so the per-AC mutation norm is not switched off; AC-356 is amended by its 2026-10-14 note] AC-356 is **PROVISIONAL, and its evidence standard is BLOCKED on S-BIN rather than provisional-but-specified**. The *sketch* is binary paths represented by manifest plus capture references rather than text diff, with per-AC mutation evidence not required for a `binary`-class AC (its evidence would be "asset touched + test/capture result"). **Switching off §6.5.3's per-AC mutation norm for a whole AC class is a substantive gate decision**, and taking it on the one class for which — in this document's own words — no review evidence of any kind exists would be exactly the move §0.1 forbids. So it is not taken: the per-AC mutation norm stays in force for every class until S-BIN reports, no fixture naming `ChangeSummary`'s fields may be written until then, and this sentence is a design sketch under a block, not a relaxation. The field names **must not be frozen** before S-BIN runs against the real Unreal project, and this criterion may not be promoted to binding on the strength of this document (ADR-0008, ADR-0012). It gates Phase 1 for the type.
- **The Advisory sink does not exist yet.** AC-344 routes `Unverifiable` findings to it; that is a *requirement on the sink*, not an assumption that one is there, and it is unmeetable until ADR-0082's sink is built.
- **No whole-suite p95 exists.** AC-330's `doctor` rule fails a pack with no numeric `timeout_secs` (an inherited default is a failure) and *additionally* fails a declared timeout below a recorded whole-suite p95 — but the field was never recorded, so at first run only the declaration half can fire. Stated as a property of the rule rather than hidden inside it.
- **The quality shorthand's default is not set here.** ADR-0080 leaves it explicitly unset (its re-run must change the criterion, ADR-0111), and the forbidden predicate `findings.none(evidence in ["Reproduced", "MutationSurvived"])` may not be a quality block's terminal condition — it is satisfiable only by a reviewer that finds nothing, which F23/F26/F31 prove this reviewer is not. What this section *does* enforce is monotonicity: **no rigor escalation may remove the review-confirm step from the resolved graph** (AC-303), with F37's `mandatory` + `has_repro` case — which selected `bisect`, an activity with no adversary and no policy step, now ceilinged at `max_rigor = "recommended"` (ADR-0093 decision 5) — as a named lattice-lint regression row. AC-303 is written so it does not depend on F37's cost ratio, which is two denominators — **3.66× (arm mean, n = 30) and 4.4× (issue-77 r1, single run)** — reconciled under ADR-0106.
- **Cascades are refused, not priced.** A pack configuring a cheap-first review cascade (a read-only pass gating whether execution-grade runs) fails validation with the reason "cascade recall loss unmeasured"; the refusal lifts only when a named spike measures that loss — **S-CASCADE**, registered in §10.5, unrun, not gating Phase 1 (AC-301; PIA-088). AC-299's read-only tier is a definition with no measurement behind it.

### 6.6 Decisions and open items

| Decision | Alternatives considered | Why this one | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Human gate is a durable `(token, tree_hash, deadline)` mark; the opening process exits 0 (**ADR-0009, ADR-0017**) | A waiting/daemon process; an item-keyed answer with no token; a commit-SHA pin as GitHub does | Nothing may depend on a process surviving; item identity cannot detect drift; a SHA pin is weaker in one direction and noisier in the other | F22, F28 [measured]; 1P platform survey [documented] |
| A stale answer is **void**, not reconciled | Merge the stale answer; defer and re-apply; path-scoped drift test | ADR-0017's rejected option is literally "reconcile stale answers"; drift is 6.9% overall and its cost (SM-081) is unpriced, so a cheaper binding cannot yet be justified | F19 [measured] |
| Expiry is terminal — `fail` / `escalate` / `auto-decide`, chosen per class by a human, resolved at the next read | An implicit default action in code; a timer or cron; no deadline at all | A silent default makes `auto-decide` reachable by omission; a process alive at a future instant is not a mechanism this system has | F22 [measured]; ADR-0017 [documented]; 1P terminal-event survey [documented] |
| Escalation **suspends and resumes into the same step**; it is not activity-terminal (**ADR-0087**, its cost clause superseded by **ADR-0106**) | Activity-terminal escalation, discarding partial work and re-entering from the top — the pre-registered branch had the >1.5× kill fired | The shape rests on losslessness — F19 174/174 at activity grain, F38 §1 3/3 across a real kill — **not on cost**: the sub-1× output-token ratios first quoted divided unlike counters (E-0087-1), the cost ordering is UNMEASURED, and placement is not derived from a cost model in either direction | F19, F38 §1 [measured, PARTIAL at n=3] |
| Review gate is **reviewer proposes, machine confirms** (**ADR-0074**, fully superseding ADR-0004) | Trust the model verdict; trust the model's own evidence label; block on findings without re-execution | The verdict is a weighted coin (3-of-10 approval on a reproduced regression) while the claims are sound (19/19 on target); removing the verdict cost no detection and conformed 52/52 | F29, F31, F27 [measured] |
| **Lenient default** (`narrowed-pin` advises) plus a first-class **Advisory sink** (**ADR-0074, ADR-0082**) | Strict default; drop advisory findings silently; reuse `Quarantined` for advisory | The relaxation is free on detection; the seven adjudicated-true `narrowed-pin` findings are the most expensive evidence the programme produced and need a destination; quarantine on a non-block would record a suppression that never happened | F31 [measured under the model-label policy — S-CLASS-REPLAY owed, E-0074-2]; **severity call is one operator's judgement** |
| Verification gate: **affected-area selection with the fallback recorded as basis**, plus per-AC mutation evidence with **author-declared mutants** (**ADR-0019, ADR-0029, ADR-0073, ADR-0104**) | Whole-suite only; per-test Red→Green as sufficient evidence; trust the toolchain's own claim of selection; machine-derived mutants from the THEN clause | A ~60× wall difference ran undetected for 26 of 68 invocations; and an AC is a claim about one assertion — inverting AC-015's ordering left 36/36 green | brief §D, F25, F11 [measured] |

| Open item | State | Consumer | Hedge |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P8's **65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three no-kill controls** (P8-B) | Unrun; **BLOCKED** for the "design final" label; the cost ordering **UNMEASURED** | ADR-0106; SM-073 (no baseline) | AC-266 enforces the 1.5× bar on `cache_creation_input_tokens` beside `total_cost_usd`, not ≤1×; no mid-graph cost sentence may state an ordering in either direction |
| **Second-arrival semantics** (does a re-reached gate mint a new mark?) | Not answerable by any spike run — P8 §5.4(3) circular by construction; **declared PROVISIONAL: a re-reach mints a fresh `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`** (AC-524; ADR-0105 clause 10) | ADR-0087 clause 4; **AC-271 and SM-078 are duplicate-answer instruments** (a second answer against one mark is refused as spent; SM-078 counts them, cannot fail); the `gate_opened`-per-`(item, gate step)` counter is the discovery instrument | AC-271 is PROVISIONAL with an empty evidence line: the semantics are pinned by a test, not claimed to be evidenced. No `rearm` flag is added **or** ruled out |

| **Prior-approval framing arm** (S-SUBTLE-CB) and its symmetric twin (S-SUBTLE-SYM) | Registered and **unrun** (ADR-0101, superseding ADR-0088 in full) | The negative-control fixture design under ADR-0096; the review card's framing rules; the policy table for S-SUBTLE-SYM | **No claim that the gate is guarded against false approval**; no S-VAR/S-TYPED FP rate offered as overall calibration; the arm is sized before it runs or reports a bound, reports detection and the CLEAN-pair approval rate separately, and AC-312 ships PROVISIONAL meanwhile |
| **E3 severity adjudication** — **CLOSED 2026-09-09, NOT BLIND** | Facts settled 6/6; severity adjudicated 2026-09-04, **NOT BLIND** (filled by the model at Ryan's direction; κ forfeited, the two sheets are not independent raters), **one operator**: 4 BLOCKING, 2 ADVISORY; the blind re-annotation is **E3-SEV-BLIND** (registered, not taken) | AC-308, AC-309, SM-086, SM-104 | Both false-positive rates and both MCCs are settled — arm (a) 1/23 / **+0.957** (tp 23 / fn 0 / fp 1 / tn 22; the value first recorded was a computation error, E-0074-3), arm (b) 0/23 / **+1.000** — and both rates stay **upper bounds**, because CLEAN's ground truth is wrong for **≥13 of 23** and may never be a denominator; SM-086 stays PROVISIONAL on the missing blind annotation, not on an open adjudication. The design does not depend on them, only claims about its calibration do, and §6 makes none. **A settled severity vocabulary is still owed** — see §10.4 |
| **Advisory-sink readership** | No prior art measured; sink record count is 0 by construction | SM-089 | Landfill kill declared in advance: p90 age above F18's stage→verdict p90 (~9 h) for a month means drop advisory findings at source |
| **Cascade recall loss** | **S-CASCADE** registered (§10.5), unrun, does not gate Phase 1 | AC-301 | The cascade is **refused at pack validation**, not priced, until S-CASCADE reports |

| **Whole-suite p95 per toolchain** | Never recorded | AC-330, SM-102 | Only the declaration half of the doctor rule can fire at first run, and the rule says so |
