Every row above appears in §10's spike register with a named consumer and a "gates Phase 1?" column, per ADR-0012. **Cascade recall loss now does too** — it once read "No spike registered" here while ADR-0012 forbids exactly that, and **S-CASCADE** is registered in §10.5 (as this section's rows now say, PIA-088); **S-DEFER** is registered for the hook-`defer` row of the §6.3.2 survey. The whole-suite p95, per-AC pinning coverage and advisory-sink readership rows resolve to **first measurements from the running product** rather than to spikes, and are enumerated as such in §10.5's closing paragraph. None is discharged by prose here.

---

## 7. Cost, cache, parallelism and telemetry

### 7.1 The split cache rule — the two mitigations point in opposite directions

**This is the headline of the section and the one thing in it that is routinely got wrong (ADR-0078).** There is no "cache rule". There are two, on two different axes, and each buys its benefit by giving up the other's.

| Axis | Mitigation | What it buys | What it forfeits | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Space** — commits, worktrees, directories | A static `--system-prompt` — measured as **a one-line static prompt, no card**: F16 and S-WT ran a cardless one-line `--system-prompt`, never combined with `--agent`, so the card + `--system-prompt` combination is **unmeasured** (PIA-066; amended 2026-09-11) | Read share **1.000** across commits, against **0.689** for commit-each under the default prompt; **1.000** across steady-state worktrees w2–w10 after a cold w1 at 0.807 | The **shared-prefix** subsidy across elapsed time — not an account-level one; there is no account-wide shared head (§7.2), and "account-wide" is retired alongside "org-scoped": **29,680** tokens created at 66.8 min at read share **0.000** (**n=1**); S-H1b independently 1.000 at 1.5 min → **0.000 / 29,619** at 66.1 min (**n=1**, no finding number). **Two single draws, never an interval** | [measured] F16, 50 dispatches, within-arm range ≤0.001; S-WT (`spikes/results/s-wt.jsonl`, n = 9 steady-state worktrees, haiku, `--strict-mcp-config`, an 18-byte fixture tree, one draw per worktree; no finding number in the brief — F30 was drafted 2026-09-06 and promoted 2026-09-11 to `spikes/F30-DRAFT.md`); F28 as corrected, S-H1b, n=1 per cell |
| **Time** — idle gaps, human gates, resumes | The default prompt (no static card) | Flat cost across the two duration points taken: **18,603** tokens at 65.2 min and **18,603** at 49.7 h — identical to the token, **n=1 per duration point**, at most one prefix rewrite | Share across commits (**0.689**) and across worktrees (**0.685**) | [measured] F28 with its **published correction: the flatness was shared-prefix subsidy, not durability**; F16 |

**The forbidden inference, stated because the earlier proposal made it.** "Held across commits" does not imply "helps across time". That inference is not merely unsupported — it is **falsified** by the same static prompt reading share 0.000 at ~66 min (ADR-0078 clause (i); brief §C.1). Any citation of F16 or S-WT that does not name the *space* axis, or of F28/S-H1b that does not name the *time* axis, is a defect.

The design consequence is that the axis is a **card property, not a global setting**: AC-383 requires every agent card to declare `cache_profile = "static" | "default"` and refuses to load a card that declares neither, and AC-384 makes it a pack-lint error to place a `static`-profile activity immediately after a human gate — the placement that guarantees the ~30k rewrite once the ~1 h boundary passes, against 18,603 flat. That lint fires on a **bracketed** boundary, not a located one: F10 measured a full hit at 45.1 min and share **0.690** at 61.6 min, so the TTL is bracketed in (45.1, 61.6) and **was never located** [measured, n=1 per gap, date-stamped platform behaviour]. It matters because **45% of gate→verdict intervals (n=174) and 48% of stage→verdict intervals (n=191) exceed one hour**, at a gate median of 10.5 min, a stage p90 of ~9 h and a maximum of 226 h [measured, F18 — **one operator, one machine**].

The Phase-1 default is the static `--system-prompt` (ADR-0016's delivery clause, untouched by ADR-0078's partial supersession). **The argv per profile, stated once (2026-09-11, PIA-012):** under `cache_profile = "static"` the adapter passes the card body as `--system-prompt` (AC-211's GIVEN is "a card whose profile is `static`", not "a repository configuration"); under `cache_profile = "default"` the adapter **omits `--system-prompt` and prepends the card body to the user message** — F28's measured arm, the one whose 18,603-token flat prefix AC-384's lint message and SM-113's kill promise — with a golden argv test per profile; `default` is a delivered mechanism, not a lint-only label. The argv is capped before spawn (a typed configuration error, fixture at the cap) and `--system-prompt-file` stays unused pending S-SPF, because the flat-prefix promise rests on the inline route (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #18). **The append route is BLOCKED: S-H1c is registered and unrun**, so `cache_profile` has exactly **two** legal values today — `"static"` and `"default"` — and gains a third **only if S-H1c runs** (AC-383). **There is no shippable append-route option in Phase 1**: the append flags are absent from argv (AC-211). Where this document says the route "may not be enabled as anything but an unmeasured config option", it means that *if* S-H1c ever licenses a third value it enters unmeasured — never that a third value exists today.

### 7.2 Scope, corrected — and one candidate mechanism, marked inferred

The proposal's "the cache is org-scoped" is **withdrawn** (ADR-0078 clause (iii)).

| Path | Isolation boundary | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claude API · Claude Platform on AWS · Microsoft Foundry | **Workspace**-scoped — a workspace is a sub-unit of an org, so two workspaces in one org share nothing | [documented, 1P] platform.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08 |
| Bedrock · Google Cloud Agent Platform | **Org**-scoped | [documented, 1P] as above |
| **Claude Code — the path this design drives** | Additionally **machine + directory + git-status** scoped: cwd, platform, shell, OS version and a git branch/commit snapshot are embedded in the system-prompt layer | [documented, 1P] code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08 |

Two consequences follow directly and are budgeted, not chased:

1. **Two worktrees of one repository never share a prefix.** Every worktree pays a cold first turn. This is **structural, not a rig defect** [documented, 1P], and ADR-0078 forbids any budget that assumes otherwise.
2. **The CLI-version pin survives on a different mechanism.** Not "an upgrade anywhere on the account invalidates a shared head" — there is no account-wide shared head. A new version typically changes the system prompt or tool definitions, so the first request after an upgrade rebuilds from the top; auto-update applies on next launch, never mid-session; and the real exposure is a **resumed** long session reprocessing its history uncached [documented, 1P, fetched 2026-09-08]. That is precisely the human-gate resume path of ADR-0017. AC-385 therefore records the observed CLI version from the child's own `system/init` on every event and attaches a typed warning to every cache metric taken under a mismatch against `TESTED_CLI_VERSION`.

**[inferred]** The embedded cwd/platform/shell/OS/git-status block is a **candidate explanation** for F13's measured two-segment prefix — **~17,439 tokens shared plus ~7,827 per-directory, identical across 19/19 fresh repos** [measured, F13] — which is what reprices a directory change at ~31% rather than 100%. The shapes agree: a machine-and-directory-varying block sitting inside an otherwise shared prefix. **Nothing measured the correspondence and no token-level attribution was attempted.** It is marked inferred and is not licensed as the cause (ADR-0078, evidence section).

### 7.3 TTL: two buckets, two variables, and a contradiction that is open

The TTL is **bucketed, not a single value** [documented, 1P code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §1 row 1, severity **high**].

| Bucket | Contents | Default TTL | Raised by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Main conversation | Interactive turns, `-p` runs, Agent SDK | 1 h — **only** on a subscription drawing within plan usage; usage credits or an API key/cloud provider drop it to 5 m | `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` |
| Everything else | **Subagents, workflows, teammates, forks, compaction** | **5 m regardless of billing type** | `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h`, or a subagent's `cacheTtl` frontmatter — itself forced to 5 m on usage-credit billing |

Precedence is layered: `FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M=1` > bucket env var > bucket setting > subagent frontmatter > `ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H=1` > bucket default. Both the setting and the variable require Claude Code **v2.1.242+** [documented, 1P].

**Every dispatch this design generates is bucket two.** The runner therefore sets **both** variables, never one (ADR-0078 clause (ii) forbids setting the first without the second), records the pinned CLI version with the measurement, and records **billing mode — plan-included versus usage credits — as a declared confound on every cache measurement**, because usage-credit billing silently forces 5 m even on an explicit 1 h subagent request. ADR-0010 fixes the adapter to subscription-only auth, which makes plan-usage-versus-credits the live confound rather than the API-key one.

**Setting is not verifying — and the verification surface exists.** The documentation names `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` against `ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` as the per-run evidence that a TTL took. F3 read those fields as absent under the *flat* naming; **the nested `cache_creation { ephemeral_1h_input_tokens, ephemeral_5m_input_tokens }` object is present on 954 of 954 recorded runs and is parsed by the runner Step 7c lifts, whose `event.rs` emits a `ttl_split` field** [measured, F10/METHOD.md and the result files; PIA-067]. Charter E1's TTL-split metric was struck on the naming mismatch, not on a missing surface; F3 is re-read as "flat naming absent, nested object present" [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-067].

**And the contradiction is two-sided, not one.** F3 is the negative-signed observation — the verification fields are absent. The programme also holds a **positive-signed one pointing the other way**: `references/2026-09-04-findings-for-product.md` §14.5 Part A records **in-session subagents on a 1 h cache, not the documented 5 m**, and `spikes/HANDOFF.md` records that the RFC's "subagents get 5 minutes, always" was **only ever documentation**, with E3 arm (a) named as the first chance to observe it [documented; HANDOFF "What is genuinely unresolved" #3]. So the documented two-bucket rule is contradicted by an observation (the bucket-two default may not hold in-session), and the field-presence half is answered by the record. **S-TTL1 narrows to the billing-mode confound and a bucket-two (`ephemeral_5m > 0`) observation on a card that spawns a subagent** — a single `-p` dispatch is bucket one, and bucket two is reachable only if a card grants subagent tooling (PIA-058) — and until it runs neither the 5 m default nor the 1 h override may be asserted of any run on this rig without the per-run `ttl_split` evidence.

> **Cache-TTL verification — DECIDED with evidence on presence, BLOCKED on the confound. Spike S-TTL1, consumer: the cost model and every cache figure in §7.4 and SM-113.** The two `ephemeral_*` fields are present on 954/954 recorded runs (PIA-067), so clause (ii)'s verification requirement is dischargeable: a run is reported as 1 h only with its per-run `ttl_split` evidence, and **`TTL-unverified`** otherwise (ADR-0078 clause (iv)). What S-TTL1 still owes is the billing-mode confound and the bucket-two observation on a subagent-spawning card, with both variables set and billing mode recorded [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-067, PIA-058]. The design acts on the documentation for the **setting** — free and reversible whichever way the fields fall — and refuses to act on it for the **claim**.

`TTL-unverified` is the same treatment F6's quantisation already forced on utilisation figures via `resolution_limited` (§7.6): a flag on the reading, not a silent assumption.

### 7.4 Attribution: one child, one event, one cost row

Attribution is free because every activity is its own child process with its own card, schema, tool set, MCP config and cost record (§3; ADR-0022). It is not a reporting layer bolted on afterwards.

| Rule | Criterion | Mechanism that makes it hard to violate | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exactly one dispatch event per dispatch, on every termination path — success, `StepError`, cancellation, timeout | **AC-360** | Exhaustive `match` over `StepError` so a new variant fails to compile without an event arm; the `EventStore` fake (ADR-0003) as the assertion surface | [measured] F1, F3, F6 |
| Rollup is the sum of child events and nothing else — no apportionment, allocation or estimated share exists | **AC-361** | Parent record has no cost field; CI grep forbids `apportion`/`allocated_cost`/`estimated_cost` in `rk-kernel` | [documented] ADR-0022 |
| A field the harness does not report is recorded **absent**, never zero | **AC-362** | `Option` with no `Default` impl and no `unwrap_or(0)` at any call site; round-trip test that absent and zero stay distinguishable | [measured] F3 — the criterion an absent field lands on; the nested `ephemeral_*` object is present on 954/954 runs (PIA-067), so it is not the live instance |
| A run whose isolation failed is quarantined, not merely failed | **AC-386** → **AC-399** | `StepError::NotIsolated { surface: tools \| mcp_servers \| env, diff_digest }` on a `system/init` tool set unequal to the card's allowlist — the one class for the condition, in AC-204 as here (ADR-0095; DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #5) — never retried; every metric row from that run marked non-comparable and excluded from every aggregate | [measured] F8, brief C.11 |
| Tier 2 is rebuildable-or-disposable, and a partial loss changes no next action | **AC-365**–**AC-370**, **AC-397**, **AC-398**; **AC-164** (§5.4); SM-115, SM-116 | The delete-the-database test applied to the telemetry half: every tier-2 row must be reconstructible from git plus the log, or classified disposable at the point it is written | [documented] ADR-0018; the split test is §5.4's |
| Headroom has exactly one source | **AC-380** | `rate_limit_event` is the **sole** admissible source — typed in both Agent SDK references as `SDKRateLimitEvent` / `rate_limit_info { status, resetsAt, utilization }`, which the adapter parses tolerating that shape and F1's `unifiedWindows` shape with a named error on neither (PIA-069) — no other field, header or estimate may feed a headroom figure, and a degraded reading is `AC-363`'s typed degradation rather than a substituted number | [measured] F1, F6; [documented] code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript and /python, fetched 2026-09-10 |
| Telemetry cannot be switched off | **AC-394** | `EventStore` is a required parameter of the dispatch entry point — a build in which an activity can dispatch without one does not compile (`trybuild`) | [documented] ADR-0003 |
| Events are append-only; a correction is a new event | **AC-364** | No `update`/`delete` on the `EventStore` port | [documented] ADR-0018 |

**Isolation is a precondition of every cost figure in this section, not hygiene.** A default `claude -p` inherits the operator's whole environment: 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools, with cache creation **8,243 → 3,611** under `--strict-mcp-config` — MCP alone was **4,631 tokens, 56% of the default cache write** — and tools 280 → 34, at a TTFB of ~1,480 of ~3,400 ms [measured, F8]. The flag must be verified from the child's own `system/init`, because **an ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one from the parent's side** (brief C.11), and F20 shows a card's own `tools` allowlist can silently void a flag at exit 0 with `subtype: success`. SM-114 tracks MCP share of the cache write with a target of 0% on 100% of dispatches, verified from the child.

`redkiln cost <item>` (AC-395) renders the per-activity breakdown — token counters, `total_cost_usd`, wall clock, cache counters, and the batch headroom delta with its `resolution_limited` flag — **from the event store alone, dispatching no model call**, verified with the harness adapter replaced by a fake that panics on dispatch. Reading the record is free: F24, F25, F34, P6, P9, P9b, S-MIG and the F31 adjudication all cost **$0** [measured].

### 7.5 The three budgets

| Budget | Scope | On exhaustion | Rule | Criteria |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `attempts_total` | Per activity, counting **agent/tool steps per pass**, never summed retries | That counter's failure path only | Harness-internal retries observed on the stream are recorded as tier-2 events **and each charges the budget** — one charge per recorded `system/api_retry` event, in the pass that dispatched it — and the kernel adds no outer retry for the failure that terminated a dispatch the harness already retried inside it (corrected 2026-09-15 per **ADR-0122** decisions 1 and 2, accepted 2026-09-14; this cell read *"Harness-internal retries observed on the stream are recorded as tier-2 events and are **not** folded into the budget — the kernel cannot see or bound platform-side behaviour"*, which was **AC-391's *Cut*** restated as this design's own rule, and the cut text is **removed here rather than annotated**. ADR-0122 withdraws that *Cut*: its first ground is refuted against the source it cites, `references/2026-09-05-architecture-proposal.md`:252 read whole, whose Inner row reads *"Inner attempts count toward the activity's budget"* — *"observe, record, do not duplicate"* governs the kernel's **retry**, not its **counting**. The cut text also contradicted **§3.6**'s **Inner retry** row of this document — *"inner attempts count against the activity's budget"* — one table away. ADR-0122's own Evidence bullet reads *"the design already agrees, so no design amendment is owed"* on the strength of that row; that is true of **§3.6**'s **Inner retry** row and **false of this one**, which that pass did not read. (Both cites re-pointed from line numbers to the section and its quoted clause 2026-09-15: this document is under edit and a line number into it is self-falsifying.) ADR-0122 is frozen and accepted, so the correction lands here and dated rather than in the record (ADR-0092 decision 1), and it lands as an in-place dated amendment rather than an erratum — **DECISIONS-2026-09-15 D15-2**. The Scope cell's *"never summed retries"* is left standing and is not disturbed by this: it is ADR-0037's **declared-field** unit, the quantity ADR-0122 decision 3 keeps apart from the runtime draw-down, and **§3.6**'s **Budgets** row carries the same clarification — *"the sentence above governs the **declared** field"*) | ADR-0037, **ADR-0122**; **AC-026**, **AC-232**, **AC-390**, **AC-391** |
| Token appetite | Per item, **fixed at triage** | Typed budget error, a durable mark, `redkiln run` resumes from it | No stage, activity or agent may raise it; raising requires returning the item to triage. It never silently truncates inputs or downgrades the model | ADR-0037; **AC-389**, **AC-392** |
| Wall clock | Per run | That counter's failure path only | The three are independent: none may be replenished, borrowed against or converted into another | **AC-390** |

The appetite is enforced **from the item's own measured token counters and never from a headroom reading**, because a per-item draw is not resolvable at the instrument's resolution (**AC-393**; §7.6). It is set at a human gate bound to the tree hash with a deadline (**AC-396**) and is **explicitly not machine-decidable**: it trades expected quality against cost, and this programme has no measurement that discriminates on quality — five of its metrics saturated, and P7's rigor-default experiment was **powerless at n=3**, every Wilson interval overlapping by construction [measured, F37; brief C.12].

**The quality shorthand's default remains BLOCKED (ADR-0080).** P7's per-run costs are usable as *scale* (§7.8) and may not be used to set a default rigor for a lane. **S-P7B** under a changed criterion — a paired per-item or pooled-difference design at the n that reaches a stated MDE; n = 10 per cell is powerless (ADR-0111) — plus a settled severity vocabulary *(2026-09-26, ADR-0168 proposed: the severity vocabulary is settled as `blocking | advisory`; no admissible severity threshold T exists on this evidence, and ADR-0080 decision 2 bullet 2 governs)* is what unblocks it.

### 7.6 The throttle, and the three things the instrument cannot see

| What the instrument shows | What it cannot show | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `rate_limit_event.unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}.utilization`, readable from a top-level stream event — **now documented in both Agent SDK references as `SDKRateLimitEvent` / `rate_limit_info { status, resetsAt, utilization }`; the `unifiedWindows` shape F1 read is not documented** [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; PIA-069]; `result.rate_limit` is **null** [measured, F1] | Anything below **0.01** — `utilization` is quantised to one tick, so **per-run cost is not measurable** [measured, F6] | Batch-delta reporting only, over readings sharing one `resetsAt` (a batch crossing a reset is split per window with its own n), `utilization` parsed into **integer ticks** (`u16` hundredths, never an `f64`), `delta_ticks <= 1` flagged `resolution_limited`, `n < 10` reported `low_sample` with no delta quoted, and `not attributable` with the interleaving count on a shared account (**AC-372**, **AC-395**; **ADR-0110**); **no per-run headroom accessor exists**, asserted by a compile-fail test and a CI grep rule (**AC-373**) |
| That `five_hour` and `seven_day` are **proven independent, not mirrors** [measured, F6] | **The binding limit.** There is **no Opus/Sonnet family sub-limit key** in `unifiedWindows` [measured, F1]; the SDK lists `seven_day_opus` / `seven_day_sonnet` keys that no F1 run observed [documented, fetched 2026-09-10] | Throttling runs on the visible windows as a **proxy**, refusing at `ticks >= 100 - margin_ticks` with `margin_ticks ≥ 2` refused below that at configuration load (**AC-376**, **AC-377**; the refusal is `StepError::RateLimited { resets_at }` appended as an event without spawning); no code path may read, derive, estimate or synthesise a family-keyed value (**AC-375**; ADR-0110) — an invented figure would be a confident fiction |
| Nothing, at the volumes this programme ran: 40 dispatches / ~1.0M tokens (854k cache-read, 157k cache-creation; "~1.9M" was a miscount, PIA-077) moved `seven_day` **0.100 → 0.100** [measured, F14] | Whether cache reads draw down the window — **E0's cache-cost gate was STRUCK at 0.01 resolution, not answered** [measured, F14, F6] | **The cache is a latency and cost lever, never a headroom lever.** No cache metric may be an input to a headroom figure and no `headroom_saved` field exists anywhere (**AC-381**), enforced by type-level separation plus a positive test so the rule cannot be satisfied by gutting the calculator |

Headroom is quoted **as a batch mean over an explicitly named set, with n on it, and never per run**: **0.0098 `five_hour` and 0.0017 `seven_day` per CLEAN draw**, both derived from 60 draws pooled over four rows, 0 void (0.0102 over the three-row pool of 56 — the four-row figure is the one used downstream) [measured, F29; PIA-077]. **F21's earlier per-draw headroom figure is superseded — it was 7.8× too high** [measured, F29], and reading a per-draw number off a small sample is the exact error AC-373 exists to forbid. SM-111 sets the reporting floor at **batches of ≥10 dispatches** (10 × 0.0098 ≈ 0.098 of the window, about ten ticks) and states plainly that **this instrument saturates at the low end and is kept anyway** — it is the only view of the platform ceiling that exists.

Cache share is likewise not quotable at small n: an identical prompt read **0.584 / 0.698 / 1.000 / 0.722** at n=2, which the brief marks **explicitly NOT a result** [measured, F5]. AC-382 attaches the sample size to every emitted share and flags anything below the configured minimum (SM-113: n=10) as `low_sample`, refused as an input to any automatic decision.

**The measured binding constraint is wall clock, not headroom**: 60 draws took **16.2 h** [measured, F29]. SM-111's kill fires if two or more batches in a month halt on a margin refusal before exhausting their wall-clock budget — at which point the proxy has failed and the parallelism design changes. **Overage has no baseline and none is obtainable from this programme**: no overage payload was ever recorded, so AC-378's *detection* half is blocked pending one and only its retry-policy half (`StepError::Overage` maps to zero attempts) is assertable today. Absence of a report is not a measured zero (SM-112).

Nothing waits. A `StepError::RateLimited { resets_at }` is handled by writing a durable `retry_at` mark and **exiting 0** — no sleep, no poll loop, no live wait (**AC-379**), because nothing may depend on a process surviving [measured, operational, F22; the incident record and its figures are §6.3.1's].

### 7.7 Parallelism, the merge queue, and the fan-out stagger

| Rule | Shape | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Sequential within a tree** | One item's stages run strictly in order; one dispatch, one schema, per activity | Scaling study: sequential decomposition **−39% to −70%**, parallel **+80.9%**, error amplification **17.2× independent versus 4.4× centralised** [preprint, arXiv 2512.08296; Google Research — a v3 revision expanded to 260 configs / 6 benchmarks, the quoted figures are the original 180-config / 4-benchmark version] |
| **Parallel across project-DAG antichains** | One worktree per concurrent story — **required, not an optimisation**: parallel arms need separate fixture trees, because a killed `vitest` leaves a Windows handle that no `Stop-Process` releases [documented, `spikes/HANDOFF.md` defect (3), #26/#30], so a shared tree cannot be reclaimed after a cancel (§3.8.2, **S-CANCEL-WIN**) | [documented] per-agent worktree isolation is the 2026 default for parallel coding agents, with the convention "worktree-per-task, assign agents to worktrees rather than owning them permanently" [blog, augmentcode.com, fetched 2026-09-08] |
| **Serialised at a merge queue** | The queue, not the agent, is the authority on what lands | [vendor] Mergify *State of Merge Queues 2026*, 200,000+ merges across 477 teams: AI-assisted PRs broke main **1.9%** versus **4.4%** for non-AI **when routed through a merge queue** — the risk reduction appears to come from the queue, not from the agent being safer |
| **Per-story failure isolation** | A failed story quarantines its dependents rather than failing the antichain | **[inferred]** from a documented negative premise, and no criterion instruments it. The premise is [documented]: a worktree buys **file-level isolation only** — not task decomposition, semantic conflicts or merge selection, which stay the orchestrator's job [blog, augmentcode.com]. **That premise says nothing about quarantining dependents**; the quarantine rule is this design's own inference from it, and it is registered as owing a criterion in §7.10 |
| **Fan-out staggered and CPU-bounded** | Identical-prefix siblings held so the first pays the cold write. **The vendor's mechanism is hold-until-the-first-response-begins, capped at 5 s, then release together — not a per-sibling interval**: AC-388's fixed `i` between siblings is a different mechanism, and SM-114's A/B compares three arms — serial chain (`i` = 5 s), simultaneous (`i` = 0) and the vendor's shape — dropping any arm that shows no difference and reporting a null at n < 50 as powerless, not as no difference [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-057] | [documented, 1P code.claude.com/docs/en/workflows and /prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-10 — the Claude Code workflow-runtime default; platform.claude.com says only "wait for the first response before sending subsequent requests", with no number]; **AC-388** requires the configurable interval so SM-114's A/B has its comparison arms |

**The stagger's benefit has no local measurement.** The rule is `[documented]` from code.claude.com's workflow documentation and nothing in this programme measured it. SM-114 makes it a decision rule rather than a target — first-write-pays share among siblings, across three arms: the serial chain (`i` = 5 s), simultaneous (`i` = 0) and the vendor's hold-until-first-response-begins-then-release shape, A/B over **n ≥ 50 sibling pairs** — and **an arm that shows no difference at n ≥ 50 is dropped**, while a null below n = 50 is reported as powerless (S-FANOUT, §10.5; consumer the `rk-kernel` scheduler, AC-388). Until it runs the stagger is tracked, not decisive.

**The per-worktree cost is priced per cell and never interpolated (AC-387) — the evidence contest is resolved by the run record.** Brief §C.1 states that `--system-prompt` holds 1.000 across worktrees w2–w10 per S-WT; F16's worktree-each arm measured **0.685**, ~31% unmitigated. **`spikes/results/s-wt.jsonl` records the configuration**: S-WT ran a cardless one-line `--system-prompt`, never combined with `--agent`, over n = 9 steady-state worktrees (METHOD-S §4b.2), so the two readings are two cells — static × worktree (w2–w10 = 1.000, n = 9; w1 = 0.807 cold) and default × worktree (F16 arm (d), 0.685, n = 9) — not a contradiction [measured, s-wt.jsonl; PIA-075, PIA-058]. The cost model prices those two cells from S-WT and F16, returns an explicit **`Unmeasured(cell)`** refusal naming the cell for every cell no spike ran — the card + `--system-prompt` × worktree cell among them (PIA-066) — and may never interpolate a cell from two separately-measured arms; a model that shipped either number for the other cell would misprice a parallel fan-out by roughly 31×. S-WT-REC is DONE (§10.5) [amended 2026-09-11].

#### The merge queue and the worktree lifecycle — the instruments, which §7.7 previously stated without

The four rules above were stated as policy with one criterion between them. The AC register's git-reconcile section (`acceptance-criteria.md` §6; **AC-189**–**AC-198**) owns them, and is cited here rather than restated:

| Rule | Criterion |
| --- | --- |
| Strictly sequential **within** a tree; **one worktree per antichain member** | **AC-189** |
| Worktree rows are keyed by **SHA, never by path** — the same keying rule §5.5 applies to every repo-referencing row | **AC-190** |
| The review clone is constructed against F17's object-store escape: `--depth 1`, no `alternates`, no remote to the parent | **AC-191**, **AC-192** (and §6.4.3's AC-292, which states the same shape from the review-gate side — the two areas must be reconciled to one instrument) |
| **No process survives** in the clone; `repro.command` is **executable inside** it; MCP is isolated there | **AC-193**, **AC-194**, **AC-195** |
| **The merge queue serialises**: the queue, not the agent, is the authority on what lands | **AC-196** |
| Admission is `GreenAt(sha)` against a declared basis — a green result at a different basis does not admit | **AC-197** |
| Nothing in the queue depends on a **surviving process** — F22's rule applied to the landing path | **AC-198** |

Mergify's figure (1.9% against 4.4%) is the *motivation* for having a queue at all; **AC-196**–**AC-198** are what make this one checkable, and the per-story quarantine rule above still has none.

**The documented worst case in the whole design.** `/compact` reads from cache when warm, but **after a break longer than the TTL it reprocesses the entire history uncached — the single most expensive documented call pattern**; `/rewind` truncates back to an already-cached prefix and is explicitly recommended over it for discarding a dead-end path [documented, 1P code.claude.com prompt-caching, fetched 2026-09-08]. Triggering `/compact` immediately after an idle human-gate wait is exactly that pattern, and ADR-0017 institutionalises idle human-gate waits. Phase 1 does not compact after a gate; it resumes from the durable mark and, where a session must be shortened, rewinds.

**Mid-graph placement is PROVISIONAL, not decided (ADR-0087; its cost clause superseded by ADR-0106).** Mid-graph resume was lossless across a real `taskkill /T /F` — 0 changed, 0 lost, digests identical [measured, F38; **n=3**]. Its cost against a stage gate is **UNMEASURED**: the sub-1× output-token ratios first quoted here (8,408 / 10,314 / 14,579) were the resumed step's `output_tokens` over F28's `cache_creation_input_tokens`; on the like counter the resumed dispatches created 84,960 / 49,468 / 46,961 cache tokens, 4.57× / 2.66× / 2.52× of 18,603, cross-fixture (E-0087-1). **All three runs sat inside F10's ~1 h bracket** (measured `gap_minutes` 0.9, 28.4, 29.1, notwithstanding the arm's `0-delay` label), **P8's 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three no-kill controls are unrun**, and there is no within-experiment baseline. "Cheaper than a stage gate" may not be stated at all, and placement is not derived from a cost model in either direction [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-080].

### 7.8 The cost table

All in-programme figures are [measured] and are quoted from the evidence brief.

| Unit | Cost | Wall clock | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Trivial haiku dispatch | $0.021–0.027 | — | F3 |
| E3 S-SILENT item | $0.45–0.64 | 97–424 s | brief §D |
| E3 CLEAN item | $1.06–4.89 | 317–2,055 s | brief §D |
| P7 graph run, `optional` | **$1.02** | — | F37 — **instrument confound:** this arm ran **wholly on `claude` 2.1.263** |
| P7 graph run, `recommended` (adds `adversary → policy`) | **$2.69** — **2.64×** the optional block | — | F37 — **instrument confound:** the CLI moved to **2.1.265 during P7**, catching exactly this arm's **r3 draw on all ten items** while `optional` ran wholly on 2.1.263. F37 §7 reports the per-repeat split and no effect is detectable; **the arms are nonetheless not instrument-matched**, and the 2.64× is quoted as scale under that confound |
| P7 graph run, `mandatory` | **No price is quoted, here or anywhere in §7.** The arm was dropped at **n=1**, and DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #7/#33 record that it priced an **unreachable configuration** — at `rigor = mandatory` with `has_repro` the engine selects `bisect`, which carries no block at all. Its single run's ratio is two denominators, reconciled: **4.4×** = $9.857 / $2.222 (issue-77 `recommended` r1, single run); **3.66×** = $9.857 / $2.693 (the `recommended` arm mean, n = 30) — neither quoted without its denominator (ADR-0106 clause 3) | — | F37, brief §E; DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #7, #33; ADR-0106 |
| P7 adversary dispatch | $1.68 | 676 s | F37 |
| **Execution-grade review (S-TYPED CLEAN), the Phase-1 review tier** | **median $3.03**; the typed contract cost **+74%** over the prose comparator ($112.25 against $64.61) | **median 1,071 s (~18 min)**, slowest **4,561 s** | F31 |
| Whole spike programme, per campaign | E3 $153.86 (arm a $97.39 against arm b $56.47 = **1.72×**) · E3-H $36.18 · F28 $18.85 · S-VAR $176.96 · S-TYPED $112.25 · P1 arm L $21.37 · **P1 arm D's scoring $0** — 140 truth cells scored offline at the pin (136 on 2026-09-09, all 140 after the key fix), model cost zero [measured, F39] · P7 $131.62 of a $200 cap · P8 **$3.01 of a $40 cap** · AFK session $156.00 of $300 | S-VAR 16.2 h for 60 draws | brief §D; F39 |

**The review tier's price is what selects it — on ADR-0015's authority, and this is NOT the ADR-0080 quality shorthand.** ADR-0015's stage weight picks the *review tier* — read-only advisory against execution-grade — and the fix lane ships execution-grade as an **authored ADR-0015 decision**, taken independently of P7 and not read off any figure in this table. The tier costs **+74% and a median of ~18 minutes** [measured, F31], and what it buys is what §6 establishes: findings whose evidence class is computed in Rust and re-executed before anything blocks. **What P7's per-run costs may not do is set a default rigor for a lane** — that is the BLOCKED row of §7.10 (ADR-0080), it is a different decision from the ADR-0015 tier assignment, and no figure in this table is used for it.

External comparators, quoted for orientation only and never as targets [documented]:

| Comparator | Figure | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cloudflare, *Orchestrating AI Code Review at scale* | Overall average **$1.19**; median **$0.98** — **the label is unreconciled**: the evidence brief calls it the *read-only tier* median, the 2026-09-08 re-fetch calls it a median *pooled across all tiers*; both readings are carried and the discrepancy is in the label, not the figure (§11) — full tier **$1.68**† | 131,246 review runs across **48,095 merge requests** over 30 days; "break glass" **288 times = 0.6% of merge requests** — the only public false-block comparator this pass found [1P/blog, blog.cloudflare.com/ai-code-review/, fetched 2026-09-08] |
| Anthropic Code Review | $15–25 | Verification-heavy tier [documented] |
| Agentless against CodeR | **$0.70/instance at 32.00%** against **$3.34 at 28.33%** | Cheaper *and* better on that benchmark; a warning against pricing rigor by spend alone [documented] |

> **†Footnote, because pairing these two figures unfootnoted invites a wrong reading.** **$1.68 is the full tier's MEAN, not its median. The full tier's median is $1.47**, and **$0.98 is a median pooled across all tiers**. Reading $1.68 as a median against $0.98 overstates the tier's premium. Everything else in the Cloudflare set re-verified verbatim on the 2026-09-08 fetch [1P/blog; research pass §1 row 13, §4 rec 14].

**Wall clock is the binding constraint and the timeouts must be sized from measurement, not from the fast condition.** SM-117 requires every activity kind's configured timeout to sit above its measured p90. This has already failed once: a **900 s default written for the fast condition cost a CLEAN record** before being raised to 2,400 → 3,600 with one 5,400 s retry; and a `confirm`-step defect ran the **whole suite for 26 of 68 invocations** where `npx vitest run <file>` is 1 file / 6 tests / **15.6 s** — a ~60× difference [measured, brief §D]. The full verification gate today has **no affected-area selection**: every run is the whole 236-file suite, ~250 s to >900 s over 1,890–1,927 tests [measured].

### 7.9 Decisions in this section

| Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why | Record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| State the cache mitigation as **two rules on two axes**, never one; declare the axis per card | One global cache rule; pick the static prompt and claim the default prompt's durability | The two mitigations are in opposite directions and the cross-axis inference is falsified, not merely unsupported | **ADR-0078** (i); AC-383, AC-384 |
| Set **both** TTL variables; report a run as `TTL-unverified` absent per-run `ephemeral_*` evidence | Set only `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`; hold the TTL clause at `proposed` until S-TTL1 runs | Every dispatch here is subagent/workflow traffic on a 5 m default; setting both is free and reversible, while the *claim* needs evidence the rig may not be able to produce | **ADR-0078** (ii), (iv) |
| Restate the scope as workspace/org/machine+directory+git-status; retire "org-scoped" | Restate as "workspace-scoped" and stop | A wrong scope licenses a wrong mitigation (coordinating account-wide upgrades), which is worse than no claim | **ADR-0078** (iii); AC-385 |
| Batch-delta headroom over a named dispatch set with `n`, in integer ticks, with `resolution_limited`; **no per-run accessor exists**; `low_sample` below n = 10; `not attributable` on a shared account | Report per-run headroom; interpolate below the tick; compare `utilization` as an `f64` | `utilization` is quantised to 0.01 and a per-draw figure read off a small sample was already wrong by 7.8×; a naive `f64` mis-flags 80 of 100 one-tick deltas (PIA-017) | **ADR-0110** (the rule's home — ADR-0003 decides the five ports, not this, PIA-082); AC-372, AC-373, AC-374, AC-395; F6, F29 |
| Throttle on the visible `five_hour`/`seven_day` windows with ≥2-tick margins; never synthesise a family value | Derive a per-family estimate; throttle on a fixed dispatch rate from day one | The binding sub-limit has no key in the stream the adapter reads; a synthesised figure is unfalsifiable, and SM-112's kill is what tells us the proxy failed | **ADR-0110**; AC-375, AC-376, AC-377; F1 |
| Cache is a latency/cost lever; **no cache metric feeds a headroom figure** | Credit cache reads against the window | The question is **unanswerable** at 0.01 resolution — E0's gate was struck, not answered | **ADR-0110** decision 4 (ADR-0016 for the cache side); AC-381; F14, F6 |
| Sequential within a tree; parallel across antichains, one worktree each, serialised at a merge queue | Parallel decomposition within an item; agents merging their own work | Sequential decomposition loses 39–70% of the benefit; the measured main-breakage reduction comes from the queue, not the agent | ADR-0003, ADR-0018; arXiv 2512.08296, Mergify 2026 |
| Cost model prices the static × worktree cell from S-WT and the default × worktree cell from F16, and returns **`Unmeasured(cell)`** for every cell no spike ran | Hard-code 0.685 (~31%) for every cell; hard-code ~0 from S-WT for every cell | The two readings are two cells (s-wt.jsonl: a cardless static prompt, n = 9), and shipping either for the other misprices fan-out by ~31×; the card + `--system-prompt` cell is unmeasured | **AC-387** — the evidence contest is resolved by the run record (S-WT-REC DONE; PIA-075) |

| Item | State | Named spike / consumer | Hedge held meanwhile |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Do the `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` fields exist on this instrument? | **ANSWERED — present**, as the nested `cache_creation` object on 954/954 recorded runs (PIA-067; **ADR-0113** reads the tier from it per run); what remains is the billing-mode confound and the bucket-two observation | **S-TTL1**, narrowed → the cost model, SM-113, every cache figure in §7 (ADR-0078 iv) | Both variables set; a run reported 1 h only with its per-run `ttl_split` evidence, otherwise **`TTL-unverified`**; billing mode recorded as a declared confound on every cache measurement |

| What does an overage envelope look like? | **No payload ever recorded**; no baseline obtainable from this programme | A recorded overage envelope → AC-378's detection half, SM-112 | Only the retry-policy half asserted (`Overage` → zero attempts); the adapter test runs against a synthetic fixture, and absence of a report is not reported as a measured zero |
| The static prompt's cost across a **24-hour** gap | **Never taken** (S-H1b); bounded below by 29,619 and unbounded above; the 2026-09-06 mark is spent | **S-H1b-24H** — a fresh mark plus a read ≥ 24 h later with no other dispatch on the machine between (METHOD-S §7) → AC-384's lint figures, SM-113's time-axis cell | The time-axis cell is reported as measured at ~66 min only |
| A default rigor for a lane, priced from P7's per-run costs | **BLOCKED** (ADR-0080) — P7's primary criterion is **powerless at n=3**, and n = 10 per cell is powerless too (ADR-0111) | **S-P7B** under a changed criterion (paired per item, or a pooled-difference interval at a stated MDE) plus a settled severity vocabulary *(2026-09-26, ADR-0168 proposed: the severity vocabulary is settled as `blocking \| advisory`; no admissible severity threshold T exists on this evidence, and ADR-0080 decision 2 bullet 2 governs)* → §7.5, SM-110 | P7's figures are used as **scale only**; each quality block is authored explicitly per activity; the mandatory arm's **3.66× (arm mean, n = 30) / 4.4× (issue-77 r1)** ratio is reconciled by denominator and stays n = 1 on the `mandatory` side |

The full register, with costs and a "gates Phase 1?" column, is §10. Every row above now appears there — including the S-WT run-record recovery (**S-WT-REC**), which had a consumer but no register row until this revision, and the lane-default row, whose authority is ADR-0080's block and **not** the ADR-0015 tier decision §7.8 takes.
