## 8. CLI, projections, migration and distribution

### 8.1 The command surface

`rk-cli` is a `clap` router over the kernel. Every verb is a read of the world, a decision, and an exit; none of them waits
(§6; brief §C.6 — nothing may depend on a process surviving, F22 [measured, operational, n≈12 kills against one 12,000 s
survivor, cause unestablished]).

| Verb | Owns | Phase | Pinned by | Evidence / source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `run <item> [--lane fix\|story]` | the one idempotent entry point; no `--resume`; a run on a complete item is a no-op that says so; `--lane` is optional and derived from the item kind when derivable, `fix` → `bug-standard`, `story` → `story` *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 D-02: this cell read `run <item>` while the denylist paragraph below read `run --lane fix --item <item>` — ADR-0107 decisions 1 and 4 disagreed; **ADR-0128** (proposed, successor-in-part) fixes this one grammar)* | 1 | AC-400, AC-401, AC-402, AC-403, AC-404 | proposal §4.5 (L2.3); **ADR-0107** [documented] — the rule's home; **ADR-0128** for the grammar; ADR-0011 decides packs and never contained it (PIA-082) |
| `answer <item> <option> [--attach <kind>=<path>] --explain-sufficed <yes\|no> --toml-opened <yes\|no>` | the only Phase-1 gate channel; the option is validated against the gate question's ADR-0063 registry entry (default question: `approve` / `changes-requested`), the token is CLI-resolved from the open gate record (AC-248), and `--explain-sufficed` / `--toml-opened` are AC-435's required field (two data, one member — corrected 2026-09-19) *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 D-04/D-05: this cell read `answer <item> approve\|changes-requested`, ADR-0009's two literals; **ADR-0129** (proposed, successor-in-part to ADR-0009) widens it — the registry **type** is Step 7d's with the default question built in, pack-declared questions are 7e's)* | 1 | AC-405, AC-406, AC-407, AC-410, AC-435 | ADR-0009, ADR-0017, **ADR-0129**, ADR-0063 [documented] |
| `status` | roll-up read; **read-only, so it does not reconcile** (ADR-0105 clause 8; AC-172 — this row read "acts on an item, so it reconciles" until 2026-09-12) | 1 | AC-172 | proposal §4.5 [documented] |
| `explain <item>` | why this DAG, from the record | 1 | AC-430–AC-435 | ADR-0024, ADR-0047 [documented] |
| `doctor` / `doctor --rebuild` | the configuration-time rule table; store rebuild from git | 1 | AC-414–AC-429 | ADR-0014, proposal §4.2.1, §4.5 rule 3 [documented] |
| `catalog search` | keyword search over an engine-ignored field | 1 | AC-436 | ADR-0030 rule 5, ADR-0027 [documented] |
| `init` | idempotent repository initialisation — a second `init` changes nothing and exits 0 | 1 | AC-437 | **ADR-0107** decision 2 [documented] |
| `doctor --probe` | refresh a dated capability claim | — | AC-491 | **`[inferred]`** — a design consequence of the capability-shelf-life lesson, recorded in no ADR. It is an unnamed verb until one exists |
| `doctor --fix` | install repair | — | AC-514, AC-516 | **CONDITIONAL** — see §8.8; no install mechanism is decided |
| `redkiln-migrate` | **a separate binary**, not a verb | 1 (v0) | AC-441 | ADR-0002; proposal §6 [documented] |

`redkiln run` refuses a resume-shaped flag or verb by a **committed name denylist** — `resume`, `continue`, `stay`, `--stay`, `--resume`, `--continue`, `--from`, `--pick-up` (ADR-0107 decision 3), with the `--retry-step`, `--from-step` and `--again` names this document first listed kept in the lint as further entries — checked against a snapshot of the whole `clap` argument surface (AC-400); that `--resume` is refused is itself AC-400's behavioural check, and the runbook's Phase-1 exit check (3) re-invokes `redkiln run <item> --lane fix` after a `taskkill /T /F`, reading the run id back from the event log (ADR-0107 decision 4; PIA-030) *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 D-02: this read `redkiln run --lane fix --item <item>`; the grammar is ADR-0128's `run <item> [--lane fix|story]` — positional item, no `--item` flag, and `--item` is not on the denylist because the snapshot pins its absence)*. The residual is stated
rather than hidden: the lint is name-based, so a resume flag under an unlisted name is caught only by human review of the
snapshot diff. F38's losslessness at n=3 does not license the stronger claim that no resume entry point is ever needed
[measured/PARTIAL, F38 — 3 of a pre-registered 27+3, its 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three controls unrun].

**Exit codes are a closed, snapshot-pinned table** (AC-438): success, configuration error — which includes any failing
error-severity `doctor` rule — findings-block and each runtime error class are distinct values, and a new
`StepError` variant with no mapping fails to compile. *(Amended 2026-09-18, DECISIONS-2026-09-18 / C-01 D-01 — operator
decision 2026-09-18: **gate-open is removed from the distinct list**, which read "… `doctor` rule — gate-open,
findings-block …". A `run` that opens a gate is `run`'s designed success outcome — `Gate:` trailer and `gate_opened`
written, state printed — and **exits 0** (AC-403, AC-240, AC-024; AC-438 amended the same day); the closed table has no
gate row. The table's values — 0 Success, 2 Usage, 3 ConfigError, 4 FindingsBlock, 5 NoRecord, 10–20 per `StepError`
variant, 70 PoisonedLockHalt (ADR-0118 clause 6), 130 SignalOutsideDispatch — are a Step 7d header-table row and the
committed snapshot's, per AC-438; the design restates and does not own them.)* **The exit code is not evidence of success**: a dispatch that exits 0
with `subtype: success` and no `structured_output` under a passed schema resolves to `StepError::Schema` (AC-439)
[measured, F20 — the one recorded fixture]. The sibling rule for `is_error`/`api_error_status` frames is **AC-428**: those strings appear nowhere in the condensed
brief, but `spikes/results/e3.jsonl` holds seven such rows (slugs fix-doctor-git-config-repair, issue-122 §2, issue-130,
issue-131, issue-72 for 529; issue-82 for 500), so the fixture exists and "may not exist" is withdrawn [measured, F21 and
e3.jsonl; PIA-075]. AC-218 in the harness area covers the same behaviour and the register's §0.3 names the pair's owner.

### 8.2 `run` — idempotence, reconciliation and no waiting

| Rule | What it forbids | Instrument | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One entry point, no resume flag | any "pick up where you left off" verb or flag | argument-surface snapshot + denylist lint (AC-400) | **ADR-0107** [documented] — F19's 174/174 rests on every entry reconstructing from git, F22 forbids depending on a process surviving |
| A committed memoised step is never re-dispatched | paying twice for the same content-addressed step | kernel test over a fake `Harness` port counting dispatches (AC-401) | **no spike has measured this.** F38 measured resume losslessness, and the brief records P8 §5.4(3), the second-arrival question, as **circular by construction** — arrivals were forced and the mark was minted by the driver that implements the semantics under test [F38] |
| `reconcile(recorded, world)` before any decision, on every entry | a `--no-reconcile` / `--skip-reconcile` / `--fast` flag | four-path kernel test + the same snapshot (AC-402) | drift 12/174 = 7% overall, 3/95 = 3% at ≤1 h vs 9/79 = 11% at >1 h [measured, F19, one operator, one machine]; 45% of gate→verdict intervals (n=174) and 48% of stage→verdict intervals (n=191) exceed 1 h [measured, F18] |
| Opening a gate writes the mark and exits 0 | any prompt, socket, sleep or blocking stdin read outside `init` | integration test under a fake clock + a lint (AC-403) | F22 [measured, operational]; ADR-0009, ADR-0017 |
| A rate-limited dispatch writes a retry mark and exits | any in-process sleep or timer | retry-policy unit test that fails if the fake clock advances (AC-404) | brief §C.6 [measured]. **The retry time's source is fixed by rule and unverified by measurement**: `RateLimited { resets_at }` takes `resets_at` from the last `rate_limit_event` in the same stream, else the pack delay (AC-523; ADR-0095) — F1 exposes `unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}.utilization` and `resetsAt`, no family sub-limit key, and F6 shows `utilization` quantised to 0.01. Named as S-RETRY's consumer in §10 |

**AC-402's cut half is disclosed, not quietly kept:** the snapshot covers flags, not configuration keys, so "no
configuration key can skip reconcile" was withdrawn rather than left uninstrumented.

The reconcile rule is where §5's store meets the CLI; the store's tier split and the two trailer axes are §5's and are not
restated here.

### 8.3 `answer` — the only channel in Phase 1

The gate *mechanism* — `(token, tree_hash, deadline)`, the void rule, the terminal expiry path and the read-site
re-validation — is §6's. What §8 owns is that in Phase 1 there is exactly **one** way in:

- the Phase-1 dependency closure contains no tracker or web crate, and the gate-answer store method has **exactly one call
  site**, in `rk-cli`'s `answer` command (AC-405; ADR-0009, ADR-0011) [documented];
- a token presented twice is refused and no second `gate_answered` event is written (AC-407);
- an attachment no declared option admits is refused **at the CLI boundary**, and nothing is written (AC-410; ADR-0063).
  The failure shape this guards against is measured: P9b's lint accepted 5 guesses silently [measured, F33];
- a graph binding a downstream step to a gate step's `outputs` is a configuration error — a gate populates `answer` and
  binds nothing downstream (AC-409; ADR-0045, ADR-0038).

The void rate is tracked by **SM-125** against the measured drift band, per duration bucket, with its Wilson intervals
carried: 12/174 = 6.9% overall [4.0–11.7%], 3/95 = 3.2% at ≤1 h [1.1–8.9%], 9/79 = 11.4% at >1 h [6.1–20.3%] [measured,
F19, one operator, one machine]. **The 3.6× ratio is a point estimate whose two intervals overlap — it is not separated at
this n**, and the measure says so.

### 8.4 `doctor` — one rule table, all configuration-time, zero model cost

Every rule below is data with a stable id, evaluated without a dispatch. The zero-cost claim is bound to a corpus so it
cannot be earned by a `doctor` that checks nothing: the integration test injects a `Harness` port fake that fails on any
dispatch, **over the AC-415 rejecting-fixture corpus** (AC-414). CI joins rule ids to fixture directories and fails the
build on any rule with no fixture that it actually rejects (AC-415).

**Two areas own a doctor rule table, and this one reconciles only the cli-doctor half.** The rows below are built from the AC register's cli-doctor section (`acceptance-criteria.md` §12; **AC-409, AC-412–AC-429** — corrected 2026-09-18, C-01: this read "§11; AC-414–AC-429", while the section is §12 and the table below already cites AC-412 and AC-413, and AC-409's gate-`outputs` rejection is a `doctor` row too). The register's packs-and-catalog section (`acceptance-criteria.md` §4) carries a **second** doctor rule table — **AC-092–AC-099** — whose rows are configuration-time and zero-dispatch at the *pack* level: **AC-095** is F20's `StructuredOutput` rule stated pack-side, **AC-097** the timeout declaration, **AC-098** the all-rules-are-configuration-time-and-zero-dispatch invariant this section's cost claim depends on, and **AC-099** the span requirement. Several are near-duplicates of rows below and **the two tables are not reconciled**; until they are, every row here names the cli-doctor criterion and the pack-side twin is cited but not restated. Also uncited by any section and owed a home: **AC-109** (boolean-facet tagging is a lint error, ADR-0060) and **AC-111**/**AC-117** (a shipped activity's coherence edit bumps its `version` and enters the changelog, ADR-0072 — now cited in §4.5; the activity `version` is not the artifact-type `schema_version`, PIA-036).

| Rule | Severity | Pinned by | Evidence for the rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Every role resolves to exactly one card per configured toolchain; zero or two-at-equal-specificity is an error | error | AC-416 | ADR-0014, ADR-0024 [documented]. For the five toolchain-keyed kinds the specificity tie-break is withdrawn and a keyed kind expands per toolchain element (ADR-0073) |
| A card's `fulfils`, a skill's `attach.roles` or a slot filler naming an undeclared role/slot is a dangling reference | error | AC-420 | ADR-0042; the failure shape is measured — the lint accepted 5 guesses silently [F33] |
| A role, skill or slot referenced by no enabled pack, or a declared slot neither filled nor marked optional, is dead | error | AC-421 | dead activities 15/101 → **6/102 = 5.9%** after decisions 1–10; **the pre-registered reading over 8 packs measured 20.8% and fired the >20% kill by 0.8 points** [measured, F35 — both readings reported] |
| A card whose role demands a schema and whose `tools` omits `StructuredOutput` | error | AC-417 | `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success`; adding `StructuredOutput` fixes it [measured, F20]. **ADR-0108** (the rule's home; ADR-0016 never contained it, PIA-082) |
| Any card or slot filler setting `model` or `effort` (tier lives on the role) | error | AC-418 | reasoning effort is applied per dispatch — thinking tokens low **0.0** / high **4.8** / xhigh **221.0** — **but low vs high did NOT separate on output** [measured, F15] |
| An activity declaring no `timeout_secs`, or a non-numeric one | error | AC-419 | the timeout history: a 900 s default written for the fast condition cost a CLEAN record, escalated 2,400 → 3,600 → one 5,400 s retry; S-TYPED's slowest CLEAN review was 4,561 s [measured, brief §D] |
| A facet outside `facets.toml`, a value outside its enumeration, or a technology pack adding a new facet | error | AC-424 | ADR-0030 rule 1, ADR-0061; the lattice figures that make the vocabulary checkable are F35, whose pre-registered reading fired the >5% kill twice (23.5%, 11.4%) before clearing at 0.3% |
| A facet with no registered computed fact, or a computed fact used in a `require` row with no registry entry; **a facet declaring `source = "derived"` with no named `deriver` or no scored precision record for it; a computed fact whose only computation is a lexical scan of free text** | error | AC-425 | `external_dep` collapsed to a constant, **200/200 false** [measured, F36]; `recent_deploy` underivable at this pin, `binary_change` measured but unregistered, `dynamism` pre-registered with no registry entry [brief §E, contested]. **The three new clauses are ADR-0089's**, forced by F39: `auth` **0.00** (0/12 [0.00, 0.24] after the key fix) and `flag_exists` **0.00** [0.00, 0.66] were both registered computed facts and both carry no signal, so "a computed fact" was satisfiable by any regex [measured, F39]. Under ADR-0093 the rule reads the closed `source` set `human \| llm \| derived \| kernel \| declared` — `kernel` facets carry no `deriver` and no record, `declared` facets may not key a `require` row, and "no scored record" means no record that exists, is pinned and is read (a NOT MEASURABLE record counts). **This rule is the mechanical face of both §0.1 selection-engine blocks** |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` present in the captured environment map the adapter receives as a value, or `--bare` in a composed argument vector; the environment is inherit-minus-a-committed-denylist (`rk-adapter-claude/src/env_denylist.rs`), and the `doctor` row and the construction test read the same list | error | AC-422 (compound, disclosed), AC-488; AC-511 is ports-dist's per the register's §0.4 (PIA-036) | ADR-0010 [documented — the flag, OAuth and the variable are **[outside the brief]**]; DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #16 |
| Installed CLI version, or `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL` / `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`, differ from the pin — **both** variables are required and a one-set configuration is a red fixture (**AC-526**; PIA-029, PIA-058) | error | AC-423, AC-526 | **The pin's value is `claude` 2.1.263**, set by DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #1, which re-pinned from 2.1.259 after the instrument moved mid-programme; `TESTED_CLI_VERSION` (§9.6) and AC-385 carry the same value. **It is a floor-and-record pin, and the programme has already broken its own lineage twice**: 2.1.259 → 2.1.263, then → 2.1.265 partway through P7, so P7's arms are not instrument-matched (§1.2, §7.8). ADR-0016, **ADR-0078**; TTL behaviour is date-stamped at n=1 per gap [F10], which is itself the reason to pin. See §7 — **no run may be reported as 1 h without per-run `ephemeral_*` field evidence**; the nested `cache_creation` object carrying both fields is present on 954/954 recorded runs, so the evidence is the `ttl_split` field, and F3's "absent" is re-read as flat naming absent [PIA-067]. The latest measured instrument is 2.1.268 (2026-09-10) |
| A store opened against a different repository (root commit + common dir, schema version — *boot-id dropped 2026-09-18, C-01 RA-M-07: `rk-store::identity` cut it "for having no source", `identity.rs:13-15`; AC-369; AC-413 amended the same day*) | error | AC-413 | proposal §4.5 rule 5 **[outside the brief]** |
| Any redkiln-authored hook in `.git/hooks` | error | AC-412 | proposal §4.5 rule 4 **[outside the brief]** |
| A capability row missing `capability` / `verified_on` / `probe`; a row older than the declared maximum claim age | error / stale | AC-489, AC-490 | shelf life "weeks" [documented, shipmates L5.7 — one project] |
| A non-Claude adapter bound to a gate-feeding activity with no recorded E3-shaped validation | error | AC-496 — **PROVISIONAL on S-CODEX (registered, unrun)** | ADR-0007 [documented] |
| An adapter named in config but not compiled in, or missing required configuration | error | AC-486 | proposal §4.1 [documented] |
| A capability mapping resolving to an empty or absent tool list | error | AC-493 | **fails open** — Claude Code inherits every tool when `tools:` is absent [documented, shipmates L4.4]. This is the *opposite* direction from F20's populated-but-incomplete allowlist, which fails closed and silent |
| **A card with no `tools:` key** — the pre-spawn effective tool set is the card's declared list and nothing else; absence is not "inherit everything" | error | the harness-area rule (ADR-0108 decision 2; DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #16) — a doctor row added 2026-09-11, PIA-060 | shipmates L4.4 is the documented fail-open direction the rule closes [documented] |
| `worktree remove` left an orphan (the Windows handle case); the store's common dir is on a network filesystem; a credential-shaped value in any retained dispatch field | error (orphans, network FS) / error (credential) | ADR-0105 clauses 6 and 7 — rows added 2026-09-11, PIA-040 | Halt 6's surviving handle [documented, `spikes/HANDOFF.md`]; WAL is unsupported over a network filesystem [documented, sqlite.org]; argv and env are retained as digests only, the child environment never |
| A stage artifact with zero downstream `artifact.read` events over the last `unread_window_runs` runs | **warning**; nothing deleted or rewritten | AC-426, AC-472 | **no measurement supports the window value or the hazard rate.** Design intent with a working instrument. **N = 50** — the required per-repo key `unread_window_runs`, which `init` writes (DECISIONS-2026-09-12 E9), read by this one row *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 D-20: this cell read "`N` is undeclared, so SM-139's target cannot be missed today, and both places say so"; N was declared 2026-09-12 and SM-139 / AC-472 are amended the same day)* |
| Every rule id has at least one committed fixture it actually rejects | build failure | AC-415 | F20 is the measured precedent for a check that silently stopped checking [measured] |

**Cost:** 0 dispatches, $0 (**SM-121**). Every comparable configuration-time analysis in the programme measured $0 — F24's
six-rejection adjudication, F25's AC-015 probe, F32/F33, F34's 90 activities, F35's 27 s lattice over 102 activities
[measured, brief §D]. **This metric saturates at 0 by design and has no discriminating power**; it is a tripwire, not a
signal, kept because F20 is the measured precedent for a rule that quietly stops checking. Wall clock (**SM-122**) has
**no baseline** — the nearest comparators are the 27 s lattice [F35, n=1 run] and the 323 s median mutant run [F11] — with
a ≤60 s target over the 102-activity v0 catalog and a >120 s kill, because a `doctor` that costs minutes gets skipped and a
skipped configuration-time rule is a runtime failure deferred. **SM-123** tracks yield per rule id, never table-wide: the
lint's first run caught **13 real errors against 34 false alarms** [measured, F32; the corpus size — **10 authored activities plus 3 registries** — is in `spikes/F32-DRAFT.md`, not in the brief's F32 row, and is cited from the draft directly], falling to **0.11**
first-run errors per file for a third author against P9b's 1.3 and P9's 4.7 [measured, F34, 90 files] — **three authors,
two of them models in the same session lineage**. A rule with zero catches across three consecutive releases is deleted.

`doctor --rebuild` reconstructs the store from git alone and CI compares the computed next action for every item in the
pinned corpus before and after (AC-427); it writes no git object, ref or working-tree file — reconciliation is git → store,
never the reverse (AC-429). **The limit travels with it:** F19 proves state *carriage* (174/174 lossless), not "same next
action" — **the FSM was never replayed** [brief §C.4] — so **SM-124** starts from no baseline and reports divergent item
ids, not a rate.

### 8.5 `explain` — both row families, printed from the record

ADR-0024 puts admissibility in the activity and requirement in the pack, and records its own cost: understanding why an
activity ran means reading two artifacts. `explain` exists to pay that cost once, in code.

| What it prints | Pinned by | Why it is this and not something else |
| --- | --- | --- |
| For every included activity, **both** row families: the activity's `provides`, `requires`, `applicable_if`, `method_rank`, `min_rigor`, **and** the pack's `baseline` plus each matching `require` row | AC-430 | ADR-0024's two homes. The snapshot test fails if either family is empty while the other is populated |
| The resolution: card, attached skills, inserted slot fillers, effective tier | AC-431 | `--agents` is **inert without `--agent`** and the card's model+effort were accepted regardless, so resolution is not inspectable from outside the record [measured, F7] |
| For an excluded-but-admissible activity, the **specific clause** — the false `applicable_if` sub-predicate, the unmet `min_rigor`, or the absent `require` row — never "not selected" | AC-433 | ADR-0033's closed predicate grammar makes the clause nameable |
| Every `unknown` facet rendered **as `unknown`**, with its position-derived treatment stated, never as a measured band value | AC-434 | `unknown` rates: novelty **46.0%**, size **39.5%**, blast_radius **31.0%**, criticality **27.0%**; `unknown` is treated as `high` by position, so an axis oscillating unknown↔low moves `R` the full band width [measured, F36; brief §C.9] |

**`explain` never recomputes.** It renders the recorded resolution only, and an item with no recorded resolution is an
error rather than a recomputation (AC-432; ADR-0036) — verified by a replay assertion that `explain` over a replayed event
log is byte-identical to `explain` at run time, plus a kernel test that the selection engine is unreachable from the
`explain` code path. The reason is measured and is the §0.1 block showing up at the display surface: **recomputing
selection returned the same modal `R` on only 5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20 **items**, each computed across all ten draws of
that item, against an 80% line** [measured, F36; 400
dispatches, 0 void, $21.37], so a recomputation at explain time would print something that never ran. **AC-434 is scoped to
display and nothing more.** No criterion here asserts the displayed vector is *stable*; that criterion is not writable
until P1's kill is retired by a re-run whose modal identity reaches **0.80**. Arm D's precision/recall **is** now computed
(F39), but it discharges AC-071's fourth clause and no other, and it measures the *derived* vector rather than the
LLM-emitted one this section renders (ADR-0079, status `superseded` — ADR-0090; corrected 2026-09-15, this read *"status `proposed`"* — see §4.1; ADR-0089 for the derived half) — see §0.1 and §10.
Nothing in this section resolves either block.

**Sufficiency is a human gate, not a machine check** (AC-435). Whether the printed fields answer "why did this run" without
the reviewer opening a TOML is a legibility judgement; the machine-checkable half is that the gate answer cannot be
submitted with the field absent. **SM-126** pre-registers the line so it can fire: the first 30 gate answers set the
baseline as raw counts, and **more than 15 of the first 30 reporting that a TOML was opened anyway** kills the two-home
split's cost/benefit case and reopens ADR-0024, as a named spike rather than a prose target (ADR-0012).

`catalog search` searches an **engine-ignored** `keywords` field: permuting `keywords` across the shipped catalog must
leave the selected activity set byte-identical, and a lint asserts `keywords` is unreachable from the admissibility
evaluator (AC-436; ADR-0030 rule 5). `init` is idempotent — a second run leaves an empty `git status --porcelain` and
unchanged store identity rows (AC-437). **Its namespacing half was cut, not weakened:** the prefix was undecided and the
"naming lint" unspecified. **The prefix is now decided — `rk-`** (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #8; the crate prefix and the AC
register's label prefix), so AC-506 carries the literal and a lint regex, and AC-437's namespacing clause returns as its own
criterion — **AC-531** (register §12.9, beside AC-437; see §8.8).

### 8.6 Projections — derived, never authoritative

Four projections, one rule: **the event log and git are the source; a projection is a rendering with at most one write, and
inbound text is untrusted and typed at the boundary** (proposal §4.8 [documented]; ADR-0005/ADR-0006's "nothing parses
structure out of prose" is §5's and applies unchanged here).

| Projection | Adapter | Phase | Write direction | The rule that keeps it derived |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Tracker** | `rk-adapter-github` (Linear as a second adapter over the same port, Phase 4) | 3 (ADR-0009, ADR-0011) | outbound per-field dirty-delta, **idempotency key on every write** | A human editing an orchestrator-only label (`rk:stage/*`, `rk:gate/*`) produces a typed `Drift` record and **no state transition**; item state remains what the event log says (AC-507). Inbound comment grammar is untrusted text parsed into a typed command through the port's inbox, never into structure. Replay issues no second external write (AC-508) — the failure shape is documented: one orchestrator posted the same report three times [documented, shipmates L5.4] |
| **Docs site** | `rk-adapter-mdbook` (Astro/Starlight, rustdoc render the same typed `DocSet`) | 3 | outbound; agents propose, CI + CODEOWNERS gate | Generator-agnostic invariants (build, links, drift-on-source-change) live in the kernel and are exercised against the no-op recorder fake with **a negative case per invariant**, so a recorder that answers yes to everything cannot satisfy a kernel that checks nothing (AC-510) |
| **Web view** | — | 3 | read-mostly, **exactly one write**: answer a gate | Built after CLI status and the tracker projection; the gate answer lands in the same `reconcile()` as the CLI's (§6) |
| **`.kb`** | — | ongoing | intake → adjudicate → integrate | Atoms are typed artifacts injected per activity by declared inclusion rules and **included in the activity cache key** (§7). **A named limit on "declared inclusion rules", acted on rather than assumed away:** Kiro-style `fileMatch` conditional inclusion **does not work for globally-scoped steering files** — it is workspace-scoped and undocumented as such [documented, research pass §1 contradiction #12, severity low–medium]. Cross-initiative atoms are the lattice's likely shape, so redkiln's inclusion rules may **not** be modelled on `fileMatch`: an atom whose scope crosses initiatives is included by an **explicit loader entry**, and the loader's resolved atom set — not the rule that produced it — is what enters the memo key (§3.5). A rule form that resolves differently by workspace would make the memo key non-reproducible, which §4.4's reproducibility contract forbids outright; ADRs are immutable — supersede, never edit. That immutability is **redkiln's chosen convention, not an inherited industry standard**: MADR itself permits in-place status transitions [1P adr.github.io/madr, fetched 2026-09-08; research pass §4 rec. 19] |

Two constraints cross every projection. **Agents hold no tracker credential**, and the assertion is made from the child's
own reported environment, not from the parent's spawn arguments (AC-509) — because an ignored flag looks identical to an
honoured one and a default `claude -p` inherits the operator's whole environment [measured, F8; brief §C.11; the figures
and the isolation-as-measurement-precondition rule are §7.4's]. And **the orchestrator never writes to the user's
checkout**: any commit, file write or index change is refused unless its target is a run-owned worktree or the store under
the resolved common dir (AC-518; ADR-0018), asserted by `git status --porcelain` empty, `git diff-index --quiet HEAD` and
an unmoved HEAD. The "index mtime unchanged" witness was struck as unsound — git refreshes the stat cache on ordinary
reads.

**One projection-adjacent question is blocked and is not routed around:** one store per repository lives at
`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ and is shared by every worktree, and gitoxide's own `crate-status.md` states the
`GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay **is not implemented** [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched
2026-09-08]. The hybrid ships meanwhile (ADR-0077, as ADR-0098 restates it); S-GIX/a is the go/no-go for the read, with the wrapper's `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` as the decided no-go fallback. Details are §5's.

### 8.7 Migration — `redkiln-migrate`, one commit, one differential report

`redkiln-migrate` is a **separate binary**. No `redkiln` subcommand performs or triggers a migration, and no `rk-*` crate
depends on the migrate crate (AC-441). A store opened against an old-layout tree refuses with a typed unsupported-layout
error naming the tool, and the fixture tree's digest is unchanged afterwards (AC-440) — narrowed on refutation to what a
read-only mount plus a digest check actually decides, which is *writing*, not *reading*.

**What crosses, and as what** (proposal §5, checked against the brief; ADR-0002):

| From the old redkiln | Crosses as | Never as | Instrument, and its real bound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `.bklg` / `.kb` content | input to `redkiln-migrate`, emitting the new typed layout | a runtime compatibility path | AC-440, AC-442 |
| Process packs (`.redkiln/processes/*.yaml`) | **content** for the new TOML process packs | code | AC-442's provenance lint — **syntactic**: it matches enumerated legacy path globs and the literals `template_sig`/`rendered_sig`, so it bounds accidental drift, not a determined one |
| **101** CLI-behaviour test files | **black-box specifications** where the behaviour is wanted | ported tests | AC-445 — content hash plus a declared normalisation (whitespace collapsed, comments and imports stripped). **It does not catch a test re-expressed in Rust idiom, which is the realistic way a behaviour gets ported**, and it is a pure absence check that passes on a workspace with no tests at all. Its positive counterpart is AC-446 |
| Recorded bugs (lock/id collisions, `--stay`, RK-B0013, the `basis` flip) | **test cases** in the new crates | fixes carried over | AC-446 — each bug id resolves to a test that fails under **a mutation reintroducing that bug's mechanism**, not under guard deletion. F25 measured the difference: removing step 7 turned the suite RED, inverting the ordering left **36 of 36 GREEN** [measured, F25, n=1 AC, one operator]. The source table's fifth entry, "F25's per-AC gap", is a method finding about this programme, not an old defect, and is excluded |
| `reconcile.rs` | **the one code import** — a re-implementation of the old tree's `src/store/verify.ts` (hash at the pin), built in the spike runner and exercised over F12's corpus, not validated against an oracle (PIA-078) | — | AC-443 — **the weakest real bound in this design.** A content-hash manifest catches a *verbatim* copy only; `reconcile.rs` itself crosses rewritten, so the one sanctioned import would not have tripped its own detector. It is a *declared*-import register with a verbatim tripwire, never a detector of imports. A second rewritten import is caught by review alone |
| The frozen corpus @ `47faec9f`, the E3 fixtures, the JSONL results, the spike runner | a separate `redkiln-corpus` repo, pinned by SHA | a permanent resident of the product repo | AC-465 — **currently violated by construction**: `spikes/corpus/`, `spikes/results/*.jsonl` and `spikes/runner/` are all tracked at HEAD `009a4ec` (2026-09-10) and `redkiln-corpus` does not exist; the 1,312-file corpus and the 118-ref history live only in `D:/repos/redkiln`, so the substrate is a `git bundle create --all` committed into `redkiln-corpus` and pinned by that repo's SHA (PIA-002). A future-state criterion, not a maintained invariant, and nothing measures the distance |

`reconcile()` itself replays against the F12 fixture — 360 trailer commits over 74 slices, **across all refs of the frozen
clone** (118 refs, of which 46 are reachable from `47faec9f`; the fixture is the bundle, and the ref set is asserted beside
the 40-hex pin, §5.7) — reproducing 15 of 74 ever-rejected and 74/74 slice states without manual annotation (AC-444)
[measured, F12; PIA-002]. **F12's registered primary was uncomputable at n=1**, so this replays the re-scoped measure, not
the original one. The tool refuses a target with a non-empty `git status --porcelain` before any write and writes into a
dedicated branch/worktree, so an interrupted run leaves the target branch untouched (**AC-522**; ADR-0100's area), and
AC-450's idempotence test gains a kill between write and commit.

**The run contract.** Exactly one commit on the target branch, or abort before writing (AC-447). A differential report in
that same commit carrying per-(kind, slot) counts of lifted / lifted-with-loss / failed and the corpus SHA, or the write is
refused (AC-448) — and the three counts stay three counts, because a report folding with-loss into lifted fails validation
(AC-468); "lifted" means the section parsed into the slot's shape, **not** that the migration was lossless. Determinism is
asserted across the swept nuisance parameters `TZ`, directory-read order and hash-map seed, and no further (AC-449); a
second run against an already-migrated tree is a no-op with an empty diff (AC-450). **Zero dispatches, permanently**: the
binary links no harness adapter and constructing a harness port inside the migration path fails at build time (AC-466).
Section accounting balances — every one of the corpus's records is lifted, lifted-with-loss, failed, opaque or
dropped-by-decision, or the run aborts rather than commits (AC-451, SM-134).

**The measured differential (S-MIG).** 1,312 files / **6,877** (artifact, section) records at corpus pin `47faec9f`, read
via `git show`, **zero model cost** [measured, S-MIG]. **S-MIG has no F-number** — the brief's ledger (F1–F38, F9 struck,
F30 unassigned) contains no entry for it, and §D records only that it cost $0; every figure below is cited as
`[measured, S-MIG]` with its own denominator. **The instrument was iterated four times against this corpus before the
recorded run**, so these are shares under a locator tuned to the corpus, not a blind parse, and every share is an upper
bound on what a fresh corpus would give (AC-467 records provenance; it does not fix the limit).

| Slot | Lifted / with-loss / failed | Share | Gate outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| spec frontmatter | 153/153 | 100% | **saturated** — tripwire only |
| anchors | 152/152 | 100% | **saturated** |
| review rubric | 38/38 | 100% | **saturated** |
| item-card frontmatter | 209/209 | 100% | **saturated** |
| spec sections | — | 98% (this slot only; the record-weighted typed-slot overall is 96.0% = 3,011/3,138, E-0075-1) | stands |
| ledger fenced YAML (n=110) | 108/0/2 | 98% | stands |
| kb atoms | — | 98% | stands |
| PR boundary · merge gate | — | 99% each | stands |
| acceptance criteria (n=153) | 134/19/0 | **88%** | REVIEW — the point estimate sits below the pre-registered "ACs lift ≥95%" line, and because falsifying a ≥95% line is an **interval** claim the interval is computed rather than declined: **Wilson 95% on 134/153 is 81.4–91.9%, which excludes 95%**, so the prediction is falsified as stated |
| impl-report TDD evidence (n=156) | 142/7/7 | 91% | stands |
| review coverage map | — | 91% | stands |
| **impl-report gates (n=156)** | 117/31/8 | **75%** | **RECLASSIFY** |
| **companion frontmatter (n=45, five of the eleven `companion.*` kinds)** | 19/6/20 | **42%** | **RECLASSIFY** — the census holds eleven companion kinds plus `ledger`; this row is the five-kind subset (E-0075-2) |
| **review gate table (n=23)** | 4/7/12 | **17%** | **RECLASSIFY** |

**Two Wilson intervals exist on these shares, and no others** — the acceptance-criteria row above (134/153, where an interval claim is being made and the interval is therefore stated, 81.4–91.9%) and SM-135's 142/156 on the impl-report TDD-evidence slot; an earlier revision claimed "exactly one" here and the register claimed a different one, and both are replaced by this sentence (PIA-107). Elsewhere no interval is computed, and the small-n slots (review gate table n=23, companions
n=45) carry intervals wide enough that the point estimates must not be compared against each other. Four of fifteen slots
are already at 100% and carry no trend information. The spec-section slot's 98% is exactly the number that hides the 17% — and the record-weighted typed-slot overall is **96.0%** (3,011/3,138; 98.3% only by counting LiftedWithLoss as lifted), not 98% (E-0075-1, PIA-078) — which is why
**SM-133** reports per slot and never as one percentage — and why its target carries a cross-instrument caveat: the
baseline came from `census-s-mig.py`, a read-only census, and the target is evaluated against `redkiln-migrate`, a
different program with its own lifters, so the first delta cannot distinguish a regression from instrument disagreement.

**The three reclassified section kinds are type decisions, not lift failures** — the type decisions themselves are §5.2's
clauses 2, 3 and 6, and what follows is the migration-side instrument for each. A slot below 80% is labelled RECLASSIFY and
the report is not green until an **Accepted ADR id** is attached to that slot; an id that does not resolve to an accepted
record fails the gate (AC-462; ADR-0012). What each one settled:

| Reclassified kind | What the census found | The type decision it forced |
| --- | --- | --- |
| impl-report gates, **75%** (156) | gate results are free-form in **25%** of 156 implementation reports [measured, S-MIG] | Gate results are **emitted by the toolchain and rendered**, never parsed. No code path outside `redkiln-migrate` may parse a gate result out of prose, bullets or fenced output (AC-456), and the typed value round-trips typed → markdown → typed from the store, never from the markdown (AC-477). ADR-0006, ADR-0074 |
| review gate table, **17%** (23) | free-form in **83%** of 23 reviews [measured, S-MIG; n=23 is small and no interval is computed] | The same rule, at its sharpest. A 17% lift is what "parse the prose" was actually worth |
| companion frontmatter, **42%** (45 records over five of the eleven companion kinds, E-0075-2) | 19 lifted, 6 with loss, 20 failed; `_grounding`/`_decomposition` have no frontmatter in 10 of 28 cases; the Phase-1 kind list is settled at AC-143's five plus `ledger` (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #3) | One **JSON sidecar schema per companion kind**; a companion with no frontmatter yields an empty-but-valid sidecar of its own kind, never a stage-schema-shaped guess (AC-460; ADR-0006). **Fixture n is unknown and below 45** — the 45-record row includes plan companions, which AC-459/AC-479 drop, and the census does not break the share out. "45" must not be quoted for this criterion |

Three further decisions came out of the same census. **`plan.md` leaves the artifact list**: 110 of 110 were the unfilled
scaffold, so no `plan` artifact is emitted and the store's artifact-kind enumeration contains no `plan` kind (AC-459), with
every scaffold recorded as a `dropped_by_decision` record carrying path, byte count, digest and the ADR id, or AC-451's
conservation identity does not balance and the run aborts (AC-479; ADR-0075 partially supersedes ADR-0006 on exactly this
point). **The AC type is permissive because the corpus forces it** (the type decision is §5.2 clause 1): 19 of 153 shipped specs (12%) write criteria
with no GWT or no verification note — 76 and 87 loss lines (the per-AC sub-counts first stated were cap-truncated at `loss[:6]`, E-0075-1) — AC-137's disjunctive predicate — so the store writes such a criterion, reads it back byte-identical and schema-validates it with both
fields absent, over all 153 specs at the pin, migration never populating `gwt` or `verification` from criterion text (AC-454; E-0075-2), and it is the **policy**, not the type, that decides whether a missing verification blocks a stage
(AC-455). *(The research pass adds, and it is a recommendation rather than a decision: EARS single-trigger phrasing is
closer to machine-parseable than Gherkin, which bears on whether `gwt` is the right default shape at all — [peer] Mavin et
al. IEEE RE'09, research pass §4 rec. 20.)* **The TDD evidence table migrates with `mutation: unknown`** on the 142 of 156
reports whose table lifted, and the tool never synthesises or back-fills a mutation outcome (AC-457); the F25 columns exist
in **0** reports [documented, proposal §4.6 only; not re-verified — E-0019-1]. The consequence is the criterion that stops the corpus's own gap importing as evidence: an AC whose only
evidence is a migrated row with `mutation: unknown` is reported **unpinned**, and a green suite alone cannot flip it
(AC-458) — four adjudicated defects escaped the deterministic gate, two through fully green suites of 1,890/1,890 and 1,907/1,907 and two against an already-red baseline [measured, F31; E-0082-2].

**What the tool must never do quietly.** An unmapped file crosses as an opaque attachment with its original bytes and a
recorded digest — 18 such files at the pin, asserted both by digest equality and by a **nonzero count**, so "zero unmapped"
(which is how a silently broken census presents) also fails (AC-452). A section that fails to lift is carried as opaque
prose with a migration-loss marker and slot value `unknown`; nothing is inferred, guessed or model-generated (AC-453) —
and the census's **total failed count across the fifteen tabled slots is 54** — not the 40 an earlier arithmetic identity claimed, nor the 53 this sentence first read (the uncapped re-run, PIA-078). An
unparseable file fails the run loudly by file and line and completes only after the fix is enumerated in the report
(AC-461): **2 of 1,312 files** at this pin, the two `_ledger.md` files with unescaped quotes inside quoted evidence strings
[measured, S-MIG]; a third at this pin goes to the human gate (SM-137). Both template generations in the corpus — the
fix-lane **43** (42 plus `issue-146-147-boundary-window/spec.md`, assigned in AC-469 — PIA-078) and the archived 110 — are covered by generation-tagged fixtures, checked by removing each recognition branch
in turn rather than by inspection (AC-469).

**`ChangeSummary` migration is BLOCKED and stays blocked here.** S-BIN is registered and unrun [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: run 2026-10-14, ten records valid at corpus `835b122`; F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*, fix the `Binary` arm's four fields and route an item carrying a `Binary` path to the human gate] and gates the type design and
the port's `change_summary` body, nothing else in Phase 1 (§0.1; ADR-0008, ADR-0012; ADR-0093 decision 4). *(noted 2026-09-25, s8a fix round 2, ADR-0165: ADR-0093 decision 4's sentence is superseded in part. `ChangeSummary` is an enum whose `Text` arm is finalised, and S-BIN now blocks the `Binary` arm and its body only.)* Until it reports, historical change/diff material crosses as **opaque content with
a loss marker and no `ChangeSummary` value is synthesised** (AC-475, PROVISIONAL) — and the second conjunct is disclosed as
trivially true while the type does not exist, kept as a tripwire against a type appearing in the migration path before the
spike that is supposed to design it. **No lift rate for this kind may be written until S-BIN runs.** [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: AC-475 is replaced by its lift criterion at lift share 0/0: the pinned corpus holds no diff-prose section, so no rate is asserted (F43, proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 accepted)*)]

**The commit lands behind a human gate.** The mechanical checks establish that the losses are *enumerated* (AC-451,
AC-461, AC-462, and AC-463, which defines "green" as those three and refuses any other reading); whether a given loss is
*acceptable* is a judgement about content and is routed to a human, not faked as a machine check (AC-476). That gate costs
a bounded ~18,603 tokens whether it lasts 65.2 min or 49.7 h — **one draw per duration point** [measured, F28, **with the published correction that the
flatness was shared-prefix subsidy, not durability**].

**Self-hosting is the switch** (ADR-0002): the claim resolves to at least one recorded fix-lane run under Redkiln-RS whose
event log contains every lane stage from intake to a landed commit, with no stage performed by the old redkiln, and is
refused if any stage is missing (AC-473). **SM-141 carries the standing warning: do not track this as a success rate.**
Five of this programme's metrics saturated and every discriminating result came from cost, stability or mechanism —
F23's detection at 23/23 vs 23/23 — no discordant pairs, no test; the McNemar p = 0.625 is on the CLEAN FP pairs (E-0001-1) — is the worked example [measured, F23; brief §C.12]. Self-hosting means both lanes: AC-521 is the story-lane twin of AC-473 (ADR-0094). A boolean plus
per-run cost and resume distributions carries everything a pass rate would and none of its false comfort. Baseline: **0**
[documented — Phase 1 is unstarted].

### 8.8 Distribution and the plugin surface — PROVISIONAL, and the evidence is borrowed

**§0.1 records this area as PROVISIONAL, and the reason is disclosed here at the point of use.** Proposal §4.9 is three
sentences and **no ADR carries it**. Every distribution finding is `[documented]` from *shipmates'* issue history — one
project, one maintainer, one release series. Redkiln-RS has shipped no binary and has **no distribution baseline of its
own**; AC-501..AC-519's distribution half are borrowed-failure criteria and seven of the area's eight measures start with no
baseline (SM-154 carries E3's $56.47) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-070]. Phase 1 ships no plugin; `rk-plugin-gen` is Phase 2 (proposal §7).

What proposal §4.9 actually decides: **the plugin is a plain git clone** containing generated `commands/`, `agents/`, `skills/`
(and Codex/Pi equivalents from the same source) plus a tiny JS shim that locates or fetches the platform binary from
GitHub Releases via `cargo-dist`. `cargo-dist` is current and not superseded — the CLI rebranded to `dist` at v0.24.0 but
`cargo dist` still works and the crate is still published as `cargo-dist`, at **0.32.0**; crates.io also carries an
unrelated abandoned crate literally named `dist` at 0.0.0, which is a naming hazard rather than a dependency [documented,
1P crates.io / axodotdev, fetched 2026-09-08].

| Rule | Status | Pinned by | Evidence, and whose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The generator's **output count is the assertion**: emitted artifacts equal the number computed from the sources, asserted as a number, and a source entry that emits nothing fails the generator | **DECIDED** by proposal §4.9 | AC-501 | shipmates L5.8 — roughly one commit in fourteen (~7%) exists only to re-sync derived artifacts [documented, one project] |
| **Per-dialect parity**: each source entry present in every supported dialect, asserted as a set difference in both directions, not a total-count check | DECIDED by proposal §4.9 | AC-502 | shipmates L5.8 — a tool shipped with correct syntax everywhere and silently dropped another tool's card. "The gap is convention + parity, not syntax" [documented] |
| Checked-in generated trees regenerate **byte-identically** in CI, or the release is blocked | DECIDED by proposal §4.9 | AC-503 | shipmates L5.8 [documented] |
| **Namespacing from day one**: every emitted name carries the **`rk-`** prefix — the prefix is decided (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #8; the same `rk-` as the crate prefix and the register's subject tags), AC-506 carries the literal and a lint regex, and an unprefixed name fails generation — so an unnamespaced artifact can never be installed even once [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-106] | **DECIDED** by proposal §4.9; the literal by DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #8 | AC-506 | shipmates L3.1 — 20+ generically-named skills, one of them called `gh`, prefixed at 0.1.15 at a cost of one release, three regressions in the next and six open follow-ups: "free at t=0; ~9 issues at t=30d" [documented, one project, one release series] |
| A card whose role declares a schema fails **generation** unless its `tools` contains `StructuredOutput` | DECIDED | AC-504 | F20 [measured] — the generation-time twin of the `doctor` rule in §8.4; the runtime signal is exit 0 with `subtype: success`, so neither check can be inferred from a run |
| The `PreToolUse` deny survives as a **stateless predicate** — it reads only its own tool input and references no stage, run, session or item state | DECIDED by proposal §4.9 | AC-505 | shipmates L1.2 ("a hook can enforce a predicate on its own input, never a position in a sequence"), whose alternative cost nine days and 6,928 lines to prove [documented, L1.1]; settings-based hooks do fire in `-p` [measured, F2 — 2 hook events per dispatch on a bare fixture]. Verified by a lint plus a differential test (same input, two stages, same verdict); **a unit test cannot establish purity** |
| **No live stubs on rename**: after a rename every new name is present on disk and the old-name count is asserted at **0**, not sampled — stubs, aliases and forwarding entries included | **CONDITIONAL** | AC-515 | shipmates L3.3 — "a stub skill is still a live name in the picker, which is the collision you were trying to kill" [documented]. The new-name-presence conjunct is not decoration: without it the criterion passes on an empty tree |
| A rename table applied on install and repair, ordered write-then-delete so an interruption leaves the new path rather than neither; receipts rewritten including siblings sharing a directory | **CONDITIONAL** | AC-514, AC-512 | shipmates L3.3 [documented] |
| Ownership states own / adopt / refuse, because failing closed on "ours but unclaimed" strands users on stale content | **CONDITIONAL** | AC-516, SM-149 | shipmates L3.2 [documented] |
| An integrity scan bounded to the payload's own subtrees, following no symlink | **CONDITIONAL** | AC-517 | shipmates L3.5 — an upgrade stuck on `node_modules/.bin` symlinks [documented] |
| **An embedded payload authoritative over a stray payload-shaped checkout** | **CUT — it contradicts the decision that exists** | (old AC-511, cut and **re-issued** to ports-dist's `--bare` / API-key assertion under the register's §0.4 hazard table — one of six cut ids re-issued with an inline note at both sites, licensed by ADR-0092 decision 3; **id AC-513 retired, not reused**). The "unresolved id conflict" this row carried is settled by the register (`acceptance-criteria.md` §0.4), and the register owns the id [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-036] | Under a plain git clone **the payload *is* the clone** and only the binary is fetched, so there is no compiled-in payload to prefer and the named instrument ("installed content digests match the embed") cannot be written against the decided shape. The lesson is real; the decision it was attributed to does not exist, and the one that does points the other way. It binds only if an embed-and-install mechanism is decided in an ADR of its own |

**The `CONDITIONAL` rows are not commitments.** They bind if and only if a distribution mechanism with an
install-and-repair step is decided; until then their `decisions:` field names a shipmates lesson and never proposal §4.9. Reading
them as settled requirements on a build runbook is the exact failure this subsection exists to prevent.

**The upgrade path is the release gate.** A release candidate is refused unless an end-to-end job installs the previous
published version, upgrades to the candidate, and asserts namespacing and generated-artifact counts on the upgraded tree —
plus receipts and rename-table application **if and only if** those mechanisms are adopted (AC-519, partly conditional;
fixtures pinned by SHA per ADR-0013). The borrowed evidence for the gate is specific and worth carrying whole: shipmates
added a CLI e2e job at 0.1.5 after **five blind releases**, and *every* escape since (#384, #386) was still an upgrade-path
escape found by the owner upgrading rather than by the job — "the upgrade path is simultaneously the least-tested and
most-broken surface in this class of product" [documented, shipmates L3.8, one project]. **SM-146** therefore targets zero
upgrade escapes per release once that job gates the pipeline, quotes the count rather than a rate, and states that no
interval is computed below n=5 releases; its kill falsifies the fixture rather than the users — escapes continuing after
the job exists means the job exercises a synthetic tree.

**SM-145** tracks the derived-artifact re-sync tax against shipmates' ~7% comparator, from **no baseline of our own**,
starting with the first 100 commits after `rk-plugin-gen` lands.

### 8.9 Decisions this section takes

| # | Decision | Alternatives considered | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 8-a | One idempotent `run` entry, no `--resume`, enforced by a committed argument-surface snapshot and a name denylist; an idempotent `init` | a `--resume` flag; a resume subcommand aliasing `run`; judgement-based review only | proposal §4.5 (L2.3), **ADR-0107** [documented]; F19, F22 [measured]; the residual (an unlisted name) is stated, not closed |
| 8-b | `reconcile()` on every item-acting entry, with no flag able to skip it | reconcile on `run` only; a `--fast` path for `status` | F19, F18 [measured, one operator, one machine] |
| 8-c | CLI is the only gate-answer channel in Phase 1, with one store call site | CLI + GitHub comments from day one | ADR-0009, ADR-0011 [documented] |
| 8-d | `doctor` is a data-driven rule table, all configuration-time, zero model cost, bound to a rejecting-fixture corpus | rules as code; a model-assisted "explain my config" check | every configuration-time analysis in the programme measured $0 [measured, brief §D]; F20 as the precedent for a check that stops checking |
| 8-e | `explain` renders the recorded resolution and never recomputes selection | recompute at explain time for freshness | F36 [measured] — a recomputation would print something that never ran |
| 8-f | Projections are derived, with at most one write (the web view's gate answer) and inbound text untrusted and typed | tracker as the state machine (Symphony's shape, named as the anti-pattern) | proposal §4.8 [documented]; AC-507/AC-508 |
| 8-g | `redkiln-migrate` is a separate, zero-dispatch binary emitting one reviewed commit plus a differential report | a `redkiln migrate` subcommand; a runtime compatibility layer; a model-assisted lifter | ADR-0002, ADR-0012; S-MIG at $0 [measured] |
| 8-h | A slot below 80% is RECLASSIFY and blocks the report until an Accepted ADR id is attached | a single overall lift percentage; a human sign-off with no id | S-MIG at pin `47faec9f` [measured] — the overall 98% hides the 17% |
| 8-i | The plugin ships as a plain clone of generated artifacts, namespaced from day one, with the generator's output count as the assertion | a binary-embedded payload with an installer, receipts and a rename table (shipmates' architecture) | proposal §4.9 [documented]; the installer machinery is CONDITIONAL and one row of it is CUT — see §8.8 |

## 9. Rust realisation — crates, dependencies, and engineering policy

**Read this section knowing what grounds it.** The external pass asked directly whether authoritative guidance exists for a ~12-crate, one-binary, per-adapter-crate workspace and answered **no** — "feature-unification mechanics are well documented; 'best practice for this exact shape' is practitioner synthesis" (evidence-augmentation §5, fetched 2026-09-08) [documented]. Everything below the dependency table is therefore **synthesis**, recorded as ADR-0083 and ADR-0084 so it is citable rather than assumed. The same pass found, by direct repository inspection and in this severity order: **no `.github/workflows` directory exists in the repository at all**; **no `rust-toolchain.toml`** anywhere; **no `[lints]` table and no `clippy.toml`**; and **no `tests/` directory**, with 17 files using inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (evidence-augmentation §3, Rust practice, direct inspection — **no URL**) [measured]. Those four absences are the starting position, not a risk forecast.

### 9.1 The crate graph

Ten crate families — **and this table is the crate-name authority** (ADR-0099 clause 5): the AC register's `rk-*` label after each id is a subject tag, mapped in its §0 — `rk-select`, `rk-catalog`, `rk-packs`, `rk-policy`, `rk-kernel-verify` are modules of `rk-kernel`; `rk-store-artifacts` of `rk-store`; `rk-port-toolchain` of `rk-ports`; `rk-doctor` of `rk-cli` (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #15) — so a checkpoint's `cargo test -p rk-kernel -- <module>` is nameable and no label is a crate until a later split. The column that changed since the rev. 4 proposal is **runtime**: ADR-0076 makes every port trait sync and confines every `.await` to an adapter, so `tokio` is **not** a workspace-wide dependency — it appears in adapter manifests only, and `async-trait` never enters the graph at all.

| Crate | Owns | Origin | May depend on an async runtime? | Evidence / decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `rk-types` | ids; typed artifacts (`Spec`, `ImplReport`, `Findings`, `DocSet`, digests); schema derivation; `StepError`; `Drift`; `GateBasis` | new | **No** | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0075; the `spoke()` predicate and raw-retention pattern carry from `spikes/runner/src/step.rs` — **the variant set is redesigned: ADR-0095 decision 2's table** (`Transport`, `Schema`, `Timeout`, `Cancelled`, `NonZero`, `RateLimited`, `NotAdopted`, `NotIsolated`, each with its payload, retry class, `spoke()` value and event arm) [measured, direct inspection; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-005]. Also owns the `EventKind` enum (§5.4's event-kind table, ADR-0105) and the closed-output schema transform (§5.3) |
| `rk-ports` — `ChangeSummary` (an opaque, non-finalised newtype, the one site Checkpoint 5's absence grep excludes — ADR-0099 clause 5) *(amended 2026-09-24, `s8a`, ADR-0165: no longer so. `ChangeSummary` is an enum defined only in `rk-ports`, with a `Text` arm finalised now and a `Binary` slot whose payload is sealed until S-BIN reports at `s8i`; S-BIN now gates the `Binary` arm and its body only)* | the non-diff change type | new | — | **BLOCKED.** S-BIN is registered and **unrun**; [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: run 2026-10-14, ten records valid at corpus `835b122`; F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*, fix the `Binary` arm's four fields and route an item carrying a `Binary` path to the human gate] no review evidence of any kind exists for items whose change is not a text diff (ADR-0008, ADR-0012). `rk-types` ships the other artifact types; `ChangeSummary`'s field set is **not fixed in Phase 1** and the five toolchain-keyed kinds (ADR-0073) cannot be finalised until S-BIN runs — which blocks that type and the port's `change_summary` body and nothing else (ADR-0093 decision 4). See §0.1 and §10 |
| `rk-kernel` | packs (process + technology), stages/activities, policy, memo, gates, budgets, retry, replay *(amended 2026-09-20, Step 7d Slice 4 cycle 1 — settling routing **RT-54**, whose owed record was “the record naming the diagnostic mode as the loader's surface” (`s7d` §7d.1 edit (4)). **The activity-catalog loader has a SECOND entry point, and it is a mode rather than a parser**: `catalog::load_diagnostic(&[(&str, &str)])` and `catalog::load_diagnostic_dir(&Path)`, returning `CatalogLoad { catalog, diagnostics }` — every defect typed as a `CatalogDiagnostic { error: CatalogError, kind: DiagnosticKind }` instead of the first one raised. `doctor` is its only caller (`s7d` §7d.8); `run` keeps loading through the strict `Catalog::from_sources` / `from_dir`. **`CatalogError` stays at three variants** — `Io`, `Toml`, `Shape` — and `DiagnosticKind` is the diagnostic's own vocabulary, not a fourth variant: its one member that is not a transcription is `GateOutputs`, which types AC-409's refusal of a `Gate` step carrying `outputs` apart from every other `Shape` so that `rk-cli`'s `RK-D-GATE-OUTPUTS` row maps a value rather than matching text inside a message. **One walker serves both modes** — the strict loader is the diagnostic walk with its first diagnostic taken as the error — because the alternative `s7d` §7d.1 names and rejects, calling the strict loader per file and collecting first errors, surfaces one defect of two when both sit in the same activity file, and that is the case `doctor` exists to report. **This takes nothing back from an accepted record and is therefore a dated note and not a supersession** (ADR-0092, which forbids an erratum that touches a `## Decision`): no Decision sentence anywhere fixes the loader's entry-point count, ADR-0045's rule — a gate step's output is `answer`, never `outputs` — is **enforced** by this edit rather than withdrawn, and the crate's forbidden-dependency and port-boundary properties in this row's last cell are untouched. Measured 2026-09-20 before the edit: `load_diagnostic` existed nowhere under `crates/`, and `outputs` was parsed for every `StepKind`.)* | new | **No — forbidden** | ADR-0021, ADR-0022, ADR-0086; ADR-0076 forbids `tokio`, `async-std`, `futures` and `async-trait` here. No dependency edge, direct or transitive, on any `rk-adapter-*` (**AC-484**); no generic parameter bound by a port trait (**AC-480**) |
| `rk-store` | the typed `.bklg`/`.kb` store, canonical serialiser, markdown rendering, locks, index | new | No | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, ADR-0018; layout and the single write path are §5's |
| `rk-git` | trailers, `reconcile()`, worktrees, disposable clones, merge queue; the journalled `GitOps` seam (a struct, so AC-483 stands) and the one exec wrapper | `reconcile.rs` (a re-implementation of the old tree's `src/store/verify.ts`, built in the spike runner) + new | No | ADR-0003 (git is **not** a port; a concrete crate the kernel names — **AC-483**); `reconcile.rs` run over 360 commits / 74 slices, not validated against an oracle [measured, F12; PIA-036]; the contract's rules are ADR-0105's. **gix scope is restricted by construction — see §9.2.1** |
| `rk-ports` | the five port traits, `Send + Sync` supertraits, held as `Arc<dyn Port + Send + Sync>` (the **EventStore** port is `Arc<Mutex<dyn EventStore>>`, because `dispatch` takes `&mut dyn EventStore` — **ADR-0115**, narrowing ADR-0099 clause 4); one fake each; the `CancelToken`; the `ChangeSummary` newtype *(noted 2026-09-25, s8a fix round 2, ADR-0165: no longer a newtype but an enum with a finalised `Text` arm and a sealed `Binary` arm)* | new | **No** — no `async fn` and no future-returning method in any trait; `cargo tree -p rk-kernel -e normal` names none of tokio/async-std/futures/async-trait (S-PORTS checks it on 1.98.1) | ADR-0003, ADR-0076, ADR-0099. Fake count equals trait count and the equality is asserted as a number (**AC-481**); divergence is measured by **SM-151** |
| `rk-adapter-{sqlite,claude,github,mdbook}`, `rk-adapter-toolchain-{node,**rust**,dotnet,python,unreal}` — Phase 1 builds `sqlite`, `claude`, `toolchain-node` and **`toolchain-rust`** (ADR-0094) | one crate per adapter; each owns its own runtime handle, constructed once at the composition root and never per call; `-claude` holds the Job Object / process-group containment in one module under a commented `#![allow(unsafe_code)]` (ADR-0099, ADR-0102) and the committed environment denylist `env_denylist.rs` | mixed — `-claude` lifts `runner.rs`, `event.rs`, `step.rs`, `throttle.rs`, `journal.rs` from `spikes/runner` | **Yes — and only here** | ADR-0003, ADR-0076, ADR-0094, ADR-0099; `-claude` is subscription-only and refuses construction when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is present in the captured environment map it receives as a value (ADR-0010, **AC-488**) |
| `rk-adapter-codex` | the second harness | new | Yes | ADR-0007 — validation-only, and **S-CODEX is unrun**; it is not in Phase 1 |
| `rk-cli` | `clap` router; one idempotent `run` entry; `answer`, `status`, `explain`, `doctor` / `doctor --rebuild`, `catalog search`, `init`, and `lint --facet-stability` / `--facet-provenance` wrapping the kernel's two facet lints *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 RA-M-02: this cell read "`answer`, `status`, `doctor`", omitting three of §8.1's Phase-1 verbs)*. **The composition root** — it constructs the `CancelToken` and owns the Ctrl-C / SIGTERM path (ADR-0099; `ctrlc`, DECISIONS-2026-09-18 D18-2), reads the environment once, and is the only place `Config` is **constructed** *(amended 2026-09-18, C-01 RB-M-05: ADR-0083's Decision "a `Config` struct constructed at the composition root and passed in" had no declaring crate — **`pub struct Config` is declared in `rk-kernel`** (`crates/rk-kernel/src/config.rs`, a Step 7d kernel edit), constructed only here, threaded into `RunInputs` and recorded on the invocation's first row — `reconcile_started`'s payload, the routed carrier of AC-485's *run-start event* until a record names one: `EventKind::ALL` is 38 with no `run_started` (s7d RT-41, measured 2026-09-18); a dated §5 note or an ADR-0116-route kind is OWED)* | new | Yes (it constructs adapters) | ADR-0009, ADR-0011, ADR-0107, ADR-0083; the adapter set is resolved once at startup and is a run-scoped constant (**AC-485**), and an adapter that is not compiled in or is misconfigured refuses the run before any spawn or store write (**AC-486**) |
| `redkiln-migrate` | the old-corpus converter; a separate binary; the **only** workspace member that may depend on a YAML crate (`yaml_serde = "=0.10.7"`, ADR-0100) | new | Yes | ADR-0002, ADR-0013, ADR-0100; behaviour and the migration differential are §8's |
| `rk-plugin-gen` | emit harness artifacts from packs and cards | new | Yes | Phase 2 (ADR-0011); the distribution surface and its borrowed-evidence caveat are §8's |

**Three properties the graph holds by construction, not by care.** `anyhow` exists in exactly one crate family (`rk-cli` and the two other binaries); the process reads its environment in exactly one place (the composition root, while building `Config`); and the async runtime exists in exactly the crates that spawn processes or open sockets. Each is a manifest-level fact a reviewer can check by reading `Cargo.toml`. **Only one of the three is instrumented today:** the runtime property is carried by **AC-484** (no `rk-kernel` → `rk-adapter-*` edge) plus the ADR-0076 dependency ban listed in §9.2's "where it may appear" column. **The `anyhow`-in-one-crate-family and environment-read-in-one-place properties have no criterion in any of the thirteen area files** — they are conventions with a manifest-level *shape*, not checks, and a manifest-level assertion for each is owed. §9.3's structural-assertion list is where they would live; until they are authored, "by construction, not by care" is true only of the third. [inferred, no criterion — recorded rather than asserted]

**What the sync-port decision changed in the imported code.** `spikes/runner` is async top to bottom — `tokio` with `rt-multi-thread`, `macros`, `process`, `io-util`, `time`, `sync` [measured, direct inspection of `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml`]. Under ADR-0076 that body survives **inside** `rk-adapter-claude`, wrapped by a sync port method that blocks on an adapter-owned runtime — **unchanged except for `runner.rs`'s kill path**: the runner does `taskkill /T /F` (victim exit code 1, n=1) and a POSIX process-group kill never obtains on Windows, so the kill is rewritten as ADR-0099's per-OS containment — a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` created before spawn on Windows, `process_group(0)` + `killpg` on POSIX — fired by the composition root's `CancelToken`, with AC-225's classifier keyed on the token rather than an exit code [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-010]. The two consequences ADR-0076 records are carried here rather than softened: an adapter must never call `Handle::block_on` from a thread its own runtime is driving, and the `-p` stream events of F4 must be consumed inside the adapter and surfaced as a completed value — the kernel cannot iterate a stream.

### 9.2 Dependencies

Two provenance classes, kept apart deliberately. **Proven** means the crate is in `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml` today and has run the P-series [measured, direct inspection, 2026-09-08]. **New** means the version was verified in the external pass on 2026-09-08 and has never been compiled in this repository. ADR-0084 explicitly forbids citing its version list as a current-versions source after 2026-09-08; this table inherits that expiry, and its 0.x rows follow ADR-0100's explicit pin list (amended 2026-09-11, PIA-028): **exact-pinned** — `gix`, `jsonschema`, `rusqlite`, `yaml_serde`; **exempt, with the reason in the manifest comment** — `chrono ^0.4` and `sha2 ^0.11`, de-facto stable multi-year 0.x lines with semver discipline; any new 0.x dependency is exact-pinned unless its row says why not.

The EventStore **port** and its SQLite **adapter** remain 7a's and 7c's.)* | **new** | WAL + `busy_timeout` = **5000 ms** (rusqlite's default) + **one writer connection per process behind a mutex, no queue, never a pool**; `Connection: Send + !Sync` [documented, docs.rs rusqlite 0.40.2 and sqlite.org WAL, fetched 2026-09-10; ADR-0105 clause 7]. Checkpoint 7d greps the pin |
| `cargo-audit` | version not verified in the pass | CI tool, not linked | CI — run as `cargo audit --deny unmaintained` | **new** | ADR-0084 makes it a required job; **its own version was never checked** [documented, gap]; retained beside `cargo-deny` for its RUSTSEC-specific output (ADR-0100) |
| `cargo-deny` | installed `--locked` at Step 5.2 | CI tool, not linked | CI — a fifth gate beside fmt/clippy/test/audit, with a committed `deny.toml` | **new** | Carries ADR-0086's four bans (`temporalio-sdk`, `graph-flow`, `dbos`, `durable` — workspace-wide, dev-dependencies included) in `[bans].deny`, `[advisories]` with `unmaintained = "deny"`, and `[licenses]`; a must-fail fixture adds a banned crate to a scratch member; AC-005/AC-044/AC-120's Verified-by lines name it (ADR-0100 decision 3; PIA-029) |
