**A clean row is not a clean bill.** No advisory check was run in the external pass for `schemars`, `insta`, `trycmd`, `assert_cmd`, `cargo-semver-checks`, `git-cliff` or `release-plz` — that is *absence of evidence*, and ADR-0084 says so in terms. The CI `cargo audit` job against the committed `Cargo.lock` is what will actually establish their status; it is also the reason the job is part of the decision rather than a nice-to-have, since the `gix-*` advisory list below was reconstructed per-advisory (rustsec.org 404'd on the 2026-09-08 fetch and returned 200 on 2026-09-10) and **is not guaranteed complete** — RUSTSEC-2024-0352 (`gix-index`) is omitted under that flag (E-0084-1) [documented].

#### 9.2.1 `gix` — restricted scope, exact pin, and an audit job that watches `gix-*`

`gix` is at **0.87.1**: pre-1.0, and a facade over dozens of independently versioned `gix-*` sub-crates. It is multi-contributor (GitoxideLabs; EliahKagan authored most of the 2024 security fixes) but has **one crates.io owner** (Byron), so publish authority rests with one person — a bus-factor note, not a single-author development risk — and it is a 0.x risk with load-bearing semantics behind it [documented, 1P crates.io + GitHub org, fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-083].

| Advisory | Sub-crate | Nature | Fixed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RUSTSEC-2025-0021 / CVE-2025-31130 | `gix-features` | missing SHA-1 collision detection | **≥ 0.41.0** (`gix-features`; 0.71.0 is the `gix` facade's fixed version — the GHSA lists 27 packages; gix 0.87.1 pulls `gix-features` ^0.49.1) [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-083; E-0084-1] |
| RUSTSEC-2025-0001 | `gix-worktree-state` | checkout sets files world-writable | ≥ 0.17.0 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0349 | `gix-worktree` | path traversal / arbitrary file write via **crafted tree or blob names** inside a crafted repository, enabling code execution because hooks can be installed (CVSS 8.8) | ≥ 0.34.0 (`gix-worktree`; `gix-worktree-state` ≥ 0.17.0); gix 0.87.1 pulls `gix-worktree` ^0.56.0 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0351 / 0353 | `gix-ref` (0351) / `gix-worktree` (0353) — not the `gix` facade; both share CVE-2024-35197 / GHSA-49jc-r788-3fc9 | Windows reserved device names (Windows only) | `gix-ref` ≥ 0.44.0; `gix-worktree` ≥ 0.34.0; gix 0.87.1 pulls `gix-ref` ^0.67.1 |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0355 / 0367 / 0371 | `gix-path` | config resolution | before 0.87.1 |
| GHSA-2vh6-hw4j-32ww | `gix-packetline` | ≤ 0.21.4 affected | patched above 0.21.4 [documented, osv.dev, fetched 2026-09-10; added 2026-09-11] |
| GHSA-7rhf-42qf-vrvc / CVE-2025-24890 | `gix-sec` | ≤ 0.13.2 affected | patched 2026-09-02 [documented, osv.dev, fetched 2026-09-10; added 2026-09-11] |
| RUSTSEC-2024-0352 | `gix-index` | omitted from the reconstructed list under the "not complete" flag | — (E-0084-1) |

*Source: [1P] GitHub Advisory Database / RustSec, via evidence-augmentation §3, fetched 2026-09-08; per-crate fixed versions re-read at rustsec.org and osv.dev on 2026-09-10 (ERRATA E-0084-1) [documented].*

**Every pin is clear: gix 0.87.1 pulls `gix-features` ^0.49.1, `gix-ref` ^0.67.1, `gix-worktree` ^0.56.0, all above the fixed versions, so the pin is CLEAN as of 2026-09-10.** The density is what drives the policy, not the current status: the cluster is **checkout and path handling, several of them Windows-specific**, which is precisely the disposable-worktree and shallow-clone-of-an-external-branch surface a merge queue exercises hardest, on the platform this system is being built on. Hence three rules from ADR-0084, none of which is optional:

1. `gix = "=0.87.1"` — an **exact** pin, never a caret range. The 0.x pin list is explicit (ADR-0100): `jsonschema`, `rusqlite` and `yaml_serde` are exact-pinned beside it; `chrono` and `sha2` are exempt with the reason in the manifest comment; stable 1.x dependencies use ordinary caret ranges, because pinning everything is cargo cult.
2. `cargo audit --deny unmaintained` and `cargo deny check` run against the committed `Cargo.lock` on every push and pull request, **watching `gix-*` specifically** — `clone` is on the list of operations the audit job and the Windows CI target cover (ADR-0098) — and a new advisory fails the build. The pin *without* the audit would be strictly worse than a caret range, because a pin makes security patches arrive only when someone bumps it.
3. Bumping the pin is a deliberate PR that re-runs `cargo audit`, re-checks the `gix-*` sub-crate set in `Cargo.lock`, and re-runs **S-GIX/a–c** (ADR-0077, ADR-0098) if the pin crosses a version where any arm's answer could have changed. gix currency is a **release-blocking check**, not a one-time approval. The **Windows CI target is not optional** given where the cluster sits.

**Scope, and what stays blocked.** gix owns `rev-parse` (the common-dir read falling back to the wrapper on an S-GIX/a no-go), status, trailer **parsing** (discharged — `BodyRef::trailers()`, E-0077-1) and the one retained **write**, the shallow clone by SHA (`PrepareFetch::with_revision` + `with_shallow`, S-GIX/c). The write path — `git worktree add/remove`, `git commit --trailer`, `interpret-trailers`, `write-tree`, `patch-id`, the integration-ref merge — shells out through the journalled wrapper (ADR-0077, ADR-0098, ADR-0105 clause 5). The two documented gaps that keep it that way are stated in full in **§5.9** and are not restated here: the unimplemented `GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` interplay, precisely the mechanism a store at `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ leans on, and trailer **writing** as a **[documented] absence at the pin** (docs.rs read 2026-09-10, the executed-test arm of S-GIX/b still owed) over what is tier-1 state, named in the pass itself as the highest-value single check in its list [documented, 1P GitoxideLabs/gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08; docs.rs 2026-09-10; evidence-augmentation §1 rows 2 and 8, and §5]. **This document makes no claim that gix can write a trailer.** Both are discharged by **S-GIX/a** and **S-GIX/b**, whose named consumers are `rk-git`'s read and write paths (§5, §10). The shell-out route is decided and unblocked; only gix's ownership of it is not.

### 9.3 Engineering policy (ADR-0083)

| Policy | The rule | What it forbids | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Edition** | `edition = "2024"` on every crate, set once as a workspace package field and inherited | — | `spikes/runner/Cargo.toml` already carries it — settled by demonstration [measured, direct inspection] |
| **Toolchain** | A root `rust-toolchain.toml` pinning an **exact** rustc version — **`channel = "1.98.1"`** (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #7; every checkpoint greps the full string; one recorded instrument move from the spike programme's 1.97 line) — declaring at minimum `rustfmt` and `clippy`; **`workspace.package.rust-version` is set equal to the pin** and inherited, to arm resolver 3's MSRV fallback and `cargo add`'s version choice, never as a consumer floor; the pin-bump PR bumps both (ADR-0102 decision 1) | a floating `channel = "stable"`; having no `rust-toolchain.toml`; omitting `rust-version` or letting it differ from the pin | A pin and a floor answer different questions and co-exist; the product is one binary plus internal crates, so the pin is the mechanism [documented, [1P] RFC 3537 — the "pin for a binary you control" / application framing is the RFC's; [blog] swatinem.de corroborates only "pin the toolchain as a dependency and make updates intentional" — re-attributed 2026-09-11, PIA-023] |
| **Lints — location** | `[workspace.lints.rust]` / `[workspace.lints.clippy]` in the root manifest; every member carries `[lints] workspace = true` **and nothing else** — Cargo rejects a `[lints]` table mixing `workspace = true` with other keys, so no per-crate manifest override exists (RFC 3389; cargo #13157 open); **the only in-source override is `#[expect(clippy::<lint>, reason = "…")]`**, which fails the build when the lint stops firing, and the only `allow` in source is one commented `#![allow(unsafe_code)]` on `rk-adapter-claude`'s Job Object module (ADR-0102 decisions 2–3) | bare `#[allow]` anywhere; `#![deny]` in source as a restatement of policy; a per-crate `[lints]` override | one place to read the policy, one place a divergence shows [documented, 1P Cargo Book **"Workspaces"** and manifest pages — `/cargo/reference/lints.html` covers Cargo's own lints and shows only `[workspace.lints.cargo]`; rust-lang/cargo #13157 ("cannot override workspace.lints in lints") supports the rule rather than contradicting it — re-cited 2026-09-11] |
| **Lints — content** | `all = { level = "deny", priority = -1 }`; an **enumerated** pedantic subset at deny (default priority, above the group) — `semicolon_if_nothing_returned`, `explicit_iter_loop`, `match_same_arms`, `redundant_closure_for_method_calls`, `items_after_statements`; `nursery = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }` only; `unsafe_code = "deny"`. **Group entries carry an explicit `priority`** — the pinned toolchain's `cargo clippy` rejects a group beside individual lints without it (`lint_groups_priority`, ADR-0102 decision 4; PIA-025) | denying `clippy::pedantic` or `clippy::nursery` **as groups**; `nursery` at any level but `warn` | `clippy::correctness` is the only deny-by-default group; the Clippy Lints book documents `nursery` as "new lints that are still under development", "buggy or need more work", and advises against enabling the whole group — "can change or be removed without notice" was a paraphrase, not the book's text, and is withdrawn as a quotation (ADR-0102 decision 5; PIA-023) — so a group deny across a dozen crates is a CI outage waiting on the next clippy release [documented, 1P Clippy Lints book, fetched 2026-09-10]. Adding a lint to the subset is a routine PR; the subset is a list, never a group name |
| **Errors** | `thiserror` in libraries so callers can match variants; `anyhow` **confined to binaries** (`rk-cli`, `redkiln-migrate`) | `anyhow::Error` in any library crate's public API | **Documented by the crate author** — dtolnay's `anyhow` README ("Comparison to thiserror") and docs.rs/thiserror describe the library/application split — and **vendor-corroborated with one named divergence**: Microsoft's M-APP-ERROR / M-ERRORS-CANONICAL-STRUCTS name the split and allow `anyhow` in in-repo application-only crates, where this workspace confines it to binaries because adapter crates are shared libraries. The stricter "never in a library's public API" is the project's own rule beyond the sources' recommendation [documented, 1P crate author; vendor, one divergence — fetched 2026-09-10; ADR-0102 decision 6; the earlier practitioner-blogs-only provenance (OneUptime 2026-01-25; Pi Stack 2026-06-22) is withdrawn — PIA-079] |
| **Tests** | Unit tests stay inline in `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`; integration tests consolidate into at most **one binary per crate under test**, `tests/it/main.rs` with `mod` declarations per case | one integration-test file per case | One file is one binary relinking the whole stack; consolidating Cargo's own suite cut build time **3×** and artifact size **5×** [measured, external — [blog] matklad.github.io, measured on Cargo's suite by its author. It licenses the direction and the mechanism, **not** a predicted 3×/5× for this workspace]. The current total absence of any integration-level test against a public API is the fourth measured gap, recorded as a known one |
| **Configuration** | Configuration reaches the kernel **as a value** — a `Config` constructed at the composition root and passed in. The process may read the environment **once**, at the edge, while building it | `std::env::set_var` / `remove_var` **anywhere in the workspace, tests included**; the kernel reading the process environment at all | `set_var`/`remove_var` became `unsafe fn` in edition 2024 because POSIX `setenv` is not thread-safe and races a concurrent `getenv` — directly implicating `cargo test`'s default multi-threaded harness [documented, 1P Rust Edition Guide; rust-lang/rust PR #124636]. This licenses "environment *mutation* is unsound under the default harness"; it does **not** license a claim that reading the environment once at process start is unsafe, which it is not |
| **CI** | A workflow from the first commit, on every push and PR, on the pinned toolchain: `cargo fmt --all --check` · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` (gated on exit code; nursery warnings visible and non-blocking) · `cargo test --workspace` · `cargo audit --deny unmaintained` (ADR-0084, ADR-0100) · `cargo deny check` against the committed `deny.toml` (ADR-0100) | merging without all five green | **There is no CI at all today** — the single largest of the four measured absences [measured, direct inspection]. This is specified as a **floor that must never again be absent**, not a ceiling |

**The env-var ban and the child's environment are different things.** The ban is on this workspace reading or mutating *its own* process environment outside the composition root. Constructing the *child's* environment explicitly — including `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=1h` in the runner env (ADR-0078) — is the opposite behaviour and is required: it is "explicit over discovered", and it is what F8 forces, since a default `claude -p` inherited 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools with MCP alone accounting for **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the cache write [measured, F8]. Whether either variable took effect is verified per run from the nested `cache_creation { ephemeral_1h_input_tokens, ephemeral_5m_input_tokens }` object, present on 954/954 recorded runs (PIA-067; F3's "absent" was the flat naming); the billing-mode confound is S-TTL1's job (ADR-0078) and §7's subject. The child's environment is the captured map inherited minus a committed denylist, received by the adapter as a value in `Config` and never read from the process environment (DECISIONS-2026-09-11 #16). Nothing here may be read as verifying a TTL without that per-run evidence.

**CI carries structural assertions, not only lints.** The AC set names instruments that have to live somewhere, and that somewhere is this workflow: a `cargo metadata` dependency-graph assertion that no `rk-kernel` → `rk-adapter-*` edge exists (**AC-484**); an API-surface lint rejecting port-bound generic parameters in `rk-kernel` (**AC-480**); the port-inventory lint with a denylist on the git surface (**AC-483**); the `rk-ports` count assertion `traits.len() == fakes.len()` (**AC-481**); a grep rule forbidding `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` on the usage struct's fields (**AC-362**); "no `async fn` and no future-returning method in any `rk-ports` trait" plus `cargo tree -p rk-kernel -e normal` naming none of tokio/async-std/futures/async-trait (**AC-528**, ADR-0076 made checkable — PIA-029); `cargo deny check bans` with its must-fail fixture (ADR-0100); and the golden schema snapshot of §9.5. Each is cheap, deterministic and zero model cost — which is the only reason a rule stated in prose is worth converting into one.

### 9.4 Two things this policy does not settle

| Open item | Why it is open | Named spike / check | Named consumer | Hedge |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Per-adapter feature isolation** | Resolver v2 scopes feature unification by target kind but still unifies workspace-wide *within* a target for any shared dependency, so a `cargo build --workspace` can leak a test-only or adapter-only feature into the shipped binary's graph. The only reliable isolations are separate `cargo build -p` invocations per adapter, or identical feature sets across every crate sharing a dependency. ADR-0083 records this as **left open and not to be assumed away** [documented, [blog] nickb.dev; 1P Rust internals] | **S-FEAT** (registered in §10.5) — build the workspace both ways against the Phase-1 adapter set and diff the resolved feature sets in `Cargo.lock`/`cargo tree -e features` for the shipped binary | the Phase-1 release build and the `cargo-dist` pipeline (§8) | Until S-FEAT runs, the release build uses **per-crate `cargo build -p`**, which is correct under either answer and costs only CI wall time |
| **rusqlite multi-process WAL and `busy_timeout` semantics** | The docs.rs read is taken: `busy_timeout` defaults to 5000 ms, `Connection: Send + !Sync`, WAL admits one writer at a time and is unsupported over a network filesystem [documented, docs.rs rusqlite 0.40.2 / sqlite.org, fetched 2026-09-10]; whether 5000 ms suffices under the self-hosting run's load is unmeasured | **R-SQLITE** (§10.5) — the two-process contention test against the pinned version is what remains | `rk-adapter-sqlite`; the store design is §5's | One writer connection per process behind a mutex, no queue, every write transaction shorter than `busy_timeout` (ADR-0105 clause 7) ships either way — the test can only narrow the rule, not licence a pool |
| **Whether a dyn-compatible native `async fn` in trait now exists on stable** | **Read, and negative through 1.98.1 (2026-09-10)**: the 1.97.0 and 1.98.0 release notes stabilise nothing async/dyn; `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` is nightly-only with tracking issue rust-lang/rust #133119 open (R-ASYNC, §10.5) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10] | a direct read of the release notes for the version in `rust-toolchain.toml`, plus the tracking issue, at every pin bump | **the review of any toolchain-pin bump** — ADR-0076 names it; ADR-0102's 1.98.1 pin | ADR-0076 is correct under either answer, so this gates nothing; the working assumption stays "still requires `async-trait` for dyn ports", and the kernel needs neither |

Every other unknown this section touches is registered in §10 (S-BIN for `ChangeSummary`, S-GIX/a–c for the git read, write and clone paths, S-TTL1 for the cache TTL, S-CODEX for the second harness, S-PORTS for the port shape on 1.98.1). None is discharged here in prose.

### 9.5 Schema-generation discipline

The closed-output rules — no `oneOf` at any node, no union nested in a union, `additionalProperties: false`, required-first, bounded optional and union-typed parameters — are constraints on the **Rust types plus one export transform** (ADR-0095). They are **not** something `schemars` enforces: schemars 1.2.2 renders `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` as `oneOf` when the variants are unique (`anyOf` otherwise), so the earlier statement that internal tagging "renders a single discriminated object" was wrong [measured, generated against the pinned version; documented, 1P docs.rs/schemars, fetched 2026-09-10]; the export passes the derived schema through a `schemars::transform::Transform` that rewrites a `kind`-discriminated `oneOf` into an equivalent `anyOf`, and the snapshot sees the exported shape. A rule that lives only in prose is not a rule, and this is a boundary where violations are known to fail **open**: F20 measured an agent card's `tools` allowlist silently disabling `--json-schema` — `structured_output` absent, **exit 0**, `subtype: success` [measured, F20].

So ADR-0084 (as ADR-0095 restates it) requires a **golden schema snapshot** (`insta`) for every type crossing the closed-output boundary, asserting on the exported JSON that it contains no `oneOf` anywhere and no nested union, sets `additionalProperties: false` at every object level, orders `required` first, and stays within the 24-optional / 16-union-typed-parameter budgets per exported schema; Checkpoint 5 greps `oneOf|anyOf` over the snapshot directory expecting `oneOf` → 0. A changed snapshot is a schema change and is reviewed as one.

Two honesty constraints on that test, plus the residual ADR-0084 names about itself. The budgets are **the vendor's**: the platform structured-outputs page carries a "Schema complexity limits" table — 24 optional parameters, 16 union-typed parameters (`anyOf` or type arrays), 20 strict tools per request, 180 s compile timeout, per request across all strict tool schemas plus the JSON output schema — present on 2026-09-10 and in a Wayback capture of 2026-09-07; the earlier reading that "no limits table was found" was a truncation artefact of the 2026-09-08 fetch (ERRATA E-0084-2), and the SDK issue's "4 tools × 6 optional params = 24" arithmetic was a restatement of it, not its source. The snapshot counts per exported schema under the stated precondition (one output schema per dispatch, no strict user-defined tools — an argv lint), and S-SCHEMA-CAP observes the vendor's failure shape [documented, platform.claude.com, fetched 2026-09-10; amended 2026-09-11, PIA-005]. And F31's schema conformance of **52/52 = 100%** measures that *emitted payloads conformed to a schema* [measured, F31]; it says nothing about whether the *generated schema* satisfies the closed-output constraints — a distinct property **nothing in the corpus has ever checked**, which is exactly why the snapshot exists. And the residual is recorded in ADR-0084 rather than mitigated: a reviewer can rubber-stamp `cargo insta accept`, and the guard against that is convention, not mechanism.

### 9.6 What transfers from `spikes/runner`

The runner is the only Rust this programme has actually run. Nine patterns in it are load-bearing and carry forward by construction — each because a specific failure is otherwise representable, not because it is tidy.

| Pattern | The failure it makes unrepresentable | Where it lands | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Result<_, StepError>` with a **`spoke()`** predicate — only `Schema` means the agent produced a judgement; everything else is silence | RK-B0013: one HTTP 529 produced a **committed `changes-requested` seal** whose entire surviving finding was "review failed to produce a digest", because `!review` conflated "the reviewer said no" with "the reviewer never spoke" | `rk-types` (the enum), `rk-kernel` (the gate evaluator) | [documented, RK-B0013]; **AC-229** pins one closed variant at the port boundary with no catch-all, **AC-230** pins silence as a block with no synthesised findings, **AC-231** pins the empty-findings case as distinguishable from it; **SM-063** instruments the variant mix |
| **Prompt on stdin, never argv** | A Windows command line caps near 32 KB; the `redkiln-review` card alone is 6.6 KB and the fix-lane prompt bodies are larger. Argv works in testing and fails on a real review | `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, direct inspection of `runner.rs`; the TS tree already carried an `--obs-file` workaround for the same cap] |
| **A record is produced even when the step fails** | A dispatch that dies without a usage record — the exact thing inversion exists to end, since cost only becomes visible if failures are priced too | `rk-adapter-claude` → the event log (§7) | [measured, `StepRecord` is written on both arms of the result]; the batch-delta cost model is §7's |
| **Raw payload retained beside the typed view** | A malformed digest and a refusal reading identically. `StepError::Schema` carries `raw`; the final assistant text is kept even when a digest parsed, and an unrecognised stream event is preserved verbatim — stdout and stderr verbatim up to a size cap (then digest + head/tail), argv and environment as digests only, the child environment never (ADR-0105 clause 7) | `rk-types`, `rk-store` | [measured, `runner.rs` / `step.rs`]; the harness area pins verbatim preservation of unknown events, and **AC-230**'s "no synthesised findings" depends on the raw text surviving |
| **Fully-resolved specs — no ambient discovery** | A design leaning on `.claude/` discovery, on a surface documented to change (`--bare` becoming the `-p` default), and on the operator's laptop leaking into the run: F8's default `-p` inherited 12 MCP servers and ~280 tools, MCP alone **4,631 tokens = 56%** of the cache write | `rk-kernel` (resolved activity inputs), `rk-adapter-claude` (`StepSpec`) | [measured, F8; direct inspection of `step.rs`]; ADR-0016 |
| **Structural resume keys** | A retry replaying a stale verdict — the dispatch identity separates attempts — and a resume that reconstructs only what the author predicted it would need | `rk-kernel` (memo key, resume) | [measured, F19 174/174; F38 3/3 across a real `taskkill /T /F`, digests identical]. **Both limits carried:** the FSM was **never replayed**, so "same next action" is unproven and ships as a kernel replay test (§3), and P8's state holds **exactly one step** with its 65-minute and 24-hour arms and all three controls **unrun** (§0.1) |
| **Provenance at dispatch time, including a digest of the child's EFFECTIVE config** | Asking after the fact what the child was actually configured with. An ignored flag looks identical to an honoured one, so isolation is verified from the child's own `system/init`, and the git snapshot (`head`, `branch`, dirty count, porcelain digest) is captured at the instant of dispatch because **it cannot be retrofitted** | `rk-adapter-claude`, the event log (§7) | [measured, F8; `fingerprint.rs` — "a run recorded without it is not a weaker measurement, it is an unusable one"] |
| **Write-path guards enforced by construction** | Telemetry written inside the tree under test, which perturbs the very thing being measured — the journal refuses to open at any path inside a forbidden root, and hygiene assertions void the run **before** dispatch (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` absent, `claude` version floor) | `rk-store` (the single write path, §5), `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, `journal.rs` / `hygiene.rs`]; ADR-0010 and **AC-488** make the API-key check a construction failure, not a warning |
| **`Option` over `0`** where a missing measurement would read as a measured zero | An absent field laundered into a measured zero. F3 read the flat `ephemeral_1h_*`/`ephemeral_5m_*` naming as **absent** from `usage` (the nested object is present on 954/954 runs, PIA-067); a `Some(0)` on a genuinely absent field would make a TTL claim look verified | `rk-types` (usage and cost types), `rk-adapter-claude` | [measured, F3]; **AC-362** discharges it with an `Option` carrying **no `Default` impl**, plus a CI grep rule forbidding `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` on those fields — and §7's SM-112 records the same laundering caught in the measure set, where an unreported overage count had been written up as a measured zero |

### 9.7 Decisions

| Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tokio` in adapter crates only; the kernel names no runtime; ports are `Arc<dyn Port + Send + Sync>` (the **EventStore** port is `Arc<Mutex<dyn EventStore>>`, because `dispatch` takes `&mut dyn EventStore` — **ADR-0115**, narrowing ADR-0099 clause 4) with a root-owned cancel token | `async-trait` on every dyn port; generic (statically dispatched) ports; wait for `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` (nightly-only through 1.98.1, read 2026-09-10) | async-trait puts an executor inside a core whose defining property is deterministic replay, costs a boxed future on the one boundary everything crosses, and does not deliver the cancellation it appears to — killing a `claude -p` tree needs an explicit call either way. Generics are the leak ADR-0003 exists to prevent. Nightly makes the toolchain pin a hostage | ADR-0076, ADR-0099; [documented] native async-fn-in-trait is not dyn-compatible and `async_fn_in_dyn_trait` is nightly-only, 1P async-fundamentals-initiative, fetched 2026-09-08, release notes 1.97.0/1.98.0 fetched 2026-09-10; [measured] F22 |
| Exact `gix = "=0.87.1"`, read path plus the retained clone, `cargo audit` and `cargo deny` in CI watching `gix-*`; an explicit 0.x pin list (`jsonschema`, `rusqlite`, `yaml_serde` exact; `chrono`, `sha2` exempt) | caret range and upgrade on cadence; `git2-rs`/libgit2 for everything; audit at release time only; caret on every 0.x but gix | pre-1.0 + a facade over dozens of independently versioned sub-crates + load-bearing worktree/trailer semantics is exactly what the house 0.x rule is for; the advisories that matter arrive with a **transitive** `gix-*` bump, so a release-time check finds them after they have been merged; libgit2 would take on C-toolchain risk *and* the worktree gap at once | ADR-0077, ADR-0084, ADR-0098, ADR-0100; [documented] RustSec / GitHub Advisory Database + gitoxide `crate-status.md`, fetched 2026-09-08; rustsec.org / osv.dev, fetched 2026-09-10 |
| `clippy::all` denied + an enumerated pedantic subset + nursery at warn, with explicit group priority; `#[expect]` as the only in-source override; `rust-version` equal to the 1.98.1 pin | deny `pedantic`/`nursery` as groups; `#![deny]` per crate; `#[allow]` overrides; a per-crate `[lints]` override; no lint policy (the status quo) | group denies break CI on a clippy bump for reasons unrelated to the code and train contributors to sprinkle `#[allow]`; a per-crate deny puts the policy in a dozen files where divergence is invisible; Cargo rejects a mixed `[lints]` table; `#[expect]` self-retires | ADR-0083, ADR-0102; [documented] 1P Clippy Lints book; Cargo Book "Workspaces"; RFC 3389; cargo #13157 |
| Configuration as a value; `set_var` banned workspace-wide | environment variables as the CLI-native configuration shape (how the spike rig works today) | `set_var` is `unsafe` in edition 2024 because it races `getenv`, and the default test harness is multi-threaded — any test that sets a variable is a latent flake against any test that reads one; it also hides the kernel's inputs from the event log | ADR-0083; [documented] 1P Rust Edition Guide + rust-lang/rust PR #124636 |
| Consolidated integration binaries | one file per case (the Rust-by-Example default) | one file is one binary relinking the whole stack; consolidating Cargo's own suite cut build time 3× and artifact size 5×. Organisation is preserved by modules at no cost | ADR-0083; [measured, external] matklad, on Cargo's suite — direction and mechanism only |
| Golden schema snapshot plus an export transform over authorship discipline; the vendor's 24/16 limits adopted per exported schema | trust the "no `oneOf`" rule to careful authoring; flatten every data-carrying enum to a struct; allow a top-level `oneOf` | the first `enum` with a payload violates it silently — schemars 1.2.2 renders `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` as `oneOf` — and F20 is the measured shape of a closed-output defect failing **open** at exit 0 with `subtype: success` | ADR-0084, ADR-0095; [measured] F20, schemars output at the pin; [documented] 1P docs.rs/schemars; platform.claude.com structured-outputs, fetched 2026-09-10 |

## 10. Phasing, open questions and the spike register

Every unknown named anywhere in §§1–9 appears below as a row with a named spike and a named consumer. That is the discharge mechanism ADR-0012 requires, and it is the only permitted one: a prose hedge does not discharge an unknown in this document. The blocked rows of §0.1 are not re-argued here — they are carried into the phase table as prohibitions and into the register as gating rows.

### 10.1 How to read a phase row

An **entry condition** is a state of the world that can be checked before the phase starts. An **exit criterion** is a command with a pass condition, not a narrative milestone — the proposal's Phase-1 exit read "runs its own fix lane end to end (self-hosting)", and AC-473 is what makes that mechanical: at least one recorded fix-lane run whose event log contains every lane stage from intake to a landed commit, with no stage performed by the old redkiln, and the claim refused if any stage is missing from the log [documented; ADR-0002, ADR-0011]. Where a phase's exit criterion depends on a blocked row, the criterion names the spike and the phase ships the hedge, not the answer.

### 10.2 The phase table

| Phase | Enter when | Scope | Exit criteria, as runnable checks | May not | Evidence / decisions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **0 — close the programme, run the gating spikes** | Now | Commit the working tree; extract `redkiln-corpus` at a pinned SHA; run every register row marked **gates Phase 1** (S-BIN — scoped to the `ChangeSummary` type and the `change_summary` body, [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: S-BIN's row has its finding, F43 (proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*), and its record, ADR-0199 (proposed)] ADR-0093 decision 4 *(noted 2026-09-25, s8a fix round 2, ADR-0165: ADR-0093 decision 4's sentence is superseded in part. `ChangeSummary` is an enum whose `Text` arm is finalised, and S-BIN now blocks the `Binary` arm and its body only.)* — and **S-FACET**, which gates the `require` rows keyed on scored derived facets and `surfaces`, not Phase-1 entry, ADR-0093 decision 3; P7-CEIL is DECIDED as a ceiling, ADR-0093 decision 5; the P1 arm-D panel is done, and P2's **perturbation half is scored** [F40] while its **advisory half is unrun** and **P2-B** — the re-run that would give recall a denominator — is newly registered); run the cheap unblockers (S-GIX/a–c, S-TTL1, **S-PORTS**, **S-GRAMMAR**); re-budget and re-run P8's unrun arms [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-001] | (a) `git status --porcelain` is empty in `redkiln-rs` — measured 2026-09-10 at HEAD `009a4ec`: 0 ` M` and **17 `??`** (driver logs, `spikes/results/p8-state/`, two build directories); everything the figures rest on is tracked, and the residual is the untracked set, not uncommitted edits [measured, PIA-019; corrects the brief §E's 2026-09-09 reading at `ab58733`]. (2) A collaborator with no CLI sees status. (3) Every projection is provably derived: `doctor --rebuild` drops and rebuilds all three from git plus the log (**AC-184**) | Let a projection become authoritative (§8) | ADR-0017; R-LINEAR, R-DOCS feed the port shapes |
| **4 — reach** | Phase 3 stable for a quarter | Codex adapter — **only after S-CODEX passes**; Linear and Astro adapters as demanded | None registered. **AC-239** is PROVISIONAL for exactly this reason: S-CODEX is not reported in the brief | Put a non-Claude adapter in a gate before it passes E3-shaped checks | ADR-0007; AC-239 |

**The Phase-1 gate on P1, stated once and mechanically.** Phase 1 is gated behind AC-071 for anything that touches selection over LLM-emitted facets, and the gate is not a review convention — it is **AC-071**, which fails pack load and CI when any facet is declared `source = "llm"` without a scored stability record. The warrant is F36: the modal `R(stage, ctx)` was identical on only **5/20, 3/20, 2/20 and 2/20** items against an 80% line, the prescribed anchor-rewrite repair did not work, `external_dep` collapsed to a constant (**200/200** false), and three axes with **byte-identical** anchors drifted up to **−0.168** — larger than the effect the alpha<0.6 rule exists to detect, so that rule **is not a decidable predicate on this corpus** [measured, F36; 400 dispatches, 0 void, $21.37]. **The arm-D panel clause is now discharged and the gate is not:** arm D was scored on 2026-09-09 over the 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f` (136 that day, all 140 after the panel-file key fix, E-0089-2) at $0 model cost, panel-adjudicated, and no derived facet clears METHOD-P §1.4's 0.95 bar — `auth` **0/12** [0.00, 0.24] (0.00 [0.00, 0.26] before the key fix), `flag_exists` **0.00** [0.00, 0.66], `binary_change` 0.60 [0.23, 0.88], `change` 0.72 [0.54, 0.85], `toolchain` 0.73 [0.48, 0.89], `ui` 1.00 [0.65, 1.00] whose interval does not clear, and `data_migration` **not measurable** — while the ≥ 0.95 clause is itself unreachable at n=20 for any facet [measured, F39]. That satisfies **AC-071's fourth clause and no other**; the gate now fails on the first, identity ≥ 0.80 against a measured 0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10. Until a record clears it, every ordinal axis is `source = "human"`, supplied or confirmed at the triage gate, and the operator carries the load that **SM-024** measures. `kind` is out of scope of the block (human input, ADR-0030 rule 4). **And the same exit criterion now carries a second, distinct gate**: no facet may declare `source = "derived"` without a named `deriver` and a record for it (six derived facets at the pin, each with a record; `stage`/`rigor`/`role`/`cost` are `kernel`, `surfaces` is `declared`, and AC-520 gates neither — **ADR-0093**), `surfaces` may not key a `require` row until S-FACET reports, and a derived boolean that cannot be computed is `null` rather than `false` (**ADR-0089**, accepted). Nothing in this section amends ADR-0079, which is `superseded` in full by ADR-0090 and which forbade being amended into a decision (corrected 2026-09-15, this read *"which remains `proposed`"* — see §4.1); F39 measures a derived vector, not an LLM-emitted one, and narrows 0079's options without deciding among them.

| Blocked row | The hedge Phase 1 ships | Where it is specified |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Context-vector source, LLM-emitted (ADR-0079) | Human-sourced ordinal facets; the journal records whatever vector was emitted and replay reads the journal, never a re-classification | §4; AC-009, AC-071 |
| Context-vector source, **derived** (ADR-0089; ADR-0093) | `facets.toml`'s `source` gains a **`deriver`** field and no facet may declare `source = "derived"` without a record for it — the closed `source` set is `human \| llm \| derived \| kernel \| declared`, with the four kernel projections and the declared `surfaces` map outside AC-520's gate (ADR-0093); the repo-wide `auth` boolean is withdrawn for a set-valued **`surfaces`** facet over a checked-in `surfaces.toml`, which may be authored and linted and may **not** key a `require` row until **S-FACET** scores it; boolean facets are tri-valued, and an absence of evidence is **`null`** routed to AC-079's `gate_opened { reason: unknown_facet }` rather than a silent `false` | §4; **AC-520** (the gate), AC-072, AC-079, AC-425; **SM-157** |

| gix write path (ADR-0077; ADR-0098) | Hybrid: gix for `rev-parse` (the common-dir read falls back to `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` through the wrapper on an S-GIX/a no-go), status and trailer *parsing*; the shallow clone by SHA is a gix **write** retained deliberately (S-GIX/c); `git commit --trailer` with `-c trailer.ifexists=add`, `interpret-trailers --if-exists=add` and `git worktree add/remove` shell out (ADR-0105 clause 5's write set) | §5 |

The re-run at n ≥ 20 items × ≥ 10 draws per item is **not budgeted here** | **YES** | **Panel DONE 2026-09-09** — F39, 140 truth cells at pin `47faec9f`, all scored after the panel-file key fix `resolve-open-questions` → `resolve-open-question` (136 on 2026-09-09; ERRATA E-0089-2), panel-adjudicated: no facet clears 0.95, `auth` **0/12** [0.00, 0.24] (0.00 [0.00, 0.26] before the key fix), and the bar is unreachable at n=20 (73 all-correct positives needed, not ~60). **AC-071 clause 4 discharged; the re-run is not started** and the block stands on clause 1, identity **0.25 / 0.15 / 0.10 / 0.10** against 0.80 |

Four pre-registered arms: **1** control (the current regex, scored per source), **2** anchored over **paths only** (the cheap fix, run as a control and predicted dead), **3** `surfaces.toml` globs intersected with the changed-file set, **4** declared + call-site, **run only if arm 3's recall is short** | `facets.toml`'s `source`/`deriver` fields for `auth`/`surfaces`, `data_migration`, `ui` and `flag_exists` (the selection crate, `rk-kernel`); **SM-024**'s missing baseline and **SM-157**, derived-facet precision per facet and per source, whose baseline is F39's own table and which reports `data_migration` **not measurable** rather than as a zero; `auth` 0 positives → **NOT ANSWERABLE**; `surfaces` has no label column → **NOT MEASURABLE** on this corpus), and the kill is re-registered on a criterion 20 items can decide — **arm 3 vs arm 1 false-positive count, paired per item**; the ≥ 0.95 bar stays recorded as powerless (73 all-correct positives needed, ERRATA E-0089-2) and is never re-thresholded. Reading: with the fixture's vitest pool uncapped (~20 workers per run), `TempDir::close()` failed with os error 32 **on the root directory only**, contents already gone, and the root was removable after 32–41 ms of 25 ms polls; with the pool capped to one fork, `close()` succeeds immediately and the parent's listing is clear within 1–5 ms (thirteen runs, alone and in the parallel suite). So a killed `vitest` under the job leaves **no handle that survives its processes** — the hold was the dead workers' cwd-handle teardown lagging the process signal by tens of ms, not DECISIONS-2026-09-09 #30's leak (which outlived `Stop-Process`); one unreproduced window of four runs found the empty root still listed after 10 s and removable by a plain `rmdir` afterwards (recorded in the test's module doc, never an unremovable handle). |

| **E3-SEV-BLIND** | A **blind** severity annotation of the preserved `e3-severity-annotation-BLANK.md` by an independent rater — what buys back the κ the 2026-09-04 sheet forfeited (that sheet is filled by the model at Ryan's direction, NOT BLIND, and E3-SEV is DONE on it) | **SM-086**; ADR-0074, ADR-0082; §6.4.4 | **$0** model cost plus Ryan's time | No | **Registered, not taken** (2026-09-11, PIA-102); §0.1's E3 row names this id as the open route |
| **S-GIX/c** | Does `PrepareFetch::with_revision` + `with_shallow` at `gix = "=0.87.1"` produce a checkout whose HEAD tree equals `git clone --depth 1` followed by `git checkout <sha>`'s, byte-compared? The review clone is a gix **write** operation retained deliberately (ADR-0098 decision 2); `with_revision` merged in PR #2824 on 2026-08-05 and is weeks old [documented, github.com/GitoxideLabs/gitoxide, fetched 2026-09-10; ERRATA E-0077-1] | The review-clone path (§6.4.3); `rk-kernel-verify`'s **AC-339** disposable clone; ADR-0098 | One fixture, minutes; **$0** model cost | No — a no-go falls back to the wrapper's `git clone --depth 1` (ADR-0098 option retained) | Registered 2026-09-11 (ADR-0098 decision 3), a third `#[ignore]`d test beside arms a and b; unrun |

Three register rows carry a discipline note rather than a cost. **S-BIN, P1-D and S-FACET are the only three rows that stop Phase-1 work**, and they stop *specific* work — the `ChangeSummary` type and the Toolchain port's `change_summary` body (ADR-0093 decision 4) *(noted 2026-09-25, s8a fix round 2, ADR-0165: ADR-0093 decision 4's sentence is superseded in part. `ChangeSummary` is an enum whose `Text` arm is finalised, and S-BIN now blocks the `Binary` arm and its body only.)*, `require` rows over LLM-emitted facets, and `require` rows over the scored derived facets and `surfaces` (ADR-0093 decision 3) — not the phase. **P7-CEIL** gates the catalog as shipped and is decided (ceiling, ADR-0093 decision 5); **S-GIX/a** gates the read-side owner of `rev-parse` on one branch, and the fallback on that branch is decided (ADR-0098). Everything else in the table is either a hedge already shipping or a label withheld. [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-001, PIA-009]

**Three residual unknowns resolve to a recorded decision rather than to a spike, and are named here so that none escapes the register.** The companion **kind list** — proposal §4.4, its machine-only list and the S-MIG census name different sets — is a runbook decision recorded against ADR-0075 clause 3, and **AC-143** cannot be verified until it is taken (§5.11). **AC-121's cross-OS determinism half and AC-158's property ordering** are unverifiable as specified until the CI shape fixes two OS targets and an order-preserving schema export; both are marked unverified rather than assumed, and §9.3 owns the CI shape. And the retry envelope constants — **`K`, `N`, the backoff base and cap, the two-tick headroom margin and the per-item token appetite** — have no measured value anywhere in the corpus; `K`, `N` and the appetite are declared in the pack and refused as code defaults, the backoff base 2 s and cap 5 min are kernel policy constants (AC-029; PIA-055), the margin is `margin_ticks ≥ 2` refused below that (ADR-0110), and the criteria verify conformance to the declared envelope rather than that the envelope is right (§3.6, §3.10). Consumer: pack authoring (Step 7e). None of the three is a measurement this programme can take, which is why each is a decision with a named home rather than a spike with a cost.

**And a fourth class, enumerated so that no section's "every row resolves to §10" is false.** Nine open items named in §§4.9, 5.11, 6.6 and 7.10 resolve to **a first measurement from the running product**, not to a spike — which is why none carries a cost, and why they appear as one row in §10.4 rather than nine in the priced register: whole-suite p95 per toolchain (**AC-330** / **SM-102**), per-AC pinning coverage (**SM-100**), advisory-sink readership (**SM-089**), the structured-drift rate (**SM-037**), `reconcile()`'s own cost (**SM-049**), lattice scaling at 2× catalog size (**SM-025**), delivered-artifact quality-block coverage (**AC-112** / **SM-032**), per-activity usage over a rolling 90 days (**SM-036**), and `doctor`'s unread-artifact window `N` (**AC-426** / **AC-472** / **SM-139**, whose target cannot be missed until `N` is declared). Each ships the hedge its own section states; none is a spike, and none is load-bearing for a decision taken here. **AC-114's ship/extend decision id** is a tenth of the same kind, except that its discharge is a criterion to be authored in the AC register's packs-and-catalog section (`acceptance-criteria.md` §4) rather than a measurement.

### 10.6 What would falsify this design

Each is a specific result from a named check, and each names what would have to be rewritten. None is hypothetical hand-wringing: every one is a spike already in §10.5.

| The result | Named check | What it forces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`doctor --rebuild` does not reproduce the same next action from git alone.** F19 proved suspension lossless 174/174 but **explicitly left "same next action" unproven — the FSM was never replayed** [measured, F19], and P8's state holds exactly one step [measured, F38]. This is the largest untested assumption in the document | Phase-1 exit (2), **AC-184** / **SM-124** | The tier-1/tier-2 split test ("if the database were deleted, would `redkiln run` take a different next action?") does not hold, and the store layout, the memo key and the replay contract (ADR-0021, ADR-0022) are rewritten. **§3 and §5 both fall** |
| **Arm D scores poorly, and a human-sourced facet set proves too costly to author.** **The first half has now happened**: arm D was scored on 2026-09-09 and **no derived facet clears 0.95** — `auth` **0.00** (0/12 [0.00, 0.24] after the key fix), `flag_exists` 0.00, `data_migration` not measurable — while the bar itself is unreachable at n=20 (73 all-correct positives needed) [measured, F39]. That is a **partial realisation of this falsifier, not the whole of it**: the second half is unmeasured, because SM-024 has no baseline and the four abstaining items on twenty are a property of an oracle-construction exercise rather than a forecast for a shipped triage gate | **P1-D** (panel done; the identity re-run outstanding), **S-FACET** for the derived replacement, then **SM-024** human-supplied facet load | If S-FACET's declared boundary map also fails to clear at an absorbable abstention rate, the `require`-row mechanism keyed on a per-item context vector has **no viable computed source at all** and those facets route to ASKED — a legitimate pre-registered result, with the gate load carried openly by SM-024. If the gate load then proves unaffordable, §4's selection function must be replaced by something that does not need a per-item vector, and ADR-0023 and ADR-0030 are reopened. What F39 has already forced is narrower and is recorded rather than deferred: ADR-0089 |
| **The short-circuit's refinement is worse than the map it refines, and step 3's `Stale` verdict is noise on the tool's own bookkeeping.** **The first half has now happened**: on the 33 rows both scopes scored, the **reconstructed** (fine) `derived_from` marks **15** `Stale` against the **declared** (coarse) map's **5**, with **10 rows flipping `Present` → `Stale` and none flipping back**, and specificity **0.55** [0.38, 0.70] against **0.73** [0.58, 0.84] — the reconstruction picks the card up as a derivation edge because the CLI has rewritten it more recently than anything else, and so fires on two artifacts the declared map does not connect to the card at all. What has **not** happened is the detection half: **0 of 41 rows carry a real upstream change**, so nothing measured says either scope catches staleness [measured, F40] | **P2-B**, **SM-021** | Criterion 1's prescribed remedy — fine scope mandatory for `L`-class — cannot be adopted, and **`stale_policy` becomes an explicit decision rather than a default** (§0.1). If P2-B then shows the declared map misses real staleness too, step 3's `Stale` stops being a short-circuit at all and `stale_policy = gate` is the only safe setting for `L`/`XL`, which is what METHOD-P §2.4's criterion 2 was written to decide and cannot |
| **S-BIN: the reviewer cannot verify ACs or catch a seeded regression on a no-diff item** | **S-BIN** | `ChangeSummary` is not a type problem but an evidence problem. The review gate's applicability to binary-asset toolchains is withdrawn, ADR-0008's Phase-2 Unreal adapter loses its premise, and the five toolchain-keyed kinds (ADR-0073) are re-cut |
| **S-GIX/a: the common-dir/work-tree scenario cannot be made to work reliably even by shelling out** (the decided no-go fallback, `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` through the wrapper, also fails) | **S-GIX/a** | One store per repository at `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`/redkiln/ shared by every worktree is not implementable as specified, and §5's store location and the parallel-worktree model in §7 are both rewritten |
| **P8-B: mid-graph resume outside the TTL costs more than a stage gate on `cache_creation_input_tokens`, against a real within-experiment baseline** | **P8-B**, **SM-073** | The pricing guidance per METHOD-P §5.4 changes — not the suspend/resume shape, which rests on losslessness (ADR-0106; placement is not derived from a cost model in either direction). Note the current state: 3 runs, cost ordering **UNMEASURED** (the sub-1× output-token ratios first quoted here divided unlike counters, E-0087-1; on the like counter 4.57× / 2.66× / 2.52×, cross-fixture), all three **inside** F10's ~1 h TTL, with no within-experiment baseline [measured, F38] |
| **S-SUBTLE-CB: detection collapses under prior-approval framing** | **S-SUBTLE-CB**, **SM-093** | The review gate cannot be run on a change carrying a prior verdict — which is exactly the resume-after-gate path. §6's gate composition, and the order in which the two gates run, are rewritten. The external prior is a **16–93 percentage-point** detection drop on 5 of 6 models under strong bug-free framing, with false positives collapsing alongside [documented, arXiv 2603.18740 v2, fetched 2026-09-10] |
| **S-TTL1: the subagent bucket's tier cannot be read on this rig** (ADR-0113 narrowed it; the `-p` bucket is read) (the nested object is present on 954/954 runs, so this falsifier now targets the billing-mode confound — a subscription drawing on usage credits silently forces 5 m) | **S-TTL1** | The TTL is unverifiable at this pin. The split cache rule survives (it rests on F16 and S-WT, not on the TTL), but every cache-cost figure in §7 becomes a point estimate at an unknown TTL and the gate-cost model loses its time axis |
| **The catalog lint cannot be made monotone in internal quality** — the `bisect` ceiling (P7-CEIL, DECIDED) fails the lattice re-run at Step 7e, or a second violation appears that no ceiling removes | **P7-CEIL**'s lattice re-run, **SM-035** | The ordinal rigor enum does not do the job asked of it, and the closed-enum risk the Dagster RFC documents has materialised: a small enum that could not compose. ADR-0081 is reopened and rigor becomes composable primitives |

One falsifier is deliberately absent, and its absence is the point. **No result from any spike above can make this design "final" on its own**: P8 must return a result, S-BIN and S-H1c must run, and S-P7B carries P7's design-final gate as a gate on the quality-shorthand default only, released otherwise (METHOD-P amendment 15; ADR-0080 decisions 1–4, ADR-0093 decision 5) — P2 sets a parameter and gates no phase (PIA-094); until then no document, section or commit message may use the word. That prohibition is not a stylistic preference — it is the direct consequence of F38 having no result on its own question, with its third criterion **circular by construction**.

### 10.x Review of this document

*(This subsection is the dated record of the 2026-09-08 passes. The pre-implementation audit of 2026-09-10 — 110 concerns, absorbed on 2026-09-11 under ADR-0092–ADR-0111 — superseded several of its readings; where a bullet below is now stale it carries a dated note, and the citation corrections are collected in §12. The audit's own record is `references/2026-09-10-pre-implementation-audit.md`.)*

This document was put through **two adversarial critic passes on 2026-09-08**, run against the same evidence brief, the same thirteen area files (session scratch, never committed; the register is the owner — ADR-0092) and the same `spikes/` records it is built from. One pass looked only for **what is missing** — material that exists in the corpus and silently failed to arrive here. The other looked only for **what overclaims** — numbers that drifted, saturated metrics reported as wins, powerless criteria reported as verdicts, bad denominators, and corrected claims that crept back under a new word. Both passes are recorded here because a document that holds every spike to a written limitation owes the same account of itself.

**What the overclaim pass found, and what was corrected.** Everything it raised is fixed; a wrong number has no upside.

| Class | The defect | The correction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drifted figure | AC-071's unblocking bar was written **≥ 5 draws per item** in §4.1, §4.9 and §10.2 exit (6). AC-071's own executable precondition reads `rec.draws_per_item >= 10`. **Load-bearing:** arm L already ran 20 × 10 and the kill fired 4/4, so the design's bar could have been cleared by a re-run at half the draws that produced the kill | Corrected to **≥ 10** at all three sites, with the release condition now stated once, in AC-071's five clauses, in §0.1 |
| Wrong predicate | §5.2 clause 1 and three other sites stated the 19-of-153 figure under **two different predicates** — "neither GWT nor a verification note" and "no GWT **or** no verification note" | Harmonised to AC-137's own disjunctive wording, with the per-AC sub-counts carried, at all four sites — *2026-09-11: the sub-counts were cap-truncated; they read 76 / 87 loss lines (E-0075-1)* |
| Conflated denominator | §5.2 clause 6 read the three reclassified kinds against the spec-section slot's 98% "(which averages *over* these three)". Spec sections is its own S-MIG slot and contains none of them | Restated against the census overall, as §8.7 had it — *2026-09-11: "the overall 98%" was the spec-section slot; the record-weighted typed-slot overall is 96.0% (E-0075-1), and both sites now say so* |
| Number outside its source | §8.4's "13 real / 34 false [measured, F32, **10 activities**]" — the corpus size is in `spikes/F32-DRAFT.md`, not in the brief's F32 row | Cited from the draft directly, and labelled as such |
| Laundered tag | §4.5's `~95`-activity ceiling shared a `[measured, F35/F34]` tag with three figures that are measured | Split: **~95 is ADR-0027's own approximate split of 267 raw rows, [documented], a planning estimate** |
| A price twice denied | §7.8 priced the `mandatory` arm at **$9.86** while §1.2 and §10.4 both state that no mandatory-tier price is quoted | **The price is struck.** The row now records why: dropped at n=1, and DECISIONS #7/#33 record it as pricing an unreachable configuration |
| Saturation laundering | F26's 20/20 was used as confirmation in the table headed “Settled”; §1.2 called the same result a prediction “**falsified twice**”; F31's 52/52 and 0/23 were quoted six times with no ceiling/floor label; §0.1 and §1.1 read a tie at ceiling as “not worse” and “nothing measurable was lost” | Every use now carries its label. **Four cells at ceiling confirm nothing**; the harder-tier prediction **could not be tested**, it was not falsified (principle 1 forbids exactly that move); and the non-inferiority language is replaced by “**no difference was detectable, and the instrument could not have detected one**” |
| Powerless criterion as verdict | “Changed the root cause on **1 item in 10**” appeared under §4.7's heading “What *is* citable”, and twice more. It is one cell (`issue-81`, 0/3 vs 1/3) moved by one draw of three — the powerless criterion's output. §6.3.6 said P8 “**falsified**” the mid-graph pricing assumption on three underpowered draws with no baseline | The 2.64× and the 33/33 stay citable — a cost and a constant predicate. **The 1-in-10 is removed from the citable set** and named as the criterion's output. §6.3.6 now reads “**untested, not falsified**”, and states that nothing there establishes mid-graph placement is safe |
| Bad denominator | §6.4.2's flat rule “CLEAN is never used as a denominator anywhere” was contradicted inside its own paragraph and in §6.4.4's telemetry table; the upper-bound label §1.2 requires “at the point of use” was absent at six sites; SM-086's baseline was stated as **0/23** in the sentence defining the denominator as adjudicated blocks only — and F31 adjudicated **14/14** | The rule is restated as it is actually kept: **CLEAN is never a denominator for an accuracy or false-positive-rate claim**, and the fractions are block counts. Upper-bound labels added at all six sites. SM-086 now records that **no compliant baseline exists** — the adjudicated reading is 0 of 14 |
| Blocked, quietly decided | §6.5.4 relaxed the per-AC mutation norm for the whole `binary` AC class, on the one class the document says has no evidence of any kind; §7.1 said `cache_profile` has two legal values and shipped a third in the next sentence; §7.8 set and priced a lane default three lines from §7.10's row calling it BLOCKED | The binary-class evidence standard is now **BLOCKED on S-BIN, not provisional-but-specified**. [S-BIN reported (s8i) 2026-10-14: F43 and ADR-0199, both proposed *(dated 2026-10-15, `s8i` close, D21-154: F43 and ADR-0199 accepted)*; ADR-0199 decision 5 keeps AC-337's `not-feasible(reason)` route with AC-357's human gate as the evidence, and grants no AC-334 exemption] `cache_profile` has two values and **no shippable third**. §7.8 names **ADR-0015** as the tier authority and states explicitly that it is **not** the ADR-0080 shorthand |
| Single draw as a rate | F28's flat gate cost was stated as duration-invariance at six sites and carried its n at one; §0.1 and principle 11 quoted **29,619–29,680** as a range — two single draws from two different spikes, one of which has no finding number; §8.7 declared a ≥95% line falsified while declining to compute any interval | “One draw per duration point” now travels with every F28 use. The two static-prompt draws are quoted **separately, with their spikes and n=1**. The Wilson interval on 134/153 is computed and stated — **81.4–91.9%, which excludes 95%** — because falsifying a line is an interval claim |
| Crept-back claim | “**Account-wide** subsidy” survived the withdrawal of “org-scoped” in three places, while §7.2 of this same document says there is **no account-wide shared head**; §1.1 cited the then-contested AC-387 cell as authority for a settled “~31%” worktree price; F21's superseded 0.077 was forbidden by a rule that quoted it | “Account-wide” becomes “**shared-prefix**” at all three sites. §1.1 no longer prices worktree cold-start; the S-WT prompt attribution was marked contested at all three earlier uses — *2026-09-11: resolved by the run record, `spikes/results/s-wt.jsonl` (a cardless one-line static prompt, n = 9); S-WT-REC is DONE and the cells are priced separately*. The F21 prohibition now reads “may not be quoted **as a value**” |
| Label carried too late | Cloudflare's $0.98 was asserted flat in §7.8 and flagged unreconciled 730 lines later | The unreconciled label is carried **at the point of use** |

Two of the overclaim pass's own readings were checked and **not** adopted as written. It read the 19-of-153 predicate as needing the *conjunctive* form restored; AC-137's text is the disjunctive one, so the correction runs the other way and the three “neither…nor” sites were changed instead. And it flagged “[measured, F32, 10 activities]” as a fabricated corpus size; the figure is real and is in `F32-DRAFT.md`, so it is cited from the draft rather than dropped. Both are corrections either way — the defect was the source of the number, not the number.

**What the completeness pass found.** Eight items had existed in the corpus and disappeared from this document entirely. Every one is now registered with a hedge, a spike and a named consumer, per ADR-0012 — none is closed by prose:

| Silent disappearance | Where it now lives |
| --- | --- |
| **8 of 23 corpus items sit outside S0.1** — a third of the corpus unmeasured by the gate FPR, while F11 is quoted as the warrant for the verification gate in four sections | §1.2 register row; a scope clause on **every** F11 citation; **S0.1-SCOPE** in §10.4/§10.5 |
| **E0 per-item stability** — no reviewer has been run twice on one item, so every 23/23 is one sample of a stochastic process | §1.2 row; **E0-VAR** in §10.4/§10.5, with E-REST as its programme home |
| **Equivalent-seed triage** (METHOD-E3 §10) never applied — the seed set the non-inferiority result rests on is untriaged | §1.2 row; **E3-SEED**, $0, §10.5 |
| **The in-session side of the cache asymmetry** — the programme's own observation of subagents on a **1 h, not 5 m** cache, opposite in sign to F3 | §7.3's contradiction box, now two-sided; the charter-E1 row of §1.2 |
| **The instrument lineage break** — 2.1.259 → 2.1.263 → 2.1.265 mid-P7, catching exactly `recommended`'s r3 draw; **the arms are not instrument-matched** | §1.2 row; the P7 rows of §7.8; §0.1's quality-shorthand row; **the pin's value (2.1.263) is now stated** at AC-423 and §9.2 |
| **The P-series remainders** — four supplementary packs unaccepted, the v0 pack untagged, the `optional`-override mode not a real reading | §4.6's pack-count discipline; two §4.9 rows; **P6-PACKS** in §10.4/§10.5 |
| **`forge-fix.js`'s absent-verdict-reads-as-approval defect**, and whether the Workflow runtime shares it — one dispatch to settle | **D-FIXCONSUMER** in §10.4/§10.5; and the three completed Charter §14 deliverables that carry it (the Run 3 scorecard, the RFC amendment, findings-for-product) are now rows in §11 |
| **The Windows handle a killed `vitest` leaves that no `Stop-Process` releases** — counter-evidence to reading AC-224 as “nothing survives” | §3.8.2 beside AC-224 and SM-068; §7.7's parallelism table; **S-CANCEL-WIN** |

Four spikes had a consumer but no register row, and one open item had **no spike at all** in violation of ADR-0012. All are registered: **S-WT-REC** (the S-WT run record — §7.9 called it the most consequential defect in the area and §7.10 falsely claimed it appeared in §10; recovered 2026-09-11), **P1-BOUND** (the boundary-distance diagnostic §4.1 claimed was “carried in the spike register” and was not), **S-CASCADE** (cascade recall loss, which §6.6 recorded as “No spike registered”), and the `system/api_retry` payload capture, now folded into **S-RETRY** with AC-026/AC-232 named. Nine further section-level open items that promised a §10 row are enumerated in §10.5's closing paragraph as **first measurements from the running product** rather than spikes — which is why they carry no cost. R-LINEAR and R-DOCS now name criteria (AC-507/AC-508, AC-510) rather than components.

Five uncovered-criteria gaps mattered enough to fix in place. The **harness area** (AC-200–AC-239) had no design section at all and was discharged in one sentence — it now has §3.6.1, which names every subject and surfaces two reconciliations it forces (AC-218 against §8.1's AC-428; the `--bare`/API-key rule living in three areas, which is the same collision §8.8 records). The register's git-reconcile section's **merge queue and worktree lifecycle** (AC-189–AC-198) was uncovered in full and now has a subsection under §7.7, where the policy had been stated with one criterion between four rules. §8.4 now states that **two areas own a doctor rule table** and that only the cli-doctor half is reconciled. §5.4 and §7.4 now cite the tier-2 half of the register's events-and-cost section. And §4.1 now pins AC-069/AC-070/AC-073/AC-074/AC-079 — including the decision-memory replay mechanism the operator-load hedge actually rests on.

Four unactioned research items are now acted on rather than dropped: Kiro-style `fileMatch` conditional inclusion is **workspace-scoped and cannot model cross-initiative atoms** (§8.6, §3.5's memo key now hashes the *resolved* atom set); Atlassian's *Agents in Jira* ships a native approval step, so the differentiation claim was narrowed to the tree-hash binding inside an AI-coding-specific tool (§6.3.2) — *2026-09-11: narrowed again, since spec-kit, Archon and Bernstein ship persisted human gates; the claim that holds is the per-kind stage machine plus the tree-hash binding (PIA-090)*; the **Dagster RFC's closed-policy risk** is now cited where the ordinal enum is decided, not only where it is falsified (§4.7, §4.8); and the withdrawn “four or more workers” threshold and “current production consensus” claims are **recorded as withdrawn** in §2.2 rather than having vanished. §6.3.2 named the Azure Durable Functions row it could not read (*read 2026-09-10: no token, no built-in deadline, at-least-once — PIA-085*), §3.7 names LangGraph as unread rather than excluded, §1.4 stated that the AC-###-names-its-instrument convention and the BLOCKED-AC phrasing are original process design (*withdrawn 2026-09-11: both specialise NASA SP-2016-6105 Appendices C/D and ISO/IEC/IEEE 29148 — PIA-086*), and §11's literature preamble stated the positive gap (*narrowed 2026-09-11: agentic workflow optimisation and Situational Method Engineering are the adjacent fields; no SDLC-tailoring system was found — PIA-092*).

**What was deliberately left, and why.**

- **Twenty SCHEMA-series ADRs (ADR-0032, 0034, 0035, 0039, 0044, 0048–0052, 0054, 0055, 0060, 0065, 0066, 0068–0071) are still not reflected individually.** They fix the activity-graph schema's *grammar*, whose home is `references/2026-09-07-activity-graph-schema.md`. A design document that restated a predicate grammar it does not own would create a second source of truth for a spec that already has one, and the two would diverge on the first amendment. **What was wrong was that they were invisible, not that they were absent** — so §4.5 now names all twenty in five groups with their substance in one line each, and the range citation `ADR-0031–ADR-0073` is no longer the only place a reader can find them. ADR-0072 was the exception and **is** now reflected, as the shipped-activity version-and-changelog rule, because it is a catalog-lifecycle decision rather than grammar. ADR-0058 and ADR-0041 are now cited at the places their substance is used (§4.6/§4.8 and §4.5's typed-exits row respectively, the latter with the ADR-0064 narrowing named).
- **The P3 tail of uncited criteria — roughly forty ids across eight areas — is left uncited.** §3.6's rule stands: the register owns the criteria (`references/2026-09-08-acceptance-criteria.md`; the area files were session scratch and never committed — ADR-0092 decision 2) and this document cites rather than restates them. Citing every id would convert this document into an index of the AC set without adding a decision. The cases that were fixed were fixed because a *section stated a rule with no instrument* (the merge queue, the harness contract, step 6 of the selection function) or because *two areas own the same rule* (the doctor tables, the `--bare` assertion) — both of which are defects in this document. A criterion that simply has no section citing it is not.
- **§8.8's AC-511 id conflict** was carried unresolved by the 2026-09-08 pass; *2026-09-11: it is settled by the register's §0.4 hazard table — AC-511 is ports-dist's, one of six cut ids re-issued under a hazard table, licensed by ADR-0092 decision 3 — and every "unresolved" note in this document is withdrawn (PIA-036). The register, not an area file, owns the id.*
- **The DECISIONS-2026-09-09 “reverse or confirm first” table is still open**, and #15's `human_authored` remap still changes what the advisory fraction measures. **D-RECON** carries it; nothing in §§1–9 rests on an unreversed entry.
- **No new measurement was taken.** Every correction above is a re-reading of records that already existed. Where a figure was wrong, it was wrong in this document and right in its source; where a figure is missing, it is still missing, and it is now in §10 rather than in nobody's hands.

**What the two passes together say about this document's method.** The overclaim pass found twelve classes of defect and **not one of them was a fabricated number** — every figure traced to a real record. What failed was the *labelling*: a ceiling read as a confirmation, a powerless criterion read as an effect, a wrong denominator carried past the sentence that forbade it, a corrected claim surviving under a synonym. That is the same failure mode this document's own principle 1 exists to catch, and it caught none of them unaided. The completeness pass found the mirror: material that was correct where it was written and simply never arrived, most of it from `spikes/HANDOFF.md` — the one source in §11 that is a **handover note rather than a finding**, and therefore the one with no F-number to make its absence conspicuous. Both failure modes are cheap to introduce and expensive to notice, which is the argument for running a pass like this again before any successor document is written, not after.

---

## 11. Sources

Built from what §§1–10 actually cite. Nothing is listed that no section used, and nothing a section used is omitted. Where a source is named in the document but could not be resolved to a fetchable record, the row says so rather than dropping it — an unverified provenance is a finding, not a tidying job (§10.4, **R-MUT**). Platform and library rows carry the fetch date because a version list has an expiry: **ADR-0084 forbids citing its own version list as a current-versions source after 2026-09-08**, and this section inherits that expiry. Rows re-fetched on 2026-09-10 by the pre-implementation audit's grounding census say so; every correction to a row below is collected in **§12**.
