### Literature and prior art

**The nearest fields, stated before the table** [amended 2026-09-11, PIA-092; ERRATA E-0081-1]. With the qualifier "LLM-driven, per-run, from internal quality signals" restored, the research pass's "no named subfield" does not hold: **agentic workflow optimisation** (arXiv 2603.22386, 2026-03-23 — AFlow, G-Designer, FlowReasoner, Workflow-R1 — frames per-run selection and generation of workflow steps as its research question, over generic agent graphs) and **Situational Method Engineering** (Henderson-Sellers, Ralyté, Ågerfalk, Rossi, Springer 2014 — the OPF deontic matrix ADR-0020 already cites) are the adjacent fields; **no SDLC-tailoring system was found** (arXiv 2509.13942 uses fixed Waterfall/V/Agile scaffolds) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10]. So the sources below anchor individual mechanisms — instability, judge bias, rank reversal, multi-agent scaling — and the two adjacent fields anchor the framing, not the shape of the thing as a whole. Where this document's conventions specialise documented practice rather than invent it, §1.4 says so.

| Source | What it contributes | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **arXiv 2603.04417**, *Same Input, Different Scores* (Feb 2026) — GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, Gemini-2.5-Flash, Claude-Haiku-4.5, Claude-Sonnet-4.5 | "Despite expectations of stability at temperature=0, substantial variability persisted across models"; temperature=0 had limited or inconsistent effect for the Anthropic models | §0.1, §1.3, §2 (principle 3), §4.1 |
| **arXiv 2604.22411**, *Introducing Background Temperature* (Apr 2026) | **Claude-Sonnet-4 measured at T_bg = 0**, perfectly deterministic in their harness. That this places P1's instability on the schema/prompt side is **this design's inference** — neither this paper nor 2603.04417 states a schema-induced mechanism (full texts fetched 2026-09-10, PIA-020) | §0.1, §1.3, §4.1 |
| **arXiv 2511.01176** (Zhu, Yoshida, Choi; code-clone classification) | Asserts as an **unmeasured premise** (§5.2) that instability correlates with proximity to the decision boundary — the basis of P1-BOUND's bucketing, which therefore rests on a premise, not a result; what it measures is consistency over five identical submissions and a small temperature effect, the reason **a temperature spike is explicitly not the answer** (PIA-020) | §4.1, §4.9 |
| **arXiv 2606.13685** (LLM-judge stability) | Coding items at **23.6%** instability in the high-flip stratum; a single trial matches a 50-trial majority only **86.6%** of the time — external consonance for removing the verdict channel | §1.1 |
| **arXiv 2603.18740** v2, *Measuring and Exploiting Contextual Bias in LLM-Assisted Security Code Review* — controlled experiment over **250 CVE vulnerability–patch pairs** (full text fetched 2026-09-10) | Strong bug-free framing cuts vulnerability detection by **16–93 percentage points on 5 of 6 models** (Opus 4.5 −4.9 pp, n.s.; weak framing 0.8–24.7 pp), and the mechanism is a **whole-classifier shift toward "clean"** — false negatives rise and **false positives collapse** (+46 to +95 pp true negatives). The mirror risk this programme has never measured; the warrant for ADR-0101's unrun prior-approval framing arm and the reason the symmetric arm is registered (S-SUBTLE-SYM) rather than rejected (PIA-013) | §1.2, §2 (principle 12 caution), §6.4.5, §10.4, §10.6 |
| **arXiv 2609.04167**, **SWE-Gate** — review constraints derived from real PR comments, 303 repair instances across 75 Python repos | **221 of 644 (34%)** functionally-*passing* agent repairs fail those constraints. A second, fresher anchor for "green ≠ pinned" — a **different quantity** from anything in this corpus, and never a rate this design inherits | §2 (principle 12), §6.2, §6.4.4 |
| **arXiv 2603.00539**, Haolin Jin & Huaming Chen — *Are LLMs Reliable Code Reviewers?* **One paper, not two** | Rationale-heavy prompting drives false rejection (GPT-4o FNR **26–36% → 58–88%**); the fix-guided verification filter (§7.2 / Table 4) recovers 54.8%→16.3% HumanEval, 69.0%→28.9% MBPP, 51.0%→24.0% QuixBugs. **The provenance correction matters**: counting it as two entries overstated the independent replication behind ADR-0074 | §6.4.5 |
| **arXiv 2512.08296** (Google Research/DeepMind — **not "Google/MIT"**; quoted figures are the original 180-config / 4-benchmark version, not the Apr 2026 v3's 260 configs / 6 benchmarks) | Sequential multi-agent decomposition **−39% to −70%**, parallel **+80.9%**, error amplification **17.2×** independent vs **4.4×** centralised | §2 (principle 9), §7.7 |
| **arXiv 2606.13003** | Automatic multi-agent systems "consistently underperform CoT-SC despite being up to **10×** more expensive" across six frameworks | §2 (principle 9) |
| **Wang & Luo 2009** (ScienceDirect S0895717708002860 — SAW and TOPSIS) · **Aires & Ferreira 2018** · quoted by **arXiv 2312.03006** · **García-Cascales & Lamata 2012** (S0895717711007850, *On rank reversal and TOPSIS method*, TOPSIS-only; full text unread — ScienceDirect returned 403 on 2026-09-10) [peer] | **Rank reversal occurs for virtually every weighted MCDM method**, TOPSIS and SAW included — the general result, so "just use TOPSIS" is pre-answered; the sentence originates in arXiv 2312.03006 citing Wang & Luo and Aires & Ferreira, and the PII first pinned here is the TOPSIS-specific paper (re-pinned 2026-09-11, PIA-098). Why rigor is an ordinal deontic enum and is never summed | §0.1, §2 (principle 14), §4.7, §4.8 |
| **NASA SP-2016-6105 Rev 2** — Appendix C (TBD/TBR items: rationale, owner, closure date) and Appendix D (Requirements Verification Matrix, Table D-1 p. 241; Table 4.2-2 p. 70) · **ISO/IEC/IEEE 29148** (per-requirement verification-method attributes and traceability) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; added 2026-09-11, PIA-086] | The antecedents the AC-### + `depends_on` / `tracesTo` convention specialises (a criterion names its verifying instrument — the norms name a method class, not a test id) and the PROVISIONAL / BLOCKED AC phrasing extends; "no documented norm" is withdrawn (E-0085-1) | §1.4 |
| **Mavin et al., EARS**, IEEE RE'09 [peer] | EARS single-trigger phrasing as closer to machine-checkable than Gherkin — a **phrasing preference with no enforcement and no measurement on this corpus**; the AC *type* stays permissive because the census requires it | §5.2 (clause 8), §8.7 |
| **SHOP2** ordered-method discipline (HTN planning prior art, named in the catalog proposal) | Lowest `method_rank` wins and **a tie is a validation error**, never a runtime choice | §4.4, §4.8 |
| **Cloudflare**, *Orchestrating AI Code Review at scale* (blog.cloudflare.com/ai-code-review/) — 131,246 review runs across **48,095 merge requests** over 30 days | Overall average **$1.19**; **pooled** median **$0.98**; full tier **mean $1.68 / median $1.47**; "break glass" **288 of 48,095 = 0.6%** — the only public false-block comparator this pass found, used as an order of magnitude and never as a threshold. **[Editorial note: the evidence brief labels the $0.98 figure as the *read-only tier* median; §7.8's footnote, from the 2026-09-08 re-fetch, labels it a median *pooled across all tiers*. Both readings are carried; the discrepancy is in the label, not the figure, and it is unreconciled here.]** | §4.9, §6.3.4, §6.4.4, §7.8 |
| **Mergify**, *State of Merge Queues 2026* [vendor] — 200,000+ merges across 477 teams | AI-assisted PRs broke main **1.9%** vs **4.4%** for non-AI **when routed through a merge queue** — the reduction appears to come from the queue, not from the agent being safer | §7.7 |
| **augmentcode.com** [blog] | Worktree-per-task as the 2026 default for parallel coding agents, and that a worktree buys **file-level isolation only** | §7.7 |
| **Agentless vs CodeR** benchmark comparison; **Anthropic Code Review** at $15–25 per review | External cost orientation only: **$0.70/instance at 32.00%** against **$3.34 at 28.33%** — cheaper *and* better, a warning against pricing rigor by spend alone | §7.8 |
| **git-branchless issue #1334** | A `reference-transaction` hook cannot be made atomic and forces full-log replays — why **no git hooks feed the store**. ADR-0018's own note records that #1334 "is about hooks, not worktrees" | §5.7, §5.10 |
| **Backlog.md** (issue **#558**; and Backlog.md + Archon as the two standalone binaries that got adopted) | Store ownership checked rather than assumed; the durable-artifact-plus-skill/MCP-layer shape behind the plugin surface — **borrowed evidence, PROVISIONAL** | §2 (principle 19), §5.7, §5.10 |
| **spec-kit** (a gate step) · **Archon** (an approval node) · **Bernstein** (approval gates — human or automated is not settled by its README; next-pass check list) · **Atlassian, Agents in Jira** (native workflow Approval step, GA May 2026) [documented, fetched 2026-09-10; added 2026-09-11, PIA-090] | Persisted human gates exist in AI-coding tools, so the novelty claim is the per-kind stage machine plus the tree-hash binding, not the gate | §6.3.2 |
| **Nx** (targets vs executors) · **Tekton** (Pipelines vs Tasks) · **Cloud Native Buildpacks** (ordered detect-and-contribute) · **GitHub reusable workflows vs composite actions** | Convergent prior art for the two-axis pack split: one layer owns structure, the other binds and contributes at named extension points | §4.2 |
| **Dagster RFC** on closed enums | The named risk if the rigor ladder cannot be made monotone: a small enum that could not compose | §10.6 |
| **Symphony** — named as the anti-pattern | Tracker-as-state-machine, rejected: projections are derived and never authoritative | §8.9 |
| **matklad.github.io** [blog, measured on Cargo's own suite by its author] · **swatinem.de** ("Should I pin my Rust toolchain version?", 2025-02-15) · **nickb.dev** · **debasishg.github.io** · **dtolnay/anyhow README** ("Comparison to thiserror") and **docs.rs/thiserror** [1P crate author] · **Microsoft Rust guidelines M-APP-ERROR / M-ERRORS-CANONICAL-STRUCTS / M-DI-HIERARCHY / M-MOCKABLE-SYSCALLS / M-AVOID-WRAPPERS** (fetched 2026-09-10) | Consolidated integration binaries (build time **3×**, artifact size **5×** — direction and mechanism only, **not** a predicted figure for this workspace); "pin the toolchain as a dependency and make updates intentional" (the binary-versus-library and pin-versus-floor framing is RFC 3537's, not the blog's — PIA-023); feature-unification leakage under resolver v2; dyn async in practice; the `thiserror`/`anyhow` split — **documented by the crate author and vendor-corroborated with one named divergence**, with the stricter public-API rule as this project's own (PIA-079; the OneUptime and Pi Stack blogs this row first cited are superseded as sources); the DI ranking this design diverges from on a stated ground (PIA-045) | §3.3, §3.8.1, §9.3, §9.4 |
| *State of Mutation Testing at Google*, ICSE-SEIP 2018 — **named as an unverified provenance candidate only** | The possible origin of "median one live mutant per changelist" and "70% of high-priority bugs". Both arXiv PDF mirrors returned undecodable content, so **neither figure is quoted anywhere in this document**; the verification gate rests on **F11's own measurement**. Discharged as **R-MUT** | §10.4 |

## 12. Errata (2026-09-11)

The pre-implementation audit of 2026-09-10 (`references/2026-09-10-pre-implementation-audit.md`; `references/audit/2026-09-10/MASTER.json`, 110 concerns) re-fetched every external citation this document carries and re-derived every restated number. Where a `[1P]` or `[documented]` tag rested on a source that said something else, or a `[measured]` figure did not reproduce from its record, the correction is made in place with a dated `[amended 2026-09-11, PIA-0NN]` marker and collected here so that no correction has to be found by diffing. Every row names the corrected fact and its source with its fetch date; a corrected figure keeps the tag its source earns, never a stronger one (ADR-0092). Corrections to frozen ADR evidence lines are `references/adr/ERRATA.md` (E-NNNN-k); this section is the design's own. Rows 1–29 cover the 38 rows of the audit's grounding census for this file (`references/audit/2026-09-10/census/grounding-by-file/references__2026-09-08-architecture-design.md.json`; claim ids that share a site are merged into one row and every id is named); rows 30 onward are citation and number corrections the concern remediations required.

**Rows the census marked `none` or already consistent, and therefore not changed:** EXT-CLAUDECO-025's operational recommendation (pin the TTL and the CLI) survives and the design's scope statement already matched the docs (row 8). No grounding row for this file was skipped.

**Stale-string note for the validator.** The 2026-09-11 fact correction that the fixed `score-arm-d-panel.py` scores all **140** truth cells (the 2026-09-09 run scored 136) means the strings "140 truth cells" and "140 cells" are now correct where they appear with "all scored"; they are not the pre-correction restatements the brief's stale-string list was written against.
